# RawPage — Architecture & File Map

## Context

This document says **what every file does and what it interacts with**. It makes the layered
architecture from [`Tech-Stack-Plan.md`](./Tech-Stack-Plan.md) concrete at the file level, and it
traces the **primary call flows** as ASCII chains so a new contributor can follow a real event from
a keystroke down to a byte on disk.

It is the *spatial* view of the codebase. It deliberately does **not** say *when* each file is built
— that is [`Implementation-Plan.md`](./Implementation-Plan.md), whose per-stage **Components** lists
name exactly the rows below. It does not say *what features exist* — that is
[`Features.md`](./Features.md), whose capabilities the flows in §5 exercise.

This document **rots faster than any other in the set**. A file's row here is part of that file's
definition of done: when a file lands, changes responsibility, splits, or is folded into another,
its row is updated in the same change.

---

## 1. Dependency rule — read this first

Dependencies point **inward only**. Nothing in an inner layer may name anything in an outer one.

```
                          app/
             (composition root — the ONLY layer that names a concrete)
                            │
                            │ constructs every concrete, injects interfaces
              ┌─────────────┴─────────────────┐
              ▼                               ▼
            ui/ ─────────uses────────▶      core/
        (Qt Widgets only)                (domain: no Qt Widgets,
                                          no PDFium, no Win32)
                                              │
                                     uses (interfaces only)
                            ┌─────────────────┴─────────────────┐
                            ▼                                   ▼
                    core/backend.h                    platform/platform.h
              IDocumentBackend, IPageRasterizer,     IShellIntegration, ICryptoProvider,
              ITextExtractor, ISigner, IOcrEngine,   IScanner, IFontResolver,
              IIndexer, MutationSink  (7 total)     IAcceleratedSurface, … (17 total)
                            ▲                                   ▲
                 implements │                        implements │
                            │                                   │
                          pdf/                            platform/win/
              (the ONLY place FPDF_* or a PDF               (the ONLY OS-specific code)
               byte write may appear)
```

### The layer contract

| Layer | May include | May **never** include | Extra rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| `app/` | everything | — | The only translation units that name `pdf::createBackend()` or `platform::createPlatform()`. Contains no domain logic. |
| `ui/` | `core/`, `core/backend.h`, `platform/platform.h`, Qt | `pdf/`, `platform/win/` | May **never** mutate a `Document`. It constructs a `Command` and hands it to `CommandJournal::run()`. That is its only mutation vocabulary. |
| `core/` | std, `QtCore` (documented exception for `QString`/containers/signals), the portable pure-computation allowlist (**ICU, HarfBuzz, Hunspell**), `core/backend.h`, `platform/platform.h` | `ui/`, `pdf/`, `platform/win/`, `QtWidgets`, `QtGui`, PDFium, any OS header | Builds and passes its whole test suite with **no display and no PDF engine linked**. If it can't, the boundary has leaked. |
| `pdf/` | std, `QtCore`, `core/backend.h`, `core/doc/` value types, PDFium, codec libs | `ui/`, `platform/win/`, `core/` behaviour classes (`CommandJournal`, `Settings`) | The only layer permitted to emit PDF bytes or call `FPDF_*`. |
| `platform/win/` | `platform/platform.h`, Win32/COM | `ui/`, `core/`, `pdf/` | Implements interfaces and nothing else. No policy decisions. |

**`i18n/`, `res/`, `corpus/`, `golden/` are data, not code.** Nothing links them; they are loaded at
runtime or read by tests.

**Reading the "Interacts with" column.** A cell names collaborators in **both** directions, and the
wording says which: *"used by / read by / consumed by / driven by / populated by"* marks an **inbound**
collaborator in an outer layer (legitimate — `core/Settings` is read by everything), while a bare name
or *"calls"* marks an **outbound** dependency, which must always point inward. A `core/` row may
therefore name a `pdf/` or `ui/` file only as an inbound consumer, never as something it calls.

**Enforcement is mechanical, not cultural.** `tools/layer-lint` fails CI on any include crossing a
boundary outward; on a PDFium call **or handle** named outside **`pdf/render/`** — narrowed from
`pdf/` at Stage 2 T2, because `pdf/cos/` is first-party by rule and is half of what the portable
presets build on Linux; on a filesystem write outside `pdf/`, `core/settings/`, `tools/` and
`tests/`; on a path read out of `argv` outside `app/main.cpp`; and on a `QAction` constructed outside
`ui/shell/ActionFactory.cpp`. Two single-file layers have deny lists of their own, because several
layers include them: `platform/platform.h` may name no OS header, and `core/backend.h` may name
neither an engine type nor a QtGui one.
See [`Tech-Stack-Plan.md` §5.6](./Tech-Stack-Plan.md#56-how-it-stays-enforced) and
[`tools/layer-lint/rules.json`](../tools/layer-lint/rules.json), which is the executable form and the
one that cannot go stale.

**One row of the table above has a narrower truth than it can express**, and it is written here
rather than in the cell: **`pdf/render/` may include `platform/platform.h` and the rest of `pdf/`
may not** (Stage 2 T16). PDFium's font mapper is a process-global hook, and `pdf/render/
FontResolverBridge` is where the engine boundary and the OS boundary physically meet. The permission
is a `pdf-render` entry in `rules.json` rather than a widening of `pdf`, so `pdf/cos/` does not
inherit it — with a gate fixture in each direction.

- **○ (97)** — planned, each with the stage tag that builds it. **None is still ○ (S2) … ○ (S5)** — the one ○ row whose tag
  mentions S2 is `ui/dialogs/*`, a FAMILY row spanning S2–S16 whose own Stage 2 to 6 members so far are built or folded; **two are
  ○ (S6)**, `core/doc/Attachments` and `ui/panes/AttachmentsPane`, Stage 6 T11's.
  `platform/win/WinAcceleratedSurface` is the one row whose ○ is an ANSWER rather than a schedule:
  **Stage 4 T2 measured it on 2026-09-19 and it is not built**, because the cost is PDFium rasterizing
  behind the engine lock and a surface presents pixels rather than making them.
- **⤵ (1)** — `tools/spike-pdfium/`, deleted at Stage 2 T2 exactly as its row said it would be. A
  folded row stays as a breadcrumb; rows are never deleted.

The stage tags are the contract between this map and the roadmap.

~~Stage 0 also adds files this map does not yet describe — the build definition, the CMake modules, the
gate fixtures, the CI workflow, and `scripts/`.~~ ✅ **Paid.** §3.18 was added by Stage 0's own T15,
and the last three rows — `scripts/symbolize.ps1` (owed since Stage 1 T1), `tests/gates/fixtures/`,
and `tests/bench/` — were added by **Stage 1 T15**. Every file in the tree now has a row.

A row whose "File" cell names a folder with a `/*` suffix covers a **tight family** of same-shaped
files (one per tool, one per filter) that share a base class and a single responsibility; the Role
cell names the members. Everything else is one row per file, with `{.h,.cpp}` counting as one file.

## 3. File map by layer

### 3.1 `app/` — composition root

| File | Status | Role | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|
| `app/main.cpp` | ✅ (S1 T1, T8) | Entry point. Sets DPI awareness before any window exists, installs the crash handler, parses the command line, hands off to `CompositionRoot`, runs the Qt event loop. **Real as of T1:** per-monitor-v2 awareness and `CrashHandler::install()` both before the first Qt object, the `app/` log sink, and the four diagnostic switches the testing guide measures the entry point with (`--version`, `--print-args`, `--print-dpi-awareness`, `--crash-test`). **Real as of T8:** flow A end to end — parse → compose → claim → translators → window → `exec()`. A secondary instance forwards and exits 0 without ever creating a window; `--dump-ui-strings` composes every service, loads the catalogs and exits 0 without one either, which is what keeps a headless smoke test meaningful now that an ordinary launch runs an event loop. **From 2026-09-13 the claim is SKIPPED for a switch that composes and exits** — `Intent::composesAndExits()`, Stage 2 guide issue **I-27**: claiming it first made `--dump-ui-strings`, `--print-screen-metrics` and `--list-actions` secondaries whenever a window was open, so each forwarded, returned 0 and printed nothing, which is indistinguishable from success. **From 2026-09-14 `--list-actions` prints each action's effective chords** — one trailing `Context:Chord` field per binding from `KeyBindingStore::bindingsFor`, a user's override marked `[user]` — for Stage 2 guide case A-72. It is also the **only** translation unit permitted to touch `argv`, enforced by `layer-lint`'s non-escapable `argv-path-read` rule. **S3 · T11:** after `window.show()` and BEFORE the command line's files are opened, `RecoveryPrompt::checkAndOffer(window, services)` — so a file that is both passed and recoverable is recovered first and then focused rather than opened clean and refused as already open; `CrashHandler::install()` is called with no last rites, by D3-11. **S4 · T13:** the third and last step of the launch order (D4-9) — `app::SessionShell` is installed on the application and then restores, AFTER the recovery prompt and after the command line's files, so the restore can skip what they opened. Installed before it restores, so a launch whose restore brought nothing back still captures on the way out. **Still owed:** Stage 14's batch verbs. | `app/CrashHandler`, `app/CommandLine`, `app/CompositionRoot`, `app/Application`, `app/RecoveryPrompt` |
| `app/rawpage.manifest`, `app/rawpage.rc` | ✅ (S2 · I-35, 2026-09-14) | **The executable's application manifest, and ONE setting in it: the Windows 10 segment heap.** Without it, everything allocated through msvcrt -- our code, Qt, every tile's pixel copy -- came from the classic NT heap, which fragmented on the tile workload: 60 → 167 MB of free-but-committed memory in 11,500 gaps over 30 rounds of ten documents while live memory was flat. With it, against a control build, private bytes held at 72.5 → 80.1 MB over 40 rounds where the classic heap reached 339.6, and A2's product figure got faster (`Tech-Stack-Plan.md` §3.3). The `.rc` embeds it as RT_MANIFEST 1 and names it from the source root. **Two traps, each met:** a double hyphen inside the XML comment makes Windows reject the manifest and the executable fail to start; and windres does not declare the manifest as a dependency, so `OBJECT_DEPENDS` is set in `app/` and again in `tests/bench/` (a source-file property is directory-scoped). Embedded also in the benches that stand in for the product. Honoured from Windows 10 2004 (build 19041); DPI awareness stays an API call in `app/main.cpp`, not a manifest entry. Guide case A-93. | `app/CMakeLists.txt`, `tests/bench/CMakeLists.txt`, `tests/platform/tst_manifest.cpp` |
| `app/Application.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S1 T8; S4 · T13) | `QApplication` subclass: application lifetime, translator loading, last-window-closed policy. **Real as of T8:** the translator stack, resolving `--lang` → `language/uiLocale` → the system locale and loading `<application dir>/i18n/rawpage_<locale>.qm`. **TWO translators are installed, `en` first and the active locale on top of it** — because the catalog key IS the source string here (T6), an untranslated string falls back to its own key rather than to English, so the source catalog goes underneath as the fallback layer. **Real as of S4 · T13 (D4-9): `app::SessionShell`, the session half of this file** — the capture on quit and the restore on launch the row above had owed since Stage 1. `core/settings/SessionState` owns the format and the arithmetic and knows nothing about a window; this class reads a `ui::MainWindow` into that model and builds one back out of it. **It stashes a window's state on `QEvent::Close` rather than reading everything at `aboutToQuit`**, because the close protocol asks about each dirty document and REMOVES the tab (D3-14) — a session read only at quit would forget a document the person had open and merely declined to save. At quit each live window is taken fresh if VISIBLE (its close was refused, so the stash is stale) and from the stash if HIDDEN (it closed); a window closed an hour ago is `WA_DeleteOnClose` and is no longer in `MainWindow::shellWindows()` to ask. **Restore runs third**, after `RecoveryPrompt` and the command line, and skips any path they already opened — two tabs over one file would be two journals over one file. `general/restoreSession` off writes nothing and restores nothing; `general/rememberWindowLayout` and `general/rememberPaneLayout` subtract the geometry and the panes from what comes back. **I-01 is settled here**: the identity a guide is keyed by is read at CAPTURE from `IDocumentBackend::documentId()`, which `Document::refreshFileState` re-reads after every save, so a save cannot orphan a guide; a file changed by somebody else between the quit and the launch loses its guides and keeps its document. Theme application is delegated to `ui/shell/ThemeManager`, which owns it. → `tst_restore` (A-75 … A-79). | `core/settings/Settings`, `ui/shell/ThemeManager`, `core/settings/SessionState` |
| `app/CompositionRoot.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S1 T8) | **The wiring hub.** Constructs `platform::createPlatform()` and `pdf::createBackend()`, the `Settings` store, the `ActionRegistry`, the `RenderCache` and the `RenderScheduler` (S2 · T13), then builds `MainWindow` injecting only interfaces. **It owns the scheduler's ONE wake handler** and posts `deliverReady()` onto the UI thread — `setWakeHandler` replaces rather than chains, so a `PageView` installing its own would silence every other view the moment a second tab opened (convention 18, `core/Log.h`'s single sink in a second disguise). Teardown drops the wake, expires its life flag so a post already in Qt's queue is inert, and destroys the scheduler BEFORE the backend, because joining the worker is what guarantees nothing is inside the engine when it goes. The only file that knows a concrete backend exists. **Real as of T8:** `Settings`, `RecentFiles`, `ActionRegistry`, `KeyBindingStore` (seeded from the defaults table, then the per-user overrides out of `settings.ini`, reporting how many were accepted and how many named an action that no longer exists), and `platform::createPlatform()`. It also owns the **logging settings**: `logging/level` sets the threshold and `logging/subsystems` becomes a filter wrapped around the sink `main.cpp` supplied, re-applied live on a settings change. Its destructor calls `Settings::sync()` — the write nothing had called before T8 (issue I-14). **Real as of T14:** that sink is now a **fan-out** rather than a wrapper — console, `app/LogFile` and `core/LogBuffer`, all from one installed sink, because `setLogSink` replaces and a chained sink would be dropped on the next `logging/` change. It owns the buffer (built first, so the settings-version note lands in it) and the rolling file (built after `Settings`, because the retention count is a setting), and it writes a **session banner** naming the build id, since everything logged before composition — `CrashHandler::install()`'s line included — reached the console and could not reach a file that did not exist yet. `RenderScheduler` and `createBackend()` arrive at Stage 2. | `pdf::createBackend()`, `platform::createPlatform()`, `core/settings/Settings`, `core/cmd/ActionRegistry`, `core/render/RenderScheduler`, `ui/shell/MainWindow`, `app/Services` **T15 makes it read `performance/` and drive the battery throttle.** The cache caps and the worker count come from the table rather than from the structs' own initializers, which is what makes `SettingsKeys.def` rule 2 true rather than aspirational; the caps are re-applied live and the worker count is not, because a pool's threads are made once. The throttle is the wire T12 built the interface for and deliberately did not connect — it needed a configuration in which the limit could differ, and `throttleOnBattery` plus `renderWorkers` is that configuration. **It subscribes to `IPowerSession` without unsubscribing, which is safe HERE and only here**: the subscriber and the subscribed-to have exactly the same lifetime, and a widget doing the same thing would be convention 10's bug. **S3 · T11: `Options::recoveryRoot`**, empty meaning `RecoveryJournal::defaultRoot()`, carried into `Services::recoveryRoot` — so a case composing the product (`tests/composition/`) neither writes into nor is offered the recoveries of whoever runs it; `documents/recoveryDir` (T12) is the user's way to set it. **S5 · T6: the one `core/text/DocumentIndex`** (D5-4), built after the scheduler whose text jobs feed it and destroyed BEFORE it -- the index holds the scheduler's text subscription and tickets -- with its budget from `search/indexCacheMB`, re-applied live on a change to the Search group, as the tile cache's is. **S5 · T7: the one `core/text/SearchEngine`**, built after the index and destroyed before it, with a wake handler of its own that only posts `deliverReady()` onto the UI thread under the same life flag as the scheduler's. |
| `app/BackendRasterizer.h` | ✅ (S2 · T13) | **A row this stage's file manifest did not list**, added with the file. The `IPageRasterizer` a render worker holds, and the only thing in the program that turns a `RenderRequest`'s `DocumentHandle` back into an engine — `RenderScheduler` builds one rasterizer per WORKER (D2-9) while `IDocumentBackend::rasterizer()` is per DOCUMENT, and something has to bridge that. It is a header in `app/` rather than a class inside `CompositionRoot.cpp` for one reason: as a private class, the one arrangement the shipped product actually runs was the one arrangement nothing tested — `tests/ui` and `tests/unit` fake the rasterizer because neither may link an engine. `tests/render/tst_renderpipeline.cpp` now drives this class rather than a copy of it. | `core/backend.h`, `core/render/RenderScheduler`, `app/CompositionRoot` |
| `app/BackendTextExtractor.h` | ✅ **(S5 · T2)** | **A row the plan's manifest did not list**, added with the file. `BackendRasterizer`'s argument for text: the `ITextExtractor` a render worker holds, turning a `TextRequest`'s handle back into that document's extractor on each call. A header naming only `core/` types, so `tests/render/tst_extract.cpp` drives this class through the real scheduler rather than a copy of it. | `core/backend.h`, `core/render/RenderScheduler`, `app/CompositionRoot` |
| `app/Services.h` | ✅ (S1 T8) | The aggregate of injected interfaces passed by reference down the UI tree. Keeps constructors from growing twelve parameters without becoming a service locator. Stage 1 carries `Settings&`, `RecentFiles&`, `ActionRegistry&`, `KeyBindingStore&`, `LogBuffer&` (T14) and `Platform&`; **Stage 2 added `IDocumentBackend&` at T9 and `RenderScheduler&` PLUS `RenderCache&` at T13** — all `core/` types, so the header stays inside what `ui/` may name. The cache is the one this file did not predict, and it is here because closing a document has to drop its tiles and nothing else can: `RenderScheduler` holds the cache privately and has no "forget this document" of its own. **REFERENCES, not pointers** — every service here is required for the window to exist at all, which is the opposite of `platform::Platform`, whose null members are the honest state of an unimplemented interface. **It is one of exactly two files `ui/` may look outward to**, and it has its own `layer-lint` layer entry constraining it to `core/` plus `platform/platform.h` — the same treatment `platform/platform.h` gets, for the same reason: without it, a `pdf/` include added here at Stage 2 would hand every widget a dependency on the engine through the one door that is open. **S3 · T10 adds one VALUE, `QString recoveryRoot`**, last and defaulted so every aggregate initializer still compiles: empty means `core::RecoveryJournal::defaultRoot()`, and the widget suite points it into its own temporary directory so no test writes into the user's recovery folder. It is not a service, and T12's `documents/recoveryDir` setting is what the product reads instead. **S5 · T6 adds `core::text::DocumentIndex&`**, with its consumers: every `DocumentWindow` attaches its handle and hands it a mutation's `Scope`, and the window's `SelectionOverlay` reads its pages from it. **S5 · T7 adds `core::text::SearchEngine&`**, with its first widget -- each document window's find bar -- as this file's rule asks. | Consumed by `ui/shell/MainWindow`, `ui/shell/DocumentWindow`, `ui/write/FindReplaceBar` |
| `app/CommandLine.{h,cpp}` | 🟡 (S1 T8 basic, S14 full) | Parses every CLI verb from [`Features.md` §9.6](./Features.md#96-command-line) into an intent object; runs headless verbs without creating a window; documents exit codes. **Real as of T8:** open-with-file, `--lang`, `--dump-ui-strings`, `--help`, `--`, and the diagnostic vocabulary, with exit code 2 for a usage error. `Intent::composesAndExits()` (2026-09-13) answers *does this switch build the product and exit without becoming a session* in one place, so the rule that such a switch never claims the single-instance bus is inherited by the next one rather than re-derived (**I-27**). A **pure** function from a `QStringList` to an intent — no filesystem, no Qt GUI type, no side effect — which is why `tst_commandline` lives in the headless suite: `tests/unit` compiles this `.cpp` in rather than linking the executable, and `rawpage_assert_headless()` still resolves to `core/` + `pdf/`. **THERE IS NO argc/argv OVERLOAD AND NEVER WILL BE** — issue I-04 — and `tests/unit/compile/argv-overload.cpp` proves a caller who tries does not compile. **Still owed:** every verb beyond these, and the headless batch path, at Stage 14. | `core/batch/BatchRunner`, `core/cmd/ActionRegistry`, `app/CompositionRoot` |
| `app/CrashHandler.{h,cpp}` | 🟡 (S1 T1) | Unhandled-exception and `SIGABRT` capture, minidump write, an I/O-free last-rites hook, opt-in report queue. The GCC toolchain emits **DWARF, not PDB**, so the dump is paired with a build ID and symbolized against the archived unstripped binary rather than by a symbol server — decide and build that path here, not at Stage 15. **Real as of T1:** `SetUnhandledExceptionFilter`, a lazily-loaded `dbghelp!MiniDumpWriteDump` writing to `%LOCALAPPDATA%\RawPage\crashes\`, a plain-text sidecar carrying the build id and the last-rites outcome, and the whole symbolization path end to end (`scripts/symbolize.ps1`, guide case A-65). **Still owed:** `SIGABRT` capture and the opt-in report queue (S15). **Corrected at S3 · T11 — no last-rites sink will be wired, by decision D3-11** (`Stage3-Mutation-Plan.md` §3): this row said the hook waited for `RecoveryJournal` to flush it, and the journal that shipped at S3 · T9 has no buffer — every operation is on disk before the edit returns, which the kill cases prove with `TerminateProcess`, a kill that runs no handler at all — and file I/O inside a broken process is a hazard on the one path that must not fail. The hook stays installed and invoked with nothing in it, and the log line and the sidecar say `none (D3-11: …)` rather than *"arrives at Stage 3"*. | `core/Log`, `platform::IShellIntegration` (dump path) |
| `app/LogFile.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S1 T14) | The **rolling log file** of [`Features.md` §9.5](./Features.md#95-logging-and-diagnostics), under `%LOCALAPPDATA%\RawPage\logs\`, and the reader `logging/retainFiles` had been waiting for since T5 declared it. One file per process plus a size cap, retention by **file count** because that is what the setting says and what a user can reason about, and pruning at open rather than on a timer. It is in `app/` because `core/` may not write to disk — `core/Log.h` says so in its own header — and it carries two of the six live `layer-lint` filesystem-write waivers, both named and both in `--explain`. Line shape is deliberately identical to `main.cpp`'s console sink and to `ui/panes/LogPane`'s, so one record reads the same in a terminal, on screen and in a support bundle. Compiled into `tests/unit` the way `app/CommandLine.cpp` is, because it names QtCore and nothing else. | `core/Log.h`; fanned out by `app/CompositionRoot`; opened by `log.openFolder` (Stage 14) |
| `app/RecoveryPrompt.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S3 · T11) | On launch, discovers recovery journals whose source still matches, presents them, and drives replay; reports the command that failed if replay stops early. **As built (D3-11):** `checkAndOffer(MainWindow&, const Services&)`, called by `app/main.cpp` after the window is shown and before the command line's files open. It discovers under `Services::recoveryRoot` (a live owner's directory is never listed), **removes a journal with no operations without asking**, describes the rest to `RecoveryDialog` — into the file, into a new file (source changed, or `meta.json` says untitled), source missing, or damaged — and acts on one disposition each: Recover is `MainWindow::recoverDocument`, Discard is `RecoveryJournal::discardDirectory`, Decide later keeps the directory. **Nobody to ask** (convention 16): nothing recovered and nothing discarded, and the log counts what was kept. A Recover answered for a row the dialog offers no Recover on is refused. It names `core/` and `ui/` only, so `tests/ui` and `tests/composition` compile it in. **S3 · T12:** it looks under `RecoveryJournal::resolveRoot` of `documents/recoveryDir` and `Services::recoveryRoot`, where `DocumentWindow` writes — and only there. → `tst_recoverydialog`, `tst_recoveryproduct` (A-74). | `core/cmd/RecoveryJournal`, `ui/dialogs/RecoveryDialog`, `ui/shell/MainWindow`, `app/Services` |
| `app/SingleInstance.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S1 T8) | Decides first-instance vs. secondary-instance at startup; a secondary instance forwards its file list and exits; the primary raises itself and opens the file. Thin on purpose — the hard parts are `platform::ISingleInstanceBus` (D1-11) and what is left here is **policy**: whether `general/singleInstance` wants the behaviour at all, and what the scope is. **The scope is a hash of the executable's own directory**, so a debug build and a release build on one desktop are two applications rather than one confusing one. A bundle with no bus is `Disabled`, not an error: every launch being its own instance is a usable product; a refusal to start is not. | `platform::ISingleInstanceBus`, `ui/shell/MainWindow` |

### 3.2 `core/doc/` — the document model

| File | Status | Role | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|
| `core/doc/Document.{h,cpp}` | 🟡 (**S3 · T4** for the parts Stage 3 reads; later parts with their stages) | The open document's aggregate root: `PageTree`, `AnnotationStore`, `FormModel`, `Outline`, `LayerTree`, `TagTree`, `Attachments`, `Metadata`, `Permissions`, `RepairReport`, current `Revision`. Owns **no** file handle and knows nothing about PDF syntax. **At Stage 3 (D3-8) it holds the handle, `DocumentId`, `PageTree`, `RepairReport`, `Permissions` and `Revision` — and nothing without a Stage 3 caller.** `hydrate()` COPIES from the backend, so a model rotation leaves the engine's reading alone (D3-15); it reads everything before assigning anything, so an unknown handle changes nothing. Revisions start at `initial()` and only go up. **`CommandJournal` is the only holder of a mutable one** (T5, D3-10). `modelHash()` (T5) is a SHA-256 over every page's index, rotation, five boxes and label, which reversibility and the journal's partial-application check compare. → `tst_document`, `tst_backendedit`, `tst_reversibility`. | `core/doc/*`, mutated only by `core/cmd/Command` subclasses; hydrated by `IDocumentBackend` |
| `core/doc/DocumentId.h` | ✅ (S3 · T4) | Stable identity for a document across sessions (source path + size + mtime + content hash). Keys the recovery journal and the per-document view state. **Header-only.** The path is normalized BY HAND with Windows' rule (convention 11) — separators, `.`/`..` without climbing past a drive or UNC root, and case-folded — so `portable-asan` computes the same identity; links, junctions and short names are not resolved. The hash is SHA-256 of the bytes the backend opened, so a same-size, same-time edit is caught (D3-16); `key()` names a recovery directory. → `tst_document`. | `core/cmd/RecoveryJournal`, `core/doc/SaveCoordinator`, `pdf/DocumentBackendImpl` |
| `core/doc/Page.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S2 · T9) | One page: MediaBox/CropBox/TrimBox/BleedBox/ArtBox, rotation, label, resource summary, per-page annotation and field indices. Pure → `tst_pagetree`. **Brought forward to Stage 2** — `core/render/TileGrid` is its first consumer and needs page geometry before any mutation exists (Stage 2 D2-5, `Stage2-Rendering-Plan.md` §3). **The box a viewer DRAWS is the CropBox, not the MediaBox**: a print-ready file routinely has a MediaBox with bleed and crop marks around the actual page, so laying out MediaBoxes puts a grey margin round every page and reports the wrong size in Properties. The defaulting is a CHAIN and is resolved here rather than stored — CropBox from MediaBox, then Bleed/Trim/Art from CropBox — and **every box is intersected with the MediaBox**, which the specification requires and real files violate. Rotation is normalized from a possibly-negative multiple of 90 (`/Rotate -90` is legal and means 270), and `displaySize()` is the size AFTER rotation, because a caller that scaled `cropBox().size` itself gets every landscape page in a portrait document wrong. | Held by `core/doc/PageTree`; read by `core/render/TileGrid` |
| `core/doc/PageTree.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S2 · T9 read half; S3 · T4 rotation — `setRotation(index, degrees)`)**; ○ (**S7** insert, move, remove, duplicate — `Stage3-Mutation-Plan.md` D3-8) | Ordered page collection with insert/move/remove/duplicate as **model** operations (no I/O), plus page-label resolution and index↔label mapping. Pure → `tst_pagetree`. **Stage 2 builds the READ half** — construct, index, count, page size, rotation, label — because `core/render/PagePresentation` lays pages out before anything can move them; **Stage 3 adds rotation, and Stage 7 insert/move/remove/duplicate with the commands that call them** (Stage 2 D2-5, narrowed by `Stage3-Mutation-Plan.md` D3-8 on 2026-09-14 — this sentence gave all four to Stage 3 until then). Same split as `Outline` below, for the same reason. **A page label is not a page number**: ISO 32000-1 12.4.2 lets a document number its front matter i, ii and then restart at 1, so such a file has TWO pages that both call themselves "1" — `indexOfLabel()` and `at()` answer separately and neither guesses. The mutating half is **absent rather than present-and-refusing**: this class has no `Result`, so an unimplemented mutation would have nowhere to report to (convention 6). | Mutated by `core/cmd/commands/PageCommands`; read by `ui/panes/ThumbnailsPane` |
| `core/doc/Annotation.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S6 · T2) | One annotation, as the model holds it (D6-3, D6-4): subtype (numbered as PDFium's constants, mapped explicitly anyway), the rectangle **normalized**, the geometry of each kind **as written** (quadpoints keep their corner order, guide A-20), colour, opacity, border, the text fields, `PdfDate` (as written and as UTC, parsed in integer arithmetic with no time-zone database -- convention 24), the flags, `/IRT` and `/RT`, a state reply's model and state, and its identity -- **`AnnotationId`**: the object number when read, (page, position) for a direct dictionary, the `/NM` when RawPage creates it. **Not every key**: D6-5 has an edit start from the dictionary on disk. `toJson`/`fromJson` are an identity for every subtype (object numbers as 64-bit integers, never doubles). Pure → `tst_annotation`. **Since S6 · T12** (D6-39(c)) it holds what a measurement draws: a Line's `/LL`, `/LLE`, `/LLO`, `/Cap`, `/CP`, `/CO`, and `core::MeasureDictionary` -- `/Measure`'s subtype, `/R` and each chain of number formats' `/U`, `/C`, `/D`, `/RT`, `/RD`, a format asking for another display marked not plain -- in its JSON (a label's one-format object reads as a chain of one) and bounded by `AnnotationCommands`' `invalidShape` (`core::isBoundedMeasure`). | Held by `AnnotationStore`; filled by `pdf/ops/AnnotationReader`; **read by** `pdf/ops/AppearanceStreamBuilder` (T3) to generate `/AP` |
| `core/doc/AnnotationStore.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (**S6 · T2 the read side**; the commands that change it from T3; **S6 · T13 `importFdf` and `exportFdf`** -- the merge and the export, defined in `core/doc/Fdf.cpp` beside the model they speak) | Every annotation of an open document, as **two views of one reading**: each page's `/Annots` LISTING entry by entry (what D6-3 compares with the engine -- `listingFacts()`), and the annotations once per dictionary, one listed by several pages OWNED by the page its `/P` names. **Threads** per ISO 32000-1 §12.5.6.3 (D6-12), built iteratively: replies, `/RT /Group` members shown as one item, Review and Marked **histories** in UTC order, checkmarks per author; a missing parent, a cross-page reply and every member of an `/IRT` cycle are reported and shown top-level. **Queries**: filter by page, author, subtype, text, hidden; sort by page, date, author, subtype. A store that could not be read **says why** (`status()`, e.g. `error.edit.encrypted`). Pure → `tst_annotstore`. **Since S6 · T3 its change half**: `insert` and `remove` over placements, all or nothing, called by a command's `apply()`/`revert()` and nothing else -- an annotation listed twice is one annotation, one listed nowhere leaves -- and `placementsOf(id)`; threads and indexes rebuilt after each, the reading's notes kept. `core/doc/Document` holds a copy from hydration, and its `modelHash()` covers every listing entry and each annotation's whole JSON -- through `annotationDigest()`, cached per annotation because the journal hashes the model before every command (78 ms on `annots-900.pdf` uncached, 2 ms cached; S6 · T3). → `tst_annotationops`. **Since S6 · T5 `replace(changed)`**, an edit IN PLACE: by identity, all or nothing (no id twice, no change of identity, subtype or owning page), each replaced annotation's digest dropped and every listing entry naming it given its new fact. → `tst_annotedit`. **Since S6 · T9 the Comments pane's queries** (D6-12, D6-36): the filter widened to status (the latest Review state, `None` without one), the current identity's checkmark, a date range, and text in any reply under an item; `grouped()` by page, author, the HOST's calendar day (its UTC offset given), subtype, status or checkmark, each group in its key's order; `reviewStatus()`; `threadOf()`, what is deleted with a comment; `sequenceNumbers()` in reading order as each page is shown; and a created identity found by map, not by walking. → `tst_annotthreads`. | `core/doc/Annotation`, `core/doc/Permissions` (its fact type); filled by `pdf/ops/AnnotationReader`, held by `pdf/DocumentBackendImpl`; **read by** `ui/panes/CommentsPane`, `core/cmd/commands/AnnotationCommands` |
| `core/doc/AnnotationGeometry.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S6 · T5)** — **a row the file manifest did not name**, added with the file | **An annotation's geometry moved, resized and hit** (D6-27, D6-30). `mappedInto(a, rect)` carries every point -- quadpoints, vertices, the line, ink samples, the callout -- by the one mapping that takes the rectangle onto `rect`, `/RD` scaled with it; `translated` is a move by addition; `isMappedCopy(before, after)` is the question `pdf/ops/MutationSinkImpl` asks before redrawing an appearance (ISO 32000-1 §12.5.5 maps a carried appearance into its new rectangle). **`hitsAnnotation(a, point, tolerance)`: hit where it is DRAWN** -- a rectangle's or an oval's outline (its interior only when filled), a line, a polyline, a polygon or ink by its stroke at its border width, a line's endings as the wedge or disk they are drawn in, a cloud over the band `/RD` gives its bumps, text markup over its quadrilaterals (the words it marks), a box kind over its box; `Hidden`, `NoView` and a `Popup` never. The tolerance is the caller's, in page units -- the overlay converts DEVICE pixels, so a hit is as easy at every zoom. Pure → `tst_annotedit`; against the rendered INK, `tests/composition/tst_annotink` (A-41). **Since S6 · T12**: a Line is hit where `core::dimensionGeometry` draws it -- its line moved by `/LL`, its leaders, its caption's box, its endings at the drawn ends -- and a measured polyline or polygon over its reading too; `mappedInto` scales the leaders and the caption's offset by the mean of the mapping, and `isMappedCopy` compares them. | `core/doc/Annotation`; **read by** `core/cmd/commands/AnnotationCommands`, `pdf/ops/MutationSinkImpl`, `ui/viewer/AnnotationOverlay` |
| `core/doc/TypedText.{h,cpp}`, `CjkCoverage.inc` | ✅ **(S6 · T7)** — **a row the file manifest did not name**, added with the file | **Typed text as an appearance will draw it** (D6-7, D6-34), shared by the editor, the overlay and the builder so the three cannot disagree: the five faces (`TypedFont` -- Helvetica in WinAnsi, the four predefined CJK collections through their UCS-2 CMaps, none embedded); `winAnsiCode`, Helvetica's AFM advances (`helveticaWidth`), and each CJK collection's coverage from the GENERATED `CjkCoverage.inc`; `firstRefusedCharacter` and `describeCharacter` -- what no face can show, refused by name; which collection draws an ideograph (kana Japanese, Hangul Korean, else the Han preference: `/DA`'s CJK face, else the host's languages through `hanPreference`); `layoutTypedText` -- lines broken after a word and between CJK characters, a soft break keeping its space, n lines n x 1.2 ems; `DefaultAppearance` read and written; the free-text frame (`freeTextFrame`, turned by `/Rotate`), `freeTextBoxAt`, `calloutLeader` and `fittedFreeText` (a box keeps its width, a typewriter fits, a callout keeps its target, `/RD` worked out). Pure. → `tst_typedtext` (unit), `tests/render/tst_typedtext` (the coverage and the advances held to the ENGINE), `tst_annotfreetext`. | `pdf/ops/AppearanceStreamBuilder`, `ui/viewer/AnnotationOverlay`, `ui/viewer/AnnotationTextEditor`, `tools/cjk-coverage/` |
| `core/doc/Stamps.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S6 · T10)** — **a row the file manifest did not name**, added with the file | **What a stamp IS before anything draws it** (D6-16, D6-37): its KIND, told apart by the dictionary -- a standard `/Name` from ISO 32000-1 Table 181, one of RawPage's five dynamic `/Name`s, an image stamp (`/IT /StampImage`), or FOREIGN, which is never redrawn; the WORDS a built-in stamp draws, its name's own split at its capitals (no catalog English in `core/`), and its colour; the Helvetica-Bold advances its words are set on; its NATURAL size -- the box its appearance is drawn in, scaled onto `/Rect`, so a resize is not a redraw -- and its TURN, any whole degree, about its centre at its scale (`rotatedStamp`, `stampFittedTo`); the lines a dynamic stamp bakes (`dynamicStampLines`, the host's UTC offset, numerals only); and an image stamp's pixels (`StampImage`, declared in `core/doc/Annotation.h`): a JPEG's header read so its bytes pass through, pixels held zlib-compressed for `/FlateDecode` with their alpha, bounded, and keyed by SHA-256 so a save writes each once. Pure, QtCore. → `tests/unit/tst_stamps`. | `core/doc/Annotation`, `core/doc/TypedText`, `pdf/ops/AppearanceStreamBuilder`, `ui/viewer/tools/StampTool` |
| `core/doc/Redaction.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S6 · T14)** — **a row the file manifest did not name**, added with the file | **A redaction MARK as a tool makes one, and a sound comment** (D6-25, D6-41(a)-(c), (h)): `newTextRedaction` over a selection's quadrilaterals (its rectangle `markupRect`'s) and `newAreaRedaction` over a rectangle, each with its `RedactionStyle` -- outline (`/C`, red), fill (`/IC`, black), opacity, overlay text, its size and its repeat -- and a `/DA` whose colour is READABLE on the fill (`redactionTextColour`: white over a fill darker than mid-grey, black otherwise; `withRedactionFill` keeps it so when the fill changes). `newSoundComment`: the `Speaker` icon 20 points square, its top-left corner where the page was clicked. A mark removes nothing; applying is Stage 10's. Pure. → `tests/unit/tst_redaction`, `tst_sounddata`. |
| `core/doc/DrawnShapes.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S6 · T8)** — **a row the file manifest did not name**, added with the file | **The geometry of what the drawing tools make and the builder draws** (D6-15, D6-35), computed once so the appearance, the hit test and the rectangle a shape is written with agree: ISO 32000-1 Table 176's ten LINE ENDINGS (`lineEnding`, a head `max(9, 4.5 w)` long, barbs 30 degrees off the shaft, the reversed arrows sweeping forward); a CLOUD's bumps (`cloudPath`, `cloudReach`: each edge cut into spans of `5 + 5 I` points, each a half circle bulging away from the polygon's inside whichever way it is written); an INK stroke's curve (`inkCurve`, centripetal Catmull-Rom through every sample, each end repeating its sample) and its deduplication (`dedupedSamples`); the rectangle each shape needs (`fittedShape`: a line's ends and heads, a polygon's corners and a cloud's bumps, the curve's control points, grown by half the stroke and a point); `Shift`'s constraints (`constrainedTo45`, `constrainedSquare`); and THE ERASER (`erasedStrokes`: the samples within its reach removed, a stroke split where it was covered or crossed, a one-sample fragment dropped). Pure. → `tests/unit/tst_drawnshapes` (A-59, A-60's geometry). | **Since S6 · T12**: `fittedShape` holds a Line's leaders, caption and endings at the DRAWN line, and a measured polyline's or polygon's reading (`core/Measure`).
| `core/doc/FormField.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S9) | One field: type, name, value, default, options, flags, format/validate/calculate specs, appearance, tab index. Pure → `tst_formfield`. | Held by `FormModel` |
| `core/doc/FormModel.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S9) | Field hierarchy, name resolution, calculation dependency graph and order, validation dispatch, tab-order computation, and the import/export data mapping. Pure → `tst_formmodel`. | `core/doc/FormField`, `ui/panes/FieldsPane`, `ui/viewer/FormOverlay` |
| `core/doc/Outline.{h,cpp}` | 🟡 **(S4 · T4 read ✅, S7 edit)** — **moved from S3 read on 2026-09-14** (`Stage3-Mutation-Plan.md` D3-8: its first reader is Stage 4's `BookmarksPane`) | The bookmark tree: nodes with title, style, destination-or-action, and children; promote/demote/reorder as model ops; generators from headings, font size, or a text pattern. Pure → `tst_outline`. | `core/cmd/commands/BookmarkCommands`, `ui/panes/BookmarksPane` |

The document's own presentation instructions, read: per-page `/Trans` and `/Dur`, and the catalog's `/PageMode`. **`TransitionStyle` is FOUR values because `Features.md` §1.2 promises four**, while ISO 32000-1 12.4.4 defines twelve — a style this build cannot draw reads as `None` and **the file's own word is kept beside it**, so a page we cannot animate stays distinguishable from one that asked for a cut; approximating `/Split` with a wipe would present an animation the author did not ask for. **Every field is optional because absence is not a default**: a page with no `/Trans` must be distinguishable from one that says `/S /R`, or the per-page override over `fullScreen/transition` is unimplementable — which is the control `corpus/ordinary/transitions.pdf`'s fourth page exists to be. → `tst_docmodels`. | `pdf/ops/TransitionReader`, `ui/viewer/FullScreenController`, `core/settings/SettingsSchema` |
| `pdf/ops/TransitionReader.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S4 · T10)** | ⚠️ **A row the file manifest did not name.** **THE FIRST READ IN THIS PROJECT THAT PDFIUM CANNOT DO, and the one deviation from D4-3 this stage makes.** D4-3 settled that the outline, the destinations and the Info dictionary are read THROUGH PDFium; its C API exposes page transitions **nowhere at all** — there is no `FPDFPage_GetTransition`, and `FPDFPage_HasTransparency` is a different subject wearing a similar name. So the rule is *read through PDFium* with the remainder *unless PDFium cannot*, and this is where that is reached. **It costs no second parse**: `DocumentBackendImpl::open` already runs `MutationSinkImpl::open` on every document, editable or not, to check our page walk against the engine's, and this reads four more keys out of dictionaries already fetched. In `pdf/ops/` rather than `pdf/cos/` because knowing that `/Trans` means a page transition is document semantics — `PageOps`'s line, not the object model's. **Tolerant throughout**: a `/Trans` that is not a dictionary, a `/S` that is not a name, a `/Dur` that is negative or absurd — each yields *no transition on that page* rather than an error, because refusing the document would turn a cosmetic defect into a file that will not open. → `tst_docmodels`. | `pdf/cos/XrefTable`, `pdf/ops/PageOps`, `core/doc/PageTransitions` |
