Pure; the backend supplies both readings | The decoded permission set and the **single** `may(Operation)` predicate every command asks before it is allowed to run. Pure → `tst_permissions`. | `core/cmd/CommandJournal` (pre-flight), `core/security/EncryptionPolicy` |
| `core/doc/SaveCoordinator.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S3 · T8) | **A row the file map did not have**, added by `Stage3-Mutation-Plan.md` D3-4. Decides whether, where and in what order a document is saved, and writes no byte itself. `save(journal, path)`: no `IDurableFiles` → refused; a clean journal → refused; a repaired document (`requiresSaveAs()`, D3-6) or a file that is no longer the one opened (path, size, time and **content hash**, D3-16) → **Save-As required**, by key; otherwise stray temporary files of dead writers are removed, the backend writes and re-reads `<dir>/.<name>.rawpage-<pid>.tmp`, `IDurableFiles` flushes and replaces, the backend adopts, and the journal takes the new identity and is marked saved. A failure before the replace leaves the target untouched, the temporary file gone and the journal dirty — and, when the FILE SYSTEM is what refused (`IoFailure`, `PermissionDenied`, `Unavailable`, `NotFound`), it is **Save-As required** carrying that refusal as its reason, since another path is a real answer to it (D3-16, guide I-15); a `Corrupted` re-read is not, and stays an error. A failure after the replace keeps the journal dirty so the next Save asks for Save-As. `saveAs(target)` skips the dirty, repaired and identity checks — except onto the document's own file. → `tst_savecoordinator`, `tst_roundtrip`, `tst_saveengine`, `bench_save`. | `core/cmd/CommandJournal`, `IDocumentBackend`, `platform::IDurableFiles`, `ui/shell/DocumentWindow` (T10), `core/batch/BatchRunner` |
| `core/doc/RepairReport.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S2 · T8) | What the parser had to reconstruct on open, per object, with severity — surfaced in the message bar and forcing first-save-as. **It is not a boolean, and that is the whole design**: *"reconstructed 3 objects of 4,000"* and *"reconstructed the entire xref"* are different risks (Stage 2 plan §5.4), so it carries per-object entries plus both halves of the ratio, and records a from-scratch rebuild as an explicit flag rather than inferring it — a two-object file rebuilt from nothing and a two-object file with two bad offsets both have a ratio of 1.0. **`requiresSaveAs()` is deliberately NOT `!isEmpty()`**: an `Info` finding such as a `/Length` recovered exactly is the commonest thing in any real PDF, and forcing the prompt on it would train users to ignore it. **The one-line summary is a CATALOG KEY plus arguments, never a sentence** — `core/` holds no user-facing English and has no `tr()`, and an English summary here is the single change that makes the product unshippable in `zh-TW` while looking reasonable in review. Guide case **A-22**. | **Populated by** `pdf/cos/XrefRepair`; **read by** `ui/shell/MessageBar`, `ui/dialogs/RepairReportDialog` |
| `core/doc/Revision.h` | ✅ (S2 · T8) | Monotonic document version. Render caches, panes, and the dirty flag all key off it, so nothing needs deep comparison to know it is stale. **Brought forward to Stage 2**: `RenderRequest` carries it, so a stale tile cannot be shown once Stage 3’s mutations land (Stage 2 D2-5, D2-12). **A type rather than a `std::uint64_t`, with no implicit conversion from one** — page indices, object numbers and tile indices are all integers here and a transposed argument between two of them compiles, and D2-12 makes the `Revision` inside a `RenderRequest` mandatory rather than defaulted, which only a type can enforce. A default-constructed one is deliberately **invalid**, so a `RenderRequest` somebody forgot to fill in cannot look like a legitimate cache key. `next()` only goes up: **undo produces a NEW revision rather than returning to the previous one**, because a cache keyed on a revision that came back would serve tiles rendered before the redo. Header-only and `constexpr`, with a `std::hash` supplied beside `operator==` so the two cannot disagree — a hash that disagreed produces a cache that misses silently, whose only symptom is that scrolling is slow. | `core/cmd/CommandJournal`, `core/render/RenderCache` |
| `core/doc/Scope.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S2 · T8; **S6 · T4 the aspect and the rectangles**) | **Since S6 · T4 a page scope has an ASPECT** -- content or annotations (D6-10): an annotation scope drops its pages' tiles and keeps their text -- **and may carry RECTANGLES**, each changed annotation's padded `/Rect` (`annotationRegions`, `regionsOf`), so `core/render/` drops only the tiles one reaches (the named work of *A2 at Stage 6*, `Tech-Stack-Plan.md` §3.3); a merge that cannot keep them exactly drops them, so it only ever widens. What a mutation touched (page set, object set, "whole document"), so invalidation repaints one page instead of everything. **Brought forward to Stage 2** — `RenderCache::invalidate(Scope, Revision)` is a Stage 2 signature (Stage 2 D2-5). **The rule that makes it safe: widening is always allowed, narrowing never is.** Claiming more than was touched wastes a repaint; claiming less shows a stale tile, which is a correctness bug that gets diagnosed as a graphics-driver problem. So every operation rounds OUTWARDS — an `Objects` scope answers *yes* to `touchesPage()` for every page, because this type does not know which pages an object appears on (that is `PageTree`'s, and it arrives at T9), and merging pages with objects gives the whole document rather than a guessed union. The default is **`Nothing`, not `WholeDocument`**, so a scope somebody forgot to fill in surfaces as *the screen did not update* — visible, reproducible and fixed — rather than as a permanent full repaint, which is invisible and merely slow. An empty page set is `Nothing` rather than an empty `Pages`, which is how the forbidden narrowing would otherwise arrive through an ordinary filtered-list code path. | Emitted by `CommandJournal::mutated`, consumed by `ui/viewer/PageView` and every pane |

### 3.3 `core/cmd/` — the action layer and the mutation journal

| File | Status | Role | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|
| `core/cmd/Command.h`, `Command.cpp` | ✅ (S3 · T5; **the `.cpp` S6 · T3**) | The mutation contract: `typeId/name/scope/operation/apply/revert/serialize`. **The only vocabulary for changing a document.** `name()` is a **catalog key and arguments** (`CatalogText`), never a sentence (`Stage3-Mutation-Plan.md` D3-10(a)). `operation()` is the one method beyond the contract's five, added at T5 because `Permissions::may()` has to be asked WHICH edit is being attempted. **`permitted(document)`, S6 · T3, is that pre-flight** and `CommandJournal::run` asks it: its default -- the one function in `Command.cpp` -- is `may(operation())`, and an annotation command adds `mayAnnotate(page)` for every page it touches, so a page whose readers disagree (D6-3) is refused by key before anything changes. **`refusal()`, S6 · T5**, a second virtual with a default (none): why the last `apply()` answered false, when the command knows better than *it failed* -- an in-place annotation command keeps the sink's refusal by key (`error.pdf.appearanceUnsupported`), and `CommandJournal::run` returns it instead of `error.command.failed`, so the status bar can say what stopped the change. **Returning false means nothing changed**, and revert is the exact inverse — both measured, not trusted (`tst_journal`, `tst_reversibility`). | Implemented by every file in `core/cmd/commands/`; run by `CommandJournal` |
| `core/cmd/CommandJournal.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S3 · T5, extended T8 and T9; **S6 · T4 the show step**) | **Since S6 · T4 a SHOW step** (`setShowHandler`, D6-2): after every apply, undo and redo -- before anything is recorded, counted or announced -- the product's handler composes and verifies the update Save would write and hands it to the engine; a refusal takes the operation back exactly and leaves no trace, so an edit Save could not write is refused when it is made. Runs commands; maintains undo and redo stacks; groups compound operations into one undo step; pre-flights `Permissions::may()`; bumps `Revision`; emits `mutated(Scope)` and `dirtyChanged`; appends to the `RecoveryJournal`. Pure → `tst_journal`. **As built at T5:** the only holder of a mutable `Document` (D3-10) — `document()` is a const reference. `run()` returns `Status`, so a refusal's key reaches `ui/`. **Dirty is *position differs from saved position***, and a run that discards redo past the saved position makes it unreachable. A refused apply that changed `Document::modelHash()` is a **partial application**, reported to a replaceable handler (assert in debug, log in release). Subscriptions are tokens, `core/settings/Settings`' pattern. Groups: the step is created by the first run, nested groups fold into the outer, an empty group records nothing, undo is refused while one is open. **From T9** it tells an `OperationRecorder` every run, group end, undo, redo and save before the call returns, and `abandonOpenGroup()` takes back a half-run group without trace, for replay. | `core/doc/Document`, `core/cmd/RecoveryJournal`, `MutationSink`, everything in `ui/` |
| `core/cmd/OperationRecorder.h` | ✅ (S3 · T9) | **A row the file map did not have**, added with the file. What `CommandJournal` tells a durable recorder: a run (and whether it opens a group), a group end, an undo or redo of a whole step, and a save at a path — each before the operation returns. An interface so the journal, the only holder of a mutable `Document`, depends on no class that writes files; a recorder that fails degrades itself and cannot refuse the edit. | `core/cmd/CommandJournal`, `core/cmd/RecoveryJournal` |
| `core/cmd/CommandRegistry.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S3 · T5) | Type-id → command for every row of `core/cmd/Commands.def`, so the recovery journal and batch runner can deserialize one. **Generated from the table, not filled at runtime** (D3-12): there is no `add<T>()` to forget. `deserialize` is a parser of hostile input — an unknown type is `NotFound` (`error.command.unknownType`, context cut to 64 characters), a malformed known one `ParseFailure` (`error.command.malformed`). `tools/mutation-lint` (T6) asserts every subclass is registered. → `tst_command_serialize`. | `core/cmd/Commands.def`, `core/cmd/commands/*`, `core/cmd/RecoveryJournal`, `core/batch/BatchRunner` |
| `core/cmd/Commands.def` | ✅ (S3 · T5) | **The command table** (D3-12): one X-macro row per command, `RAWPAGE_COMMAND(Class, "type.id", "header")` — the class, its permanent serialized name, and the header that declares it. `CommandRegistry` is generated from it and `tools/mutation-lint` reads it as text (convention 8). One row at T5: `RotatePageCommand`, `page.rotate`. A type id is written into recovery journals, so a row's id never changes. | `core/cmd/CommandRegistry`, `tools/mutation-lint` |
| `core/cmd/RecoveryJournal.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S3 · T9) **S6 · T11 the side files** (D6-23, D6-38(g)): a serialized string over 64 KiB as `<sha256>.side`, through the existing whole-file write, before the line naming it; replay checks each hash; a seeded journal copies them; a save and a discard remove them | Append-and-fsync `journal.ndjson` plus `meta.json` per open document; discovery, source-match validation, and ordered replay with a stop-at-failure report. Degrades to memory-only if the directory is unwritable. **As built (D3-11):** `<root>/<DocumentId key>-<pid>-<n>/` holding a `QLockFile`, `meta.json` (replaced atomically) and one flushed and `syncToDisk`ed line per operation — undo and redo lines carrying the whole step. Discovery skips a directory whose lock is live; replay rebuilds the document and its history by undo step, stops at a malformed step keeping the ones before it, abandons a group that fails part-way, drops an unterminated trailing group and ignores a torn trailing line, each reported; an undo of a step from before the last save replays as the commands' `revert()`. A save rewrites `meta.json` and truncates the lines. An unwritable directory, a null `IDurableFiles` or an unserializable command degrade to memory and warn once. Two waivers. **S3 · T11:** a directory name already on disk is never reused — Windows reuses process ids, and a new session matching a crashed one's pid and counter overwrote that journal before anyone was asked (Stage 3 guide I-11) — and `meta.json` carries `"untitled":true` for a document recovered into a new file, until its first save gives it one, so a second crash offers it as a new file again. **S3 · T12:** `resolveRoot(configured, fallback)` — `documents/recoveryDir` when absolute, else the composition's root, else the default — read by `ui/shell/DocumentWindow` and `app/RecoveryPrompt`; this file still names no `core/settings/`. → `tst_recovery`, `tst_recoverykill`, `tst_stage3settings`. | `core/cmd/OperationRecorder`, `core/cmd/CommandRegistry`, `core/doc/DocumentId`, `platform::IDurableFiles`, `app/RecoveryPrompt` |
| `core/cmd/ActionId.def`, `ActionId.h` | ✅ | **One declarative table, four artifacts** (D1-6): the `.def` is an X-macro list of `RAWPAGE_ACTION(Enumerator, "string.id", DefaultContext, "action.catalog.key")`, and the enum, the string↔id maps, the default-context map and the `action.*` key list are all generated from it — so a typo is a build error *on the row that contains it*, verified by negative control. **327 actions declared** across `Features.md` §1–§7 and §9; declaring is not registering. The string id is a permanent public name written to disk, and `RAWPAGE_ACTION_ALIAS` rows keep a renamed id resolving forever (D1-16) — none yet, and the mechanism is tested before its first real use. Ribbon buttons, menu items, shortcuts and CLI verbs all resolve to one of these — never to a slot directly. | `core/cmd/ActionRegistry`, `ui/shell/RibbonTabBuilder`, `app/CommandLine`, `tools/i18n-lint` (reads the `.def` as text) |
| `core/cmd/ActionRegistry.{h,cpp}` | ✅ | `ActionId` → handler, enablement predicate, checked-state, icon, and tooltip; the label comes from `ActionId.def`, not from the caller, so nobody can disagree with the table. `dispatch(ActionId, KeyContext)` is the single entry point for all input. **Two accessors arrived at T11 because a caller finally needed them:** `isToggle()`, the question `isChecked()` cannot answer since it conflates "not a toggle" with "a toggle that is off" — without it `ActionFactory` had never called `setCheckable` and its header's promise about toggles had gone untested for want of a toggle (I-21); and `tooltipKey()`, a `Registration` field that had been unreadable since T3. **Three pinned properties:** an unregistered id returns `NotFound` rather than asserting or silently no-opping; a registered-but-disabled id returns the *distinct* `Unavailable`, so a CLI can tell "does not exist" from "not available right now"; and registration is **not** idempotent — a second claim on an id is an error and the first survives. Not thread-safe by design: composition and dispatch are both main-thread. Pure → `tst_actionregistry`. | `ui/shell/ShortcutBinder`, `ui/shell/Ribbon`, `app/CommandLine`, `core/batch/BatchRunner` **`Registration::unavailableKey` arrived at S2 · T14 with Stage 1's I-53**, and it is a SECOND field rather than a reuse of `tooltipKey`: the two are shown in mutually exclusive states and say opposite things ("what this does" against "why it cannot"), so one field serving both puts *needs a document to be open* on an enabled button — which is what the first attempt at I-53 actually produced. Stage 2 guide issue I-10. **S3 · T10 adds `Registration::tooltipText` and `unavailableText`** — functions returning already-resolved text, each read before its key — because a key cannot say *Undo: Rotate page 3 by 90 degrees* or *this document is encrypted*: the first needs a command's own name and the second a document's refusal. They are written in `ui/`, where `tr()` lives; `core/` stores them and produces no sentence (convention 5). |
| `core/cmd/KeyContext.h` | ✅ | The input contexts of [`Features.md` §8](./Features.md#8-key-bindings-authoritative-reference): `Global, Common, Viewer, Write, AnnotationTools, PageThumbnails, Bookmarks, Comments, FormsDesign, FormsFill, FindSearch, FullScreen` — **twelve**, being §8's eleven contexts plus `Common`. This enum is why one chord can mean two things. `Common` is present even though focus is never *in* it: resolution is layered — innermost context, then `Common`, then `Global` — and the store must be able to hold a binding at the `Common` layer. `ShortcutBinder::activeContext()` never returns it. | `core/cmd/KeyBindingStore`, `ui/shell/ShortcutBinder` |
| `core/cmd/Chord.h`, `Chord.cpp`, `KeyBindingDefaults.def`, `KeyBindingStore.{h,cpp}` | ✅ | `(chord, KeyContext) → ActionId`, seeded from the §8 defaults, overridable per user, persisted in settings. Resolution is **innermost context, then its fallback, then `Common`, then `Global`** — that order *is* the model. **The fallback is S6 · T5's** (D6-9, `KeyContext.h`'s `keyContextFallback`): **Annotation tools resolves through the Viewer**, so a tool armed on the page keeps every page chord; it is the one chain, and every other context keeps its single step. Detects and **reports** (does not forbid) a remap that shadows a `Global` chord, because §8 ships six such collisions by design. **Changed at T14:** an override **replaces** an action's chords in its context rather than adding one — the persisted family is `shortcuts/<context>/<action>` = ONE chord, so a rebind that left the §8 default firing was a state no restart could reproduce. The rule is derived from the overrides and nothing new is persisted, so a settings file written before T14 means exactly what it meant. Found by building the Shortcuts page, the first caller that ever remapped a chord for a user rather than for a test. Emits the **two-class collision census**: class 1 (a context takes a chord back from `Global`) = **6**, class 2 (a context overrides `Common`, which §8 explicitly permits) = **9**, and Write mode's **13** = 9 over Viewer + 4 over Global + 0 over Common (**12**, 8 over Viewer, until 2026-10-04, when Stage 6's Q1 made `Ctrl+Alt+M` a Viewer chord). `shadows()` reports class 1 only. **`Chord` is first-party rather than `QKeySequence`**, which lives in QtGui and `core/` may not touch — `ui/shell/ShortcutBinder` converts at the boundary. Pure → `tst_keybindings`, which **parses `Features.md` §8 itself** and asserts the store matches it in both directions (D1-9), verified by negative control. | `ui/shell/ShortcutBinder`, `ui/dialogs/ShortcutsPage`, `core/settings/Settings` |
| `core/cmd/commands/PageCommands.{h,cpp}` | 🟡 (**S3 · T5 rotate ✅**, S7 rest) | Rotate, insert, delete, move, duplicate, extract, replace, crop, resize, renumber — one `Command` subclass each. `RotatePageCommand` is the first-milestone mutation. **As built:** a **relative quarter turn** (90, 180, 270 clockwise; −90 normalized to 270), flow D's `{page, +90}`, so its inverse is exact in any state without remembering a *from*. It validates, asks the sink, and changes the model only once the sink accepts. A serialized page index above ISO 32000-1 Annex C's object limit is refused. → `tst_command_serialize`, `tst_reversibility`. | `core/doc/PageTree`, `MutationSink`, `ui/panes/ThumbnailsPane` |
| `core/cmd/commands/AnnotationCommands.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (**S6 · T3 `AddAnnotationCommand` and `DeleteAnnotationCommand`**; **S6 · T5 `EditAnnotationCommand`, `MoveAnnotationCommand`, `SetAnnotationAppearanceCommand`, `LockAnnotationCommand`**; S6 · T9 reply and status; **S6 · T13 `ImportAnnotationsCommand`**, the last of §4's eleven) | Add, edit, move, delete, reply, set-status, set-appearance, lock, import-merge — **each over a SET of annotations, one undo step** (D6-1), not one class per kind. **At T3:** `annot.add` adds annotations RawPage created (`core::newAnnotation` -- a `Created` identity, its `/NM` generated once and serialized with the command, D6-4) at the end of each one's page listing; `annot.delete` removes every listing of each named annotation (object number, page and position, or `/NM`), keeping what it removed for its revert. Both tell the store and the sink the same PLACEMENTS (`core::AnnotationPlacement`), sink first, model second; both override `Command::permitted` to ask `Permissions::mayAnnotate` for every page they touch (D6-3). Hostile serialized input refused by `Result` under `AnnotationCommandLimits`. → `tst_annotationops`, `tst_command_serialize`, `tst_reversibility`. **At T5, the four that change annotations IN PLACE**, one shape: each works out against the document what each named annotation BECOMES, keeps what it WAS, and tells the sink (`MutationSink::replaceAnnotations`) and the store (`AnnotationStore::replace`) the same pair -- `annot.edit` carries whole annotations (a resize, new text), `annot.move` an offset in page units (a drag, a burst of nudges), `annot.setAppearance` colour, interior colour, opacity and border width, `annot.lock` the three lock bits set and cleared; each stamps `/M` with the instant it carries (D6-4). **D6-27 in `permitted()`**: `Locked` refuses a move, a resize, a restyle and a delete and leaves the text editable (ISO 32000-1 Table 165), `LockedContents` refuses the text, `ReadOnly` every change -- by key, the page named; `annot.lock` alone is never refused for a flag. → `tst_annotedit`. **At T9, the two that ANSWER one** (D6-12, D6-36): `annot.reply` (`ReplyToAnnotationCommand`, `core::newReply`'s `Text` notes) and `annot.setStatus` (`SetAnnotationStatusCommand`, `core::newStateReply`'s hidden state replies, a review status or a checkmark), each on `AddAnnotationCommand`'s add path and each checking its own promise in `apply()` and in its parser -- a parent the document holds on the answer's page, a state its model allows; a `ReadOnly` parent refused (D6-27), a DIRECT one refused by key (`error.edit.annotationDirect`, guide I-14), a `Locked` one answered. Nine of the plan's twelve. → `tst_annotthreads`, `tst_command_serialize`, `tst_reversibility`. **At T13:** `annot.import` -- `ImportAnnotationsCommand` -- ADDS created annotations and REPLACES held ones in place as ONE step (replaced first, added second, a failure part-way put back), each half checked again in `apply()` and the parser (D6-40(b)); `importCheck` is what an import, a migration and a paste ask of each annotation before a command exists -- the shape a command carries, the page's permission, the backend's `annotationWritable`. `addCreated` hands the sink's refusal back by key. | `core/doc/AnnotationStore`, `core/doc/AnnotationGeometry`, `MutationSink` |
| `core/cmd/commands/AttachmentCommands.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S6 · T11)** — **a row the FileMap did not have** (the plan's §4 marked it ⚠️), added with the file | `AddAttachmentCommand` (`attachment.add`) and `DeleteAttachmentCommand` (`attachment.delete`) -- the last two of the eleven commands `Stage6-Annotations-Plan.md` §4 names -- each over a SET of document-level files, named by KEY, one undo step (D6-1, D6-17). The add CARRIES each file's bytes, so a replay, an undo and a redo attach the same file -- its journal line moves them to a side file over 64 KiB (D6-23); the delete carries the keys and keeps what it removed. Sink first, model second; scope `nothing` (an attachment is no ink). `Operation::Attach`, so Stage 10 can answer it by its own permission bit. A file attached at a place on a page is a `FileAttachment`, made by `AddAnnotationCommand`. Each with its `Commands.def` row and `[serialize:]`/`[revert:]` cases (`mutation-lint` names eleven). | `core/doc/Attachments`, `core/backend.h`, `ui/shell/MainWindow` |
| `core/cmd/commands/FormCommands.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S9) | Create/delete/edit field, set value, set tab order, reset form, import data, flatten form. | `core/doc/FormModel`, `MutationSink` |
| `core/cmd/commands/BookmarkCommands.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S7) | Create, rename, delete, re-parent, reorder, set destination or action, bulk-generate. | `core/doc/Outline`, `MutationSink` |
| `core/cmd/commands/StructureCommands.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S7) | Layer and tag mutations: create/delete/merge/flatten layers, retype and move tags. *(`SetAltText` removed 2026-08-23.)* | `core/doc/LayerTree`, `core/doc/TagTree`, `MutationSink` |
| `core/cmd/commands/SecurityCommands.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S10) | Apply/remove password or certificate security, apply a scheme, apply redactions, sanitize. Every one is a document mutation and therefore a `Command`. | `core/security/*`, `MutationSink` |
| `core/cmd/commands/ContentCommands.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S13) — **re-tagged 2026-10-04 from *S6 typewriter, S13 full*** by `Stage6-Annotations-Plan.md` Q2 (D6-8): typewriter text is a `FreeText` annotation, made by `AnnotationCommands` | Review-mode content edits: edit a text run, move/scale/delete an object, insert an image, change z-order. | `core/text/PageTextModel`, `MutationSink` |
| `core/cmd/commands/ReflowCommands.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S13) | Write-mode edits against `core/reflow/ReflowDocument`, coalesced so a burst of typing is one undo step. | `core/reflow/*`, `MutationSink` |
| `core/cmd/commands/DecorationCommands.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S7) | Watermark, header/footer, Bates, imposition, flatten, and their remove/update inverses. Expresses intent only — the realization lives in `pdf/ops/DecorationOps`. | `MutationSink`, `core/settings/ProfileStore` (the saved specs) |

### 3.4 `core/render/` — rendering policy (no rasterization here)

| File | Status | Role | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|
| `core/render/DpiPolicy.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S2 · T10)** | Turns (zoom, monitor scale, monitor physical DPI) into the *device* resolution a tile must be rasterized at, and decides when a scale change requires re-rasterization versus a cheap resample. Pure → `tst_dpipolicy`. **The file most likely to be the root cause of a blurry-text bug.** **Actual Size is the monitor's PHYSICAL DPI** ([`Features.md` §1.2](./Features.md#12-page-display)) and the OS scale factor is deliberately absent from the raster resolution — it is carried out separately as `Resolution::devicePixelRatio`, which is what makes moving a window between two monitors of equal glass DPI and different scale factors a **resample rather than a re-render**. `requiresRerender()` compares the DPI alone and divides the larger by the smaller, so the answer is identical going up and coming down: hysteresis here means two monitors that disagree about which of them is sharp. `Resolution` carries an **`upscale`** beside the DPI, because capping without a compensating factor silently shrinks the page — the cap then reads as a zoom bug rather than as a cap. | `platform::IDisplayTopology`, `core/render/TileGrid` **`FitMode` MOVED HERE from `ui/viewer/ViewportController.h` at S2 · T15**, because `pageDisplay/defaultZoom` is a settings key and `SettingsKeys.def` may only name `core/` types — the alternative being a second enum in `core/` listing four of its five values, which is the two-lists-that-agree-until-Friday shape both that file's rule 2 and `ActionId.def`'s rule 3 exist to prevent. It belongs beside `Zoom` in any case: a fit IS a zoom, computed from a page and a viewport rather than chosen. `fitModeName()` is the token the settings file holds. |
| `core/render/FitPolicy.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S4 · T12)** | **Which rectangle a fit MEASURES (D4-14), and the zoom that satisfies it.** `fitRect()` answers the CropBox — **the page as a reader is shown it** — for Fit Page, Width, Height and None, and **the page's CONTENT bounding box for Fit Visible**, falling back to the CropBox where a page has no objects or the engine cannot answer; `zoomToFit()` turns a rectangle, a viewport, a margin and the monitor's PHYSICAL DPI into a clamped `Zoom`. **This is where Stage 2's I-28 is closed** — from T13 of Stage 2 until this task Fit Visible fitted the CropBox, so on every corpus file `Ctrl+3` was `Ctrl+0` under another name. **It is in `core/render/` and not in `ui/viewer/PageLayoutEngine`, where the arithmetic lived, for a testing reason rather than a taxonomic one**: I-28's observable is a NUMBER read off a real file, only `rawpage_render_tests` can open one, and that target links no widgets — before the move the assertion could not be written at all. The rotations stay in the widget: a rectangle in PDF space does not know how anybody intends to show it. **A `PageBoxes` struct rather than three `PdfRect` parameters**, for `RenderRequest`'s reason — two of three rectangles transposed compiles, and the transposition is invisible on every document whose CropBox is its MediaBox, which is how I-28 survived a month. Pure → `tst_fitpolicy`, `tst_contentbox`. | `core/Geometry.h`, `core/render/DpiPolicy`; used by `ui/viewer/PageLayoutEngine` |
| `core/render/TileGrid.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S2 · T10)** | Splits a page at a given device resolution into tiles; computes the visible, prefetch, and evict sets from a viewport rect. Pure → `tst_tilegrid`. **It never enumerates the page**: the index range the viewport covers is computed, so an A4 page at 6400 % — 14.6 gigapixels, 222,000 tiles — still costs a viewport's worth of work. A grid that built the list and filtered it would get slower the further you zoom in, which reads as a rendering problem and is a loop bound. `evict` takes the caller's HELD set, because a pure grid has no memory of the last frame. It tiles the **raster** size where `PagePresentation` lays out the **presented** size, which is the whole meaning of a capped resolution. | `core/render/RenderScheduler`, `ui/viewer/TilePresenter` |
| `core/render/RenderRequest.h` | ✅ **(S2 · T9, brought forward from T10; S3 · T8 the rotation delta)** | An immutable rasterization request: page, tile rect, device resolution, `Revision`, **the unsaved page-rotation delta** (Stage 3 T8, D3-15 — the model's rotation minus the engine's, part of the key), layer visibility, render flags (anti-alias, line-weight, transparency grid), priority. Its equality *is* the cache key. **Built at T9 rather than T10** because `IPageRasterizer::raster()` takes one and `core/backend.h` has carried a forward declaration of it since T1 — T9's exit criterion is a rasterized page, which cannot be written without the type the method takes. `DpiPolicy`, `TileGrid` and `PagePresentation` are still T10's and are built ON it. **No pointers, and the `Revision` is mandatory rather than defaulted** (D2-12): a pointer member makes the key identity-based and the cache then misses on every lookup with no symptom but slowness, and a defaulted `Revision` makes a stale tile after a Stage 3 mutation the DEFAULT behaviour. `isValid()` enforces both, and the `std::hash` specialization is supplied beside `operator==` so the two cannot disagree. The tile rect is INTEGRAL device pixels rather than `core::DeviceRect`'s doubles: two adjacent tiles that rounded a shared edge independently would disagree by a pixel, which shows as a hairline seam that moves when you zoom. | `core/render/RenderCache`, `IPageRasterizer` |
| `core/render/RenderScheduler.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S2 · T11; S5 · T2 the text job; S5 · T3 `CachePolicy`; S5 · T8 the engine task; S5 · T9 exclusive tasks; S5 guide I-12 the inversion count; S6 · T4 a mutation cancels only what it reaches)** | **Since S6 · T4 a mutation cancels only what it reaches** (Stage 6 guide I-06): a queued, running or finished tile of the document the scope does not reach (`render::tileTouchedBy`) is RE-KEYED to the new revision where it stands -- a queued one meeting an equal request at the new revision joins it -- instead of every tile of the old revision being cancelled; `documentChanged` takes the engine's page tree to place a scope's rectangles, and `Stats::rekeyed` counts what it kept. **Since S5 guide I-12 (after T14) `Stats::startedWhileMoreUrgentWaited`**: a job started while a strictly more urgent one was pending, counted AT THE PICK under the lock that decides it -- zero by strict priority except where an exclusive task holds a document back. `bench_pipeline --stage5` reads its inversions here; it had inferred them from start stamps taken after the pick and counted a column's first job. **Since S5 · T9 a task may be of NO document** -- a folder search's open, which has no handle until its last piece -- **and EXCLUSIVE**: it does not start while any job of its document runs, and nothing of its document starts while it runs, which is how a folder search closes a file on the render thread with any number of workers and no drain. **Since S5 · T8 (D5-12 clarified) a THIRD kind of job, the ENGINE TASK**: `enqueueTask(document, lane, callable)` runs a callable ON THE WORKER that keeps its own result, and `subscribeTasks` tells the pumping thread which ticket's task ran -- the text job's queue, priority, tickets and cancellation, `cancelDocument()` reaching it in all three states, never coalesced (a callable has no key), never cached, superseded or probed. Its first use is a document's search units, read part by part, each no longer than one page's work. **Since S5 · T3 a tile may be asked for with `CachePolicy::Bypass`** — neither looked up nor kept — for D5-4's rasters that are consumed and not shown (a comparison's); one holder asking to keep a coalesced tile keeps it. **Since S5 · T2 (D5-3) a second kind of job on the same thread**: `enqueueText(TextRequest, lane)` extracts one page through a second, per-worker factory, in the same queue under strict priority, with a fourth lane **`Background`** below `Speculative`; coalesced and raised like a tile, cancelled with its document, **never superseded** (a `TextRequest` has no revision — the text is in page space, and no command up to Stage 6 changes a page's content: the engine's S6 T4 reload re-reads the same content bytes), never cached, never probed, and **a failure is delivered**. Priority queue over requests, coalescing, cancellation on scroll, worker-pool dispatch to `IPageRasterizer`, and delivery back on the UI thread. **The UI thread never rasterizes.** **This header is where issue I-05 is closed.** D2-9's *thread-confined per worker* was measured false at T3, and the arrangement chosen here is **one render thread with a queue**: eight workers behind the engine lock retire 1.05× the tiles of one, and per-tile p95 is 0.532 ms against a 16.667 ms budget — the parallelism buys 5 % and costs a concurrency bug. `Config::workerCount` still works and defaults to 1, each worker getting its **own** rasterizer from a factory. Delivery happens on the thread that calls `deliverReady()` and **no subscriber is ever entered from a worker** — a worker's only outward call is a data-free wake handler — which is also what lets `RenderCache` be lock-free. A cancelled request that has not started is removed and the rasterizer is never entered; one already running is dropped on delivery rather than cached. **S4 · T11 adds three counters to `Stats` — `enqueuedVisible`, `enqueuedPrefetch`, `enqueuedSpeculative`, summing to `enqueued`** — because A2's named work (an unfocused window queueing at `Prefetch`) is otherwise invisible: the tiles still arrive, the picture is identical, and only the lane differs. A cache hit counts in its lane too, or the counters would disagree with `enqueued` for exactly the workload they were added to measure. | `IPageRasterizer`, `core/render/RenderCache`, `ui/viewer/TilePresenter`, `platform::IPowerSession` (throttle) **S4 · T14 adds `setLatencyProbe`** — a MEASUREMENT HOOK, and nothing in the product installs one. It reports each delivered tile's lane and the milliseconds from `enqueue()` to delivery, which is the quantity A2's product figure names. It exists because T2 could stamp its own enqueues and T14 cannot: the subject there is the shipped widgets issuing the requests (`tests/bench/bench_surface`), and without a hook here the figure the verdict rests on could only be taken from a harness that is not the product — the exact gap D2-1 exists to keep visible. The same call T11 made for its lane counters: a claim that cannot be observed from outside gets an observation rather than a comment. |
| `core/render/RenderCache.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S2 · T11; S6 · T4 `tileTouchedBy`)** | **Since S6 · T4 `render::tileTouchedBy`**, the one predicate the tiers and the scheduler share: an annotation scope's rectangle placed in a tile through the crop box, the engine tree's `/Rotate`, the unsaved delta, the view rotation and the scale, padded two device pixels -- every doubt (no tree, no page) a yes; `invalidate` takes the engine's tree. Memory-and-disk tile cache keyed by `RenderRequest`, size-capped, LRU, invalidated by `Scope` and `Revision`. Pure → `tst_rendercache`. Two tiers behind one `ITileStore` interface (D2-14) — the memory one here, the disk one below. **`invalidate()` drops what the scope touches and RE-KEYS the rest to the new revision**; without the re-key `Scope` buys nothing at all, because the revision inside every key would have moved and a one-page rotation would re-render a 900-page document. It takes a `DocumentHandle` that the plan's signature omits: a `Scope` names pages, not documents, and O7 leaves open whether one cache serves several. Not thread-safe **on purpose** — only the thread that pumps `RenderScheduler::deliverReady()` touches it. `CacheStats` is O7's instrumentation, and the number that answers O7 is `crossDocumentEvictions`. | `core/doc/Revision`, `core/doc/Scope`, `core/settings/Settings` **`setMemoryCapacityBytes` / `setDiskCapacityBytes` arrived at S2 · T15** with `performance/tileCacheMB` and `diskCacheMB`, which are changed WHILE the application runs: a person told the cache is too large expects the next scroll to obey, not the next launch. A disk capacity of zero REMOVES the tier rather than keeping an empty one, because D2-14's default is off and *installed but holding nothing* is a third state nobody asked for. |
| `core/render/DiskTileStore.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S2 · T11; S6 · T4 `tileTouchedBy`)** | **Since S6 · T4** it drops by `tileTouchedBy`, as the memory tier does, so the two tiers cannot disagree about a rectangle. The **second** cache tier — D2-14 requires the disk tier to sit behind the same `ITileStore` interface as the memory one. It spills tiles out of memory so a long document scrolled twice does not re-rasterize everything. **It does not claim to survive a restart**: the index is in memory, so the directory is purged on construction, and a cross-session cache needs an on-disk index plus a validity check with a security dimension — which belongs with **O7** at T18. **The only file in `core/render/` that writes to disk**, carrying exactly two `layer-lint` waivers, one write and one delete, because every deletion is funnelled through a single helper for that reason. The directory is a constructor argument: `core/` does not name a user's folders, so `app/` resolves `QStandardPaths::CacheLocation` and hands it down. **Off by default** (`diskCapBytes` is 0) until T18 measures whether it earns its place. | `core/render/RenderCache`, `core/settings/Settings` (`performance/diskCacheMB`, T15) |
| `core/render/FontSubstitution.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S2 · T16)** | **A row this stage's file manifest did not list**, added with the file on the precedent `DiskTileStore` set at T11 and `WinMessageWindow` at T12. **WHICH face stands in for a font a document names and does not embed** — the ordered candidate list, `normalizeBaseFontName()` for the `/BaseFont` forms real producers emit, and `isStandardFontName()` for the base-14 decline (issue **I-13**). It is here rather than in `platform/win/` because that layer makes no policy decisions and this is nothing but policy: the same judgement on every operating system, and therefore assertable by `tests/unit` with no display, no Windows and no engine — which is what makes guide case **A-66**'s *our policy, not the engine's* a claim a test can name. Its one non-obvious branch is **Han disambiguation**: the same code point is drawn differently in Simplified Chinese, Traditional Chinese and Japanese, and a reader calls the wrong one broken rather than slightly off. | `platform::IFontResolver`, `platform/win/WinFontResolver`, `pdf/render/FontResolverBridge` |
| `core/render/PagePresentation.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S2 · T10)** | Page-arrangement geometry for single / continuous / two-up / two-up-continuous / RTL, cover-page handling, and page-rect ↔ document-point mapping. Pure → `tst_presentation`. Both of plan §5.5's asymmetries fall out of **one** rule rather than two special cases: pages are grouped into spreads (a cover takes one of its own, shifting every later pair's parity); a page's column is its position within its spread and a missing partner leaves its column **empty** rather than sliding its neighbour across; and RTL maps column 0 to the right and changes nothing else — not the grouping, not the page order. Columns are as wide as the widest page in the document, so a mixed-size document does not change its content width as it scrolls. Three signature deviations from `Implementation-Plan.md` — a `Resolution` rather than a `Zoom`, a current page (without which Single and Two-Up are indistinguishable from their continuous twins) and `DeviceRect` rather than `QRectF` — are recorded in the Stage 2 status block. **S5 · T5 adds `PageLayout::toPage`**: a content point in a NAMED page's space wherever it is, which a column selection's drag and a flow drag into a gap need (round-tripped outside each page in `tst_presentation`). | `ui/viewer/PageLayoutEngine`, `ui/viewer/ViewportController` |

### 3.5 `core/text/` — text, selection, search, indexing

| File | Status | Role | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|
| `core/text/PageTextModel.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S5 · T2; T4 `rules()`)** | **Since T4 it also carries the page's RULING LINES** — thin horizontal and vertical paths, `rules()` — because D5-6's ruled tables need them and a model of characters has none. **One entry per Unicode scalar, not runs** (D5-2): each character's loose box and origin in unrotated page space, its angle, its size and an index into a deduplicated font table (name, flags, weight — `FontIdentity::isBold()`/`isItalic()` fall back to the flags and the standard name, because a base-14 font reports weight 0), and three flags — **generated**, **hyphen**, **unmapped**. **An unmapped glyph is stored as U+FFFD whatever it arrives as** — enforced in `append()`, where every producer passes — and `hasExtractableText()` is the question every consumer asks first. Hydrated from `ITextExtractor`. **Not** a reading order (`LayoutAnalyzer`'s) and not a normalized string (`TextNormalizer`'s). Pure → `tst_textmodel`; the substrate for selection, find, copy, and the reflow converter. | `ITextExtractor`, `core/text/TextSelection`, `core/reflow/FixedToReflowConverter` |
| `core/text/TextNormalizer.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S5 · T4)** — **a row the FileMap did not have** (plan §10), added with the file | D5-5's ONE pipeline for find, search, whole-word matching and the comparison: per cluster NFKC, simple case folding, diacritics stripped on request, with an **offset map** from each output unit to the first and last model character, so a hit maps to whole glyphs; line-final hyphens joined before a lowercase letter and kept otherwise; unmapped glyphs produce nothing. QtCore only — no ICU. Pure → `tst_normalize`. | `core/text/PageTextModel`, `core/text/LayoutAnalyzer`, `core/text/SearchEngine`, `core/text/DocumentComparer` |
| `core/text/LayoutAnalyzer.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S5 · T4)** | Groups characters into lines, lines into blocks, blocks into a logical order; detects columns and table grids — **geometry only** (D5-6), never the model's order: vertical CJK lines, baseline bands, lines at a 0.8 em gap with word gaps reported, right-to-left lines by UAX #9 L1–L2 (no explicit embeddings), ruled tables from `PageTextModel::rules()` and unruled ones by alignment with short cells, blocks by leading, list markers, XY-cut order. Non-finite geometry is reported unplaced and every loop is bounded (D5-21). `const TagTree*` is null for every Stage 5 caller. **Measured on §3.2's targets at T4 — every one met on the generated corpus, and not out of sample** (R1's row). **S5 guide I-07 (T12):** a band under ONE column of the band above it continues that column, so a column longer than its neighbour is read whole -- A-38 unchanged by the fix. Pure → `tst_layout`, `tst_layoutcorpus`, `fuzz_text_pipeline`. **The quality of Write mode and Reflow view both rest on this one file** — which is why it outlived the 2026-08-23 accessibility removal; Read Aloud was only ever a third consumer. | `core/text/PageTextModel`, `core/doc/TagTree`, `core/reflow/FixedToReflowConverter` |
| `core/text/TextSelection.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S5 · T5; S6 · T6 the markup geometry)** | **Since S6 · T6** `markupQuadsOn` gives a selection's runs as text markup's quadrilaterals -- Acrobat's corner order, in the LINE's frame, so a vertical line's top is its right side -- and `insertionPointAt` the boundary between the two characters either side of a caret, at its line's foot (D6-12, guide A-49, A-52). Selection as a range over the DOCUMENT's READING order — two carets, each a page and a position in `LayoutAnalyzer`'s sequence, so a selection crosses pages and a drag between columns takes the end of one and the start of the next (D5-8) — or a COLUMN, a rectangle on one page taking the spans of every line it crosses. **`PageReading`** is one page read once (the analyzer runs there) with each position's line and block kept beside it; **`DocumentText`** the pages a selection has read; hit-testing finds the nearest line, then the character, and the caret on the side of its centre read in the character's OWN direction; word, line and block expansion; **`copy()`** writes plain text (lines as seen, a blank line between blocks and pages), HTML (paragraphs, D5-5's hyphen rule, each run's family, size, bold and italic) and, for a column or a table block, tab-separated text and a `<table>` (D5-9). **An unmapped glyph is never copied** (D5-2): skipped, counted, and a selection of nothing else copies nothing. A page it was not given is REPORTED (`missingPages`), never guessed. Pure and headless → `tst_selection`; `fuzz_text_pipeline` drives it over hostile geometry. | `ui/viewer/SelectionOverlay`; the clipboard through Qt's `QMimeData` (plain, HTML, table — `Stage5-TextSearch-Plan.md` D5-9, D5-10) |
| `core/text/ReflowText.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S5 · T11)** — a row added with the file (`Stage5-TextSearch-Plan.md` §4) | **A page as Reflow view shows it** (D5-16 clarified): `reflowParagraphs(PageReading)` -- one paragraph per block in `LayoutAnalyzer`'s reading order, its lines JOINED (re-wrapping is the point), each carrying its page and block, a table block one paragraph per line; runs by size, weight and italic, never a face; D5-5's hyphen rule and D5-2's unmapped rule read as `TextSelection::copy` reads them (the third reading, asserted on the same lines). `blockAtTop(PageReading, DepthBelowEdge)` is the place on the page view's side: the first block in reading order not wholly above the viewport's top edge, measured along the SCREEN's down -- `DepthBelowEdge`, linear in page space, because page-space -y is down only on an upright page (S5 guide **I-06**; an upright overload takes a height). Pure and headless → `tst_reflowtext`. | `core/text/TextSelection` (`PageReading`), `core/text/LayoutAnalyzer`, `ui/viewer/ReflowView` |
| `core/text/SearchEngine.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S5 · T6: page text; S5 · T8 the four other content classes, D5-12; a folder is T9's, D5-13)** | Executes a `SearchQuery` over one document: literal, whole-word, case/diacritic folding, and regex. **Since S5 · T8 over all five content classes**: a run's SLOTS are the pages in scope -- each matched once its text AND, with comments or fields included, its search-unit part are held -- and, for a whole-document search including bookmarks or attachments, one document slot ahead of them; `findInUnit()` matches a unit's string through `TextNormalizer::normalizeString`, the page's own pipeline, with the same match loop as a page; hits come in document order (the document's units, then page by page, text before comments and fields) and a field shown by several widgets is one hit; a slot waiting for one piece re-asks for one the index dropped meanwhile; a class switched off is never read. **As built (D5-3, D5-5): two halves.** The MATCH is pure -- `compile()` normalizes a literal through `TextNormalizer`'s query path, or compiles PCRE2 over a pattern NFKC-normalized and mark-stripped like the page and never case-folded (case is `CaseInsensitiveOption`, because folding `\S` makes `\s`), and REFUSES an empty pattern or a regex that does not compile (`error.search.emptyPattern`, `error.search.invalidPattern` naming it -- guide A-57: an empty result would read as *not found*); `findOnPage()` finds it in a `SearchablePage`, block by block so nothing matches across a paragraph, with WHOLE WORDS as a boundary test (a word character meeting anything else, or either side of an ideograph -- UAX #29 without a dictionary, so in CJK each ideograph is a word, D5-5's recorded caveat) and a SELECTION scope honoured per page. The RUN is asynchronous: `start(entry, query, handlers)` asks the index for every page in scope it does not hold, at `Background` and at most `kExtractionWindow` (32) at a time, matches pages on a pool of its own (half the hardware's threads, one to four -- never the UI thread, never the engine thread, guide A-67), and delivers on the pumping thread from `deliverReady()`, the scheduler's contract. `maxResults` keeps the first N hits in document order and says so (`limitReached`, A-61), and stops asking for pages once the pages done from the start already hold more; UNMAPPED glyphs match nothing, not even `.`, and the outcome lists every page in scope holding them with the count (A-21's find half). `SearchSession` is one caller's current search: `start` cancels the previous run AFTER the new one has asked, so an index build both want is not re-asked per keystroke, and its destructor cancels. **Measured (A-63, `text-900.pdf`, 7.2 M characters):** the first search, which builds the index, 1.7 s; twenty literal queries after it, **p95 13.2 ms** against the guide's 100 ms. **Composed into the product at S5 · T7** with its first widget, the find bar: `app/CompositionRoot` builds it after the index and pumps it with its own wake handler, and `app/Services.h` carries it. **Since S6 · T9 (D6-11) an open document's COMMENTS are its store's**: `commentUnits(store)` makes a part per page (`kStoreCommentsPart`, each unit's locator the annotation's identity, state replies left out), a run handed them takes none from the engine's page parts -- which it then asks for only for fields -- so an unsaved comment is found and a hit can select it; a folder search hands none and reads the file. → `tst_annotthreads`, `tst_commentscale`. | `core/text/DocumentIndex`, `core/text/SearchQuery`, `core/text/TextNormalizer`, `core/text/TextSelection` |
| `core/text/SearchQuery.h` | ✅ **(S5 · T6; S5 · T8 the content-class switches)** | **Since S5 · T8 `SearchClasses`**, the four `search/include*` switches, read by `fromSettings()` with the flags: a page range searches the comments and fields on its pages and not the document's bookmarks or attachments, and a selection is page text alone. The query value type: pattern, the four matching flags (`SearchFlags`, which give `TextNormalizer` its options so the page and the query are normalized alike), a scope -- the whole document, a page range, or inside a `TextSelection` (D5-14) -- and `maxResults`. **`fromSettings()` is the one reader of the `search/*` flags and bound**, because the find bar's toggles WRITE those keys (D5-14, D4-5's one truth per fact). The four `search/include*` keys, declared at T6, were read by nothing until T8 built the reader they switch. | `core/text/SearchEngine`, `core/settings/Settings`, `ui/write/FindReplaceBar` (T7), `ui/panes/SearchPane` (T9) |
| `core/text/DocumentIndex.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S5 · T6; S5 · T8 the search units; S5 guide I-13 the releaser; S6 · T4 the aspect)** | **Since S6 · T4 (D6-10) an annotation-only scope keeps a page's text** and drops its search units alone, told to subscribers as an EVICTION (the text is what it was). **Since S5 guide I-13 (after T14) what it lets go of is freed OFF the calling thread**: a dropped, evicted or invalidated document's pages and parts go, as shared pointers, to a releaser thread of the index's own, started when first needed and drained before the entries are destroyed -- a 900-page entry had taken 36-51 ms to free on the UI thread, twice in every folder search and when a searched tab closed. **Since S5 · T8 (D5-12) it also keeps each document's SEARCH UNITS**, part by part (`core/text/SearchUnit.h`): `requestUnits(owner, entry, part, lane)` mirrors `request()` -- one outstanding read per part, owners, release, re-sent when the leader's handle detaches -- and runs the reader `setUnitReader()` was given (`app/CompositionRoot` wires `IDocumentBackend::searchUnits`) as an engine task; each part is read once, stored failed rather than retried, dropped with its page by a `Scope`, counted in the budget and evicted with its document. **Where extracted text LIVES** (D5-4): per document, built lazily, so a second search of a 900-page file is instant. **As built:** keyed by `DocumentId` -- a window ATTACHES its handle with the document's identity, and two handles over one identity are ONE entry, so `window.new`'s companion extracts nothing its leader has (guide A-66; Stage 4 T14 measured 1,369 cross-document evictions from keying by handle); a handle with no stated identity is an entry of its own. Each `IndexedPage` holds the engine's model and builds, on first use and on whichever thread asks, the `PageReading` (a selection's on the UI thread, a search's on its worker) and one `SearchablePage` per normalization -- D5-5's pipeline per BLOCK, its offsets turned into reading positions. Every text request in the product goes through `request(owner, entry, page, lane)`: one ticket per page, RAISED when a more urgent owner asks, cancelled only when its last owner lets go (a cancelled search keeps a selection's page), sent through the entry's first handle and **re-sent through the next when that handle detaches** -- the leader closing under a companion. **A mutation's `Scope` drops the pages it names** (A-64). **Its own budget, `search/indexCacheMB`**, outside the tile cache (O7's revisit): whole documents evicted, least recently SEARCHED first, a document never searched before any that was, and **the document being searched never evicted for itself** -- kept when alone over the budget, and said once per episode (A-65). **Memory only**, the entry dropped with its last handle. *(This row said "shares the cache directory policy", a policy that does not exist -- `DiskTileStore` does not ship, O7 -- corrected at T6 as the plan's §10 required.)* **Measured:** `text-900.pdf`'s complete index -- models, readings and one normalized form -- is **835 MB** (A-63), which is what the 1024 MB default was checked against. **Since S6 · T12 (D6-14, D6-39(g)) a page's PATHS are kept beside its text**: `requestGeometry` mirrors `requestUnits` -- the reader `setGeometryReader` was given (`app/` wires `IDocumentBackend::pageGeometry`) run as an ENGINE TASK, once per page, keyed past every page index (`kGeometryBase`), `Event::GeometryArrived`; a failed read stored empty; dropped with a CONTENT scope, kept through an annotation's; counted in the budget, released off the calling thread. → `tst_index` (`[A-91]`), `tests/render/tst_measuregeometry`. | `core/render/RenderScheduler` (its text jobs feed it), `core/text/PageTextModel`, `core/text/TextSelection`, `core/text/TextNormalizer`, `core/text/SearchEngine`, `ui/viewer/SelectionOverlay`, `ui/shell/DocumentWindow`, `app/CompositionRoot` |
| `core/text/SearchUnit.h` | ✅ **(S5 · T8)** — **a row the FileMap did not have** (plan §4), added with the file | **What a search finds outside the page text** (D5-12): a comment, a form field, a bookmark or an attachment as a plain SEARCH UNIT -- `ContentClass`, which string of its object (`Field`), the text, its page or -1, its page-space rectangle, a locator naming the object, a bookmark's `Destination`, an attached PDF's page -- and `SearchUnitPart`, what one read delivered. **Strings with places, not models**: Stage 6's and Stage 9's editing models are not built here, and become the source when they exist. Its header argues why a document is read in PARTS -- a page's annotations, `kOutlinePart`, `kAttachmentsPart` -- each no longer than one page's work. | `core/backend.h` (`searchUnits`), `core/text/DocumentIndex`, `core/text/SearchEngine`, `pdf/render/PdfiumDocument` |
| `core/text/SearchResults.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S5 · T9)** — **a row the FileMap did not have** (plan §4), added with the file | **What an Advanced Search found, and everything done with the list** (D5-13), pure: each file's `FileResult` -- its hits with their snippets, its Info, or why it was not searched (an encrypted file is `PermissionDenied`) -- **relevance DEFINED** as the file's hit count, ties by path, and the page and file sorts; the filter by author and subject (contains, any case) and a date (`/ModDate`, else `/CreationDate`, inclusive), a filter excluding a file whose Info lacks what it asks; refine as `hitPages()` and `keepOnly()`; a snippet as five whole words either side of the hit, never an unmapped glyph; and the CSV's bytes -- a UTF-8 byte-order mark, RFC 4180 quoting, CRLF, the headings the caller's. | `core/text/FolderSearch`, `ui/panes/SearchPane` |
| `core/text/FolderSearch.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S5 · T9)** — **a row the FileMap did not have** (plan §4), added with the file | **Advanced Search over a folder** (D5-13 clarified): lists the folder -- recursive or not -- on a HELPER thread of its own, then one file at a time: `IDocumentBackend::beginOpen`/`continueOpen`, each ENGINE piece as an engine task of no document at `Background` and every other piece on the helper; the file attached to `DocumentIndex` under its identity and searched by `SearchEngine` with the query (all five classes, the flags, what is left of the bound); its hits snippeted; then detached, `cancelDocument`, and closed by an EXCLUSIVE engine task. Never a tab, never a password: an encrypted file is a `FileResult` refused with `PermissionDenied`. `cancel()` stops at once, and the piece in flight cleans up after itself -- an opening abandoned or a handle closed -- whichever of the two finishes second. Its helper's results reach the pumping thread through `deliverReady()` and a wake handler; `setPieceObserver` is a measurement hook (guide A-88, the bench's folder column). | `core/backend.h`, `core/render/RenderScheduler`, `core/text/DocumentIndex`, `core/text/SearchEngine`, `core/text/SearchResults`, `ui/panes/SearchPane`, `tests/bench/bench_pipeline` |
| `core/text/CatalogIndex.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S16) | First-party inverted index over a folder tree: build, incremental update, purge, stop words, metadata fields. Implements `IIndexer`. See risk R2. | `IIndexer`, `core/batch/BatchRunner`, `ui/panes/SearchPane` |
| `core/text/DocumentComparer.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S5 · T12)** | **Two documents compared in three layers (D5-17), pure `core/`, over inputs the engine thread produced** -- each side a list of `ComparePage` (a `PageReading`, the crop box, the rotation, the label). (1) **Pages aligned first**: body words hashed and the page sequences diffed for anchors; between anchors the leftovers paired in order by the Dice similarity of their body words; a leftover anywhere paired with one anywhere else as MOVED; the rest of a gap paired by position, and what has no partner INSERTED or DELETED. (2) **Each pair's words diffed** by Myers' O(ND) algorithm in linear space (diff-match-patch's `bisect`) over `TextNormalizer`'s words, case kept: hunks split by region, then insertions, deletions and replacements, and a deleted run equal to an inserted run of at least `minMoveTokens` words is a MOVE -- tried at each SLIDE of a pure run, the matching slide applied to the script. **The bound**: a pair past `replaceShare` of its words, or `maxEdits`, is REPLACED, the forward search abandoned at half the cap. At *text and layout*, matched words shifted past `layoutShiftPoints` are LAYOUT differences; with `ignoreWhitespace` off, a measured change in the spaces between two matched words on one line is WHITESPACE. (3) At *appearance*, 72 dpi rasters from a `RasterProvider` compared outside every rectangle layer 2 reported and every word an ignore-list dropped, the differing pixels clustered into rectangles. **The ignore-lists**: the header/footer band (a block wholly in the top or bottom tenth whose text, digits normalized, recurs on half the pages and three, over BOTH documents), page numbers (digits-only in the band or equal to the page's label), whitespace. `ComparisonRun` runs it on a thread of its own with progress and cancel between pairs, delivering on the pump. → `tst_compare` (headless), `tests/render/tst_comparecorpus` (A-95 … A-102). **S5 · T13:** `CompareOptions::fromSettings`, the one reader of the four `documents/compare*` keys (D5-20), and `ComparisonRun::setReadingSource` -- a page handed in without its reading has it made ON THE RUN'S THREAD, the cancel honoured between pages, so the product hands over each page's extracted text and the UI thread analyzes nothing (D5-18 clarified (c)); the sensitivity enum moved to `core/text/CompareSensitivity.h`. Reused in S11 to answer "was this post-signature change permitted?" | `core/text/PageTextModel`, `core/text/TextSelection` (`PageReading`), `core/text/TextNormalizer`, `core/text/CompareSensitivity.h`, `core/settings/Settings` (`fromSettings`), `core/backend.h` (`RasterImage`) -- **not ICU** (D5-5: the normalizer is QtCore's); **read by** `ui/viewer/ComparisonView`, `ui/panes/DifferencesPane`, `core/security/SignatureValidator` |
| `core/text/CompareSensitivity.h` | ✅ **(S5 · T13)** — **a row the FileMap did not have** (plan §10), added with the file | Which layers a comparison runs -- *text only*, *text and layout*, *appearance* (D5-17) -- and the token `documents/compareSensitivity` stores: the enumerator's name in lower camel case, never a number. **A header of its own, moved out of `DocumentComparer.h`**, because `core/settings/SettingsSchema.h` names it and is included by every settings reader, which would otherwise include the comparer's threads and raster types to read three words -- `core/Transition.h`'s shape for the same reason. Header-only. → `tst_stage5settings` (the token in the file, an unknown word read as the default). | none; **read by** `core/text/DocumentComparer`, `core/settings/SettingsSchema.h`, `ui/viewer/ComparisonView`, `ui/dialogs/CompareDialog` |

| File | Status | Role | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|
| `core/reflow/ReflowDocument.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S13) | The **second document model**: sections → paragraphs → runs, plus tables, anchored objects, styles, headers/footers, footnotes, fields, and a TOC model. Deliberately *not* a mode flag on `core/doc/Document`. | `core/cmd/commands/ReflowCommands`, `ui/write/WriteCanvas` |
| `core/reflow/StyleSheet.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S13) | Named paragraph and character styles, inheritance, and the resolved-format computation for a run. Pure → `tst_styles`. | `core/reflow/ReflowDocument`, `ui/write/StyleGallery` |
| `core/reflow/TableModel.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S13) | Rows, columns, spans, per-cell format, repeat-header, sort, and the formula/AutoSum evaluator with cell references. Pure → `tst_table`. | `core/reflow/ReflowDocument`, `ui/write/TableTools` |
| `core/reflow/LineBreaker.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S13) | Line breaking, justification, hyphenation, widow/orphan and keep-with-next, and column/page break placement. Pure → `tst_linebreak`. | HarfBuzz + ICU via `core/reflow/Shaper`, `core/reflow/ReflowToFixedComposer` |
| `core/reflow/Shaper.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S13) | Complex-script shaping and font fallback for document text, resolving missing fonts through `IFontResolver`. | HarfBuzz, `platform::IFontResolver` |
| `core/reflow/FixedToReflowConverter.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S13) | *Convert to Writable*: turns positioned glyphs plus the tag tree into a semantic `ReflowDocument`, and records every element it could not reconstruct. | `core/text/LayoutAnalyzer`, `core/doc/TagTree`, `core/reflow/FidelityReport` |
| `core/reflow/ReflowToFixedComposer.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S13) | The way back: lays a `ReflowDocument` out onto pages and emits the page-content **intent** through `MutationSink`. **Also emits a structure tree derived from the reflow model (decision O2)** — headings, paragraphs, lists, tables and reading order — so the semantic model persists in *standard* PDF rather than in a private stream, and a later re-conversion reads tags instead of re-inferring from glyphs. Also the *Prevent Further Edits* finalization path. | `core/reflow/LineBreaker`, `core/doc/TagTree` (**written**, not just read), `MutationSink` (realized by `pdf/cos/ContentStreamBuilder` on the far side) |
| `core/reflow/FidelityReport.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S13) | Per-element list of what the conversion lost or approximated, with page references — shown before the user commits to Write mode. | `ui/dialogs/ConversionFidelityDialog` |
| `core/reflow/ChangeTracker.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S13) | Tracked insertions, deletions, format changes, and moves with per-reviewer identity; accept/reject one, all, or all-by-reviewer. Pure → `tst_changetracking`. | `core/reflow/ReflowDocument`, `ui/write/TrackChangesBar` |
| `core/reflow/SpellChecker.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S13) | Per-language dictionaries, as-you-type checking, custom dictionaries, and the suggestion ranking. Pure → `tst_spell`. | Hunspell, ICU (word boundaries), `core/reflow/ReflowDocument`, `core/doc/AnnotationStore` (comment spell check); **driven by** `ui/write/SpellCheckPane` |
