### 3.8 `core/batch/`, `core/settings/`, and shared headers

| File | Status | Role | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|
| `core/batch/ActionSequence.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S14) | An ordered list of parameterized actions, each either fixed or prompt-at-run-time; serialize/deserialize for sharing. | `core/cmd/ActionRegistry`, `core/batch/SequenceStore` |
| `core/batch/BatchRunner.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S14) | Runs a sequence over a file list, folder, tree, watched folder, or the open documents; continue-or-stop on error; dry-run mode; progress and cancellation. **Runs with no UI attached** — the proof that the action layer is real. | `core/cmd/CommandJournal`, `IDocumentBackend`, `core/batch/BatchReport`, `app/CommandLine` |
| `core/batch/BatchReport.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S14) | Per-file outcome, timing, and reason for skip or failure; renders as text, CSV, or JSON for the CLI. | `ui/dialogs/BatchEditorDialog`, `app/CommandLine` |
| `core/batch/SequenceStore.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S14) | Save, rename, duplicate, delete, import, and export sequences. | `core/settings/ProfileStore` |
| `core/batch/WatchedFolder.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S14) | Watches a folder, debounces file-settling, and enqueues a sequence run per arrival. | `core/batch/BatchRunner`, `QtCore` file watching |
| `core/settings/Settings.{h,cpp}` | ✅ | The **one** typed settings store. **`isSet()` arrived at T11**: whether the FILE carries a key, which is a different question from whether its value equals the default — the Quick Access Toolbar ships three commands and the user may remove all three, and only one of those two empty lists means "give them the factory toolbar". Groups mirror the Preferences pages of [`Features.md` §9.1](./Features.md#91-preferences-groups). Typed get/set with defaults, a `changed` subscription, reset. **Loads once into memory and writes only on an explicit `sync()`**, because D1-16's downgrade rule — a file a NEWER build wrote is read for what we understand and left byte-identical — is not expressible through a live `QSettings`, which syncs on destruction. An out-of-range value is clamped and logged; an unreadable one falls back to its default; a `shortcuts/` key naming an action that no longer resolves is dropped, counted and removed. Pure → `tst_settings`. **Changed 2026-08-31 (I-47): a change is flushed to disk when it is made**, not at teardown — `setVariant` and `clearShortcutOverride` are the two funnels every mutation passes through, and both now `sync()`, with `CompositionRoot`'s teardown call kept as a backstop. Before, a crash or a kill lost every preference the user had watched take effect, which the immediately-applying, no-OK-button Preferences dialog made worse rather than better. A write failure is logged rather than returned: `set()` is a void. **Changed 2026-09-14 (Stage 2 I-39): a RETIRED action spelling is rewritten at load.** A `shortcuts/<context>/<retired>` key, and a retired id inside `ui/qatCommands`, are brought to the current spelling in memory and written that way at the next sync (`migrateRetiredActionIds`). Resolving the old spelling had been the only half of D1-16's promise: every write builds its key from the enum, so a Preferences reset cleared a key that did not exist and the retired one brought the remap back on the next launch. When two keys land on one current path, the current spelling in the file wins, then the later alias row. The constructor takes the alias table as a parameter, defaulting to the shipped one, so a test can load a file through rows the shipped table does not have. **`general/` IS STORED AS `General/`** (Stage 4 guide **I-15**, 2026-09-26): QSettings writes the group `general` as `[%General]` and reads that back as `General/`, so until then no `general/` key was ever read back after a restart — the mapping is applied at every read from and write to the QSettings backing, and nowhere else. A test that claims a key survives a restart must reload from a COPY of the file: QSettings caches a file per process. | Read by nearly everything; written by `ui/dialogs/PreferencesDialog` |
| `core/settings/SettingsKeys.def`, `SettingsSchema.{h,cpp}` | ✅ | **One declarative table, three artifacts**, the same shape as `ActionId.def` (D1-6): the constexpr `Key<T>` objects, the runtime descriptor table a Preferences page iterates, and the group census all come from the `.def`. Type, default, range, group, and whether policy may lock it. **There is no string overload anywhere**, so an undeclared key is a name that does not exist — three negative-compile fixtures prove a misspelled key, a string key and a wrong-typed value each fail to compile (D1-10). The `shortcuts/<context>/<action>` family is generated over `KeyContext` × `ActionId` rather than opened as a string API. Enums persist **by name**, so a reordered enum cannot change what an existing file means. Carries `kCurrentSchemaVersion` and the ordered migration chain (D1-16). **Extended at T14 to a fourth artifact:** each descriptor now also carries the **editor kind** its type asks for (`SettingsEditorTraits`), the closed set of **choices** where the type has one, and a **catalog key derived from the path** (`prefs.setting.<group>.<key>`) — all three derived from the row's type and path rather than added as columns somebody fills in, so a Preferences page can be generated and a key with no label is mechanically detectable. | `core/settings/Settings`, `core/settings/PolicyOverlay` **THE SIXTEEN STAGE 2 ROWS LANDED AT S2 · T15** — eleven `pageDisplay/` and five `performance/` — and adding them was the whole of the Preferences work for them, which is convention 17 doing what it was built for. **Four of the sixteen took over a default that had been living in a C++ struct**, each of which had said in its own comment that the row would become the owner: `DpiPolicy::Limits::maxRenderDpi` (2400), `RenderCache::Config::memoryCapBytes` (256 MiB) and `diskCapBytes` (**0**, which is D2-14's decision rather than an unset field), and `RenderScheduler::Config::workerCount` (**1**, which is I-05's answer). `tests/unit/tst_stage2settings.cpp` asserts the row and the struct still agree, so the day one moves without the other is a failure rather than a surprise. **S3 · T12 adds D3-9's two `documents/` rows** — `recoveryDir` (empty: the default folder; only an absolute path counts) and `warnOnRepairedSave` (`true`; it skips a repaired document's explanation and never the forced Save-As) — asserted by `tests/unit/tst_stage3settings.cpp`. **S6 · T8:** `commenting/stickyTool` and `commenting/popupBehaviour` (D6-13), and the `commenting/default<Property>.<tool>` family recognised by `isDeclaredPath` (`core/settings/CommentingTypes`). |
| `core/settings/CommentingTypes.{h,cpp}`, `CommentingTools.def`, `ToolDefaults.def` | ✅ **(S6 · T8)** — **rows the file manifest did not name**, added with the files | **The Commenting vocabulary and the `commenting/default<Property>.<tool>` key FAMILY** (D6-13). `CommentingTools.def` is the 23 tools a person arms (`core::CommentingTool`, which `ui::AnnotationToolKind` IS since T8), each with the token its keys use; `ToolDefaults.def` one row per (tool, property) a tool has -- colour, fill, opacity, thickness, line style, blend, font size -- with its DEFAULT. A key exists only as a `ToolDefaultKey`: `of<Tool, Property>()` is a `static_assert` on `toolHasProperty` (constexpr over the table), so an undeclared pair DOES NOT COMPILE (`unit.settings.compile.undeclared_tool_default`, A-61); `fromPath` resolves a path a file holds and refuses one outside the family. One parse/format pair per property validates the file, the page and the defaults alike; `dashFor`/`lineStyleOf`, the blends and `blendNameFor` (a kind's own blend written as nothing) and `PopupBehaviour` live here. `core::Settings` gained `toolDefaults`, `setToolDefaults`, `resetToolDefaults`. Pure. → `tests/unit/tst_stage6settings`. |
| `core/settings/PolicyOverlay.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S15) | Machine-wide administrative defaults and locks layered over user settings, with "who locked this" reporting. | `core/settings/Settings`, `ui/dialogs/PreferencesDialog` |
| `core/settings/ProfileStore.{h,cpp}` | 🟡 **(S6 · T10: the custom-stamp library ✅; the other profile kinds below, S7–S14)** | Generic named-profile persistence reused by stamps, watermarks, headers/footers, security schemes, signature appearances, conversion profiles, and batch sequences. **What S6 · T10 built is the STAMP library** (D6-37(g)): one `<id>.json` and the image as given (`<id>.png`/`.jpg`) per custom stamp, and `favourites.json` for the built-in favourites, each written whole through `QSaveFile` -- so a damaged file is REPORTED by name and loses only itself (`damaged()`), and the rest loads (A-79); name, category (the reserved `:signatures` among them), favourite, rename, refile, remove, and a check that the bytes ARE a PNG or a JPEG (a PDF refused by name). Its root is `%APPDATA%\RawPage\stamps` (`defaultRoot()`), passed by `app/main.cpp` alone; one per process (`app::Services::stamps`). It writes files, `core/settings/`'s added privilege -- no waiver. The other kinds this row names have no caller before their stages. → `tests/unit/tst_profilestore`, `unit.profile_store.restart`. | `core/security/SecuritySchemeStore`, `core/batch/SequenceStore`, `ui/dialogs/*` |
| `core/settings/RecentFiles.{h,cpp}` | ✅ | Recent list with pinning and the clear action. A pinned entry is **not** subject to eviction and does not spend a `documents/recentCount` slot; `clear()` clears the history and keeps the pins, and `clearIncludingPins()` is the other one, called by name. Paths are canonicalized, so one document reached through `/`, `\` and `./` is one entry. **Keyed on the path, not a `DocumentId`** — that type arrives with `core/doc/` at Stage 2, and a recent list is a list of paths by nature; Stage 2 adds an overload, not a new key. Per-document view state is Stage 4's `SessionState`. | `core/doc/DocumentId` (S2 overload), `platform::IShellIntegration` (jump list) |
| `core/settings/ComparePairs.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(the user's request, 2026-10-04; S5 guide A-126)** — **a row the FileMap did not have**, added with the file | **The Compare dialog's memory**: for each older file, the newer file it was last compared against, in the internal key `documents/recentComparisons` (older, newer, older, newer, most recent first; 20 pairs). Two free functions over `Settings`, no state of their own, so two windows cannot disagree. A new pair for one older file replaces its old one. The match canonicalizes separators by hand, as `RecentFiles` does, and **ignores case** -- every path RawPage sees is a Windows path -- while the spelling noted is the one returned. It does not ask whether the file still exists; the dialog does. Not `documents/compare*`, which is the four comparison options. → `tests/unit/tst_comparepairs`. | `ui/shell/MainWindow` (notes an opened comparison), `ui/dialogs/CompareDialog` (asks) |
| `core/settings/SessionState.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S4 · T3) | The open-document set, tab order, window geometry per monitor arrangement, and pane layout, for session restore — plus the per-document view state nothing else has a home for: layout mode, the zoom INTENT and its multiplier, the scroll position, and **guides** (D4-7), which are view state and never reach `pdf/`. **Window geometry is keyed by a monitor-arrangement FINGERPRINT** (D4-8) — a hash over `IDisplayTopology::monitors()`, sorted by `id`, over id, position, pixel size, `scaleFactor` and `primary`, and deliberately **not** `friendlyName` (may be empty, not unique — I-20's lesson) or `physicalDpi` (a property of the glass, not of the arrangement). An unknown arrangement falls back to the most recent and **every window is then clamped onto a screen that exists**, which is what makes a wrong fingerprint cosmetic rather than a window nobody can reach. The clamp's first branch is *already reachable → untouched*, so a window straddling two monitors is left alone. **It stores no content, no dirty flag and no undo history** (D4-9): unsaved work belongs to the recovery journal, and two mechanisms restoring one edit would restore it twice. A file it cannot read — truncated, empty, a directory, or a format from a newer build — yields an empty session, one log line and a product that starts. **Its path is a constructor argument** so a test or a composition case cannot write the session of whoever ran the suite; `defaultFilePath()` (S4 · T13) resolves `%APPDATA%\RawPage\session.json` for the product alone. No `layer-lint` waiver: `core/settings/` already carries the filesystem-write privilege. **S4 · T13 adds `SessionDocument::splitState`**, the split as one opaque string for the reason `SessionWindow::paneLayout` is one — `SplitViewContainer::Mode` is a widget's enum and this layer never reads the value — and **settles I-01**: the identity is read at CAPTURE, where it is already post-save, so the key does the one job D4-7 chose it for. | `app/Application`, `ui/shell/MainWindow`, `platform::IDisplayTopology` |
| `core/backend.h` | ✅ (S2 · T1, T13, T14; S5 · T2 `ITextExtractor` implemented; S5 · T8 `searchUnits`; **S6 · T2 `annotations()`**, the store hydrated at open -- planned for T3 with the intents, needed at T2 by the reader's own cases; S5 · T9 the open in pieces; **S6 · T4 `showEdits`**); **S6 · T11 `attachments()`, `attachmentData()`, `extractAttachment()`, `openBytes()` and `MutationSink::insertAttachments`/`removeAttachments`** (D6-38(d)) -- members of the root and of the sink, so the implemented census is unchanged at 4 of 7; **S6 · T13 `readAnnotationExchange()`, `writeAnnotationExchange()` and `annotationWritable()`** (D6-40(e)) -- members of the root, the census unchanged | **Since S6 · T4 `showEdits(document, viewers)`** (D6-2): compose and verify the update Save would write and have the engine of the document -- and of each companion -- draw the newest revision on disk followed by it; refused, with every engine unchanged, as `error.edit.notShown`. **Since S5 · T9 `beginOpen`/`continueOpen`/`abandonOpen` and `OpenProgress`** -- the same open as `open()`, a piece at a time, each saying whether the NEXT needs the engine, for a folder search (D5-13 as amended at T3). **Since S5 · T8 (D5-12) `IDocumentBackend::searchUnits(handle, part, into)`** -- the one read-only reader of comments, form fields, bookmarks and attachments as plain search units, a part at a time and on the engine thread; a member of the root, not an eighth interface, so the census is unchanged. **The implemented census is 4 of 7 since S5 · T2** — `ITextExtractor` by `pdf/render/PdfiumTextExtractor`; `IIndexer` stays null, the catalog index being Stage 16's. **The engine contract.** Declares `IDocumentBackend`, `IPageRasterizer`, `ITextExtractor`, `MutationSink`, `ISigner`, `IOcrEngine`, `IIndexer` — **all seven, now** (D2-7); **from S3 · T4 `IDocumentBackend` also answers `permissions()` and `documentId()`**, because the assessment needs both readings and the identity needs the bytes, and both live in the backend, because a boundary added later is a boundary added under pressure by whoever needed one method. The narrowest and most important header in the project: it is what makes `core/` testable and the engine replaceable. **An unimplemented interface returns `notImplementedError()`** — an `Unsupported` `Result` naming the interface and the method — never a silent success and never an empty value, and `unimplementedBackendInterfaces()` makes the gap countable the way `platform::unimplementedInterfaces()` does. **What crosses this boundary is buffers and callbacks**: `IPageRasterizer` hands back a `RasterImage` (width, height, stride, format, bytes) and **not a `QImage`**, because `core/` may not name a QtGui type — `ui/` wraps the bytes at the far side. It carries **its own `layer-lint` entry**, for the reason `platform/platform.h` has one: four layers include it, so an `fpdf_` or QtGui include here would hand every one of them the engine through the one door that is open. **Since S6 · T12** `IDocumentBackend::pageGeometry` (D6-14): one page's stroked or filled paths in page space, on the engine thread, bounded at `core::PageGeometry::kMaxSegments` (100,000), past which the page is `tooManySegments` -- SUCCESS with no targets; `core/Snap.h`'s types, which this header now includes. `DocumentBackendImpl`, `CosBackend`, `FakeBackend` and `tst_backend`'s null backend implement it. | Implemented by `pdf/`; consumed by `core/*` and (read-only interfaces) by `ui/` **`pages()` MOVED ONTO `IDocumentBackend` AT T13** (issue I-08): T9 put it on the concrete `pdf::DocumentBackendImpl` with a comment saying `ui/` would reach it "through the backend it was handed", which was not achievable — `ui/` is handed this interface, may never name `pdf/`, and a `dynamic_cast` would be the violation the boundary exists to prevent. Opening a document and saying what is in it are the same responsibility, and `open()` already returns a handle that means nothing without it. **`repairReport()` followed at T14**, at its first consumer — `ui/shell/MessageBar` — rather than in advance, which is the rule that produced I-08 in the first place and the rule that closed it correctly the second time. **S4 · T4 adds `outline()`, `destinations()` and `metadata()`** on the same argument reaching its third subject: they are not capabilities a build may lack — which is what `BackendInterface` enumerates — but *what this document says about itself*, so the implemented-interface census is unchanged at **3 of 7**. Each returns null for a handle the backend did not open and an EMPTY model for a document that carries none, and a caller is expected to tell those apart. **S4 · T12 adds `contentBox(handle, pageIndex)`, and it is the ONE reader on this interface that is not hydrated at open** (D4-14): a content box is per PAGE, and computing every page's at open would walk every object of a 900-page document to answer a question about the one page being fitted — so it is computed on demand and cached, and asking twice calls the engine once. It answers a `Result` with **three distinguishable outcomes a caller must not collapse**: a rectangle; `NotFound` for a page with **no objects**, which is an ordinary empty page and is what `core/render/FitPolicy` falls back to the CropBox for; and `InvalidArgument` for a handle this backend did not open or an index outside the tree. This is also the change that gave the header `core/Geometry.h` — a `PdfRect` crosses the boundary now. |
| `core/Result.h` | ✅ (S0 · T10) | `Result<T>` / `Error` with a code, a message key for `tr()`, and context — so no layer signals failure by exception across a boundary. | Everything |
| `core/Log.h` | ✅ (S0 · T10) | Subsystem-tagged, level-filtered logging macros. Enforces the rule that document text and file paths never appear above debug level. It defines **one** sink and `setLogSink` REPLACES it, which is why nothing chains it: `app/CompositionRoot` installs a single sink that fans out to the console, `app/LogFile` and `core/LogBuffer`. | Everything; `core/LogBuffer`, `app/LogFile`, `ui/panes/LogPane` |
| `core/LogBuffer.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S1 T14) | The last N log records in memory, with a token-based subscription, so that `ui/panes/LogPane` has something to show. Bounded, thread-safe, and it **counts what fell off** rather than silently beginning mid-session. It exists as an owned object rather than as a chained sink because `applyLoggingSettings()` re-installs the sink on every `logging/` change — a pane's chain would be dropped at exactly the moment the user changed the log level. Notifies **on the appending thread**; a widget subscriber marshals. Pure → `tst_logbuffer`. | `core/Log.h`; owned by `app/CompositionRoot`, read by `ui/panes/LogPane` |
| `core/Snap.h`, `core/Snap.cpp` | ✅ **(S4 · T8)** **; S6 · T12 point targets** | The snap resolver (**D4-6**): page space in, page space out, with a fixed precedence — object edges, then guides, then grid — resolved **per axis**, and **ONE threshold expressed in DEVICE pixels** converted once against the view's `presentedScale()`. A threshold in page units grabs from a centimetre away at 50 % and needs a steady hand at 400 %; the tolerance is what the HAND does, and the hand works in screen pixels. The kind decides before the distance does, because the kinds state intent — an object edge is a thing in the document, a guide is a thing the person placed, a grid line is a thing the settings produced. **`objectEdges` is a `std::span` the caller fills and every product caller passes EMPTY until Stage 6**, so Stage 6 adds a source and a key, not an algorithm. **This row was lockstep debt** — the stage's §4 manifest named only `ui/viewer/GridGuideLayer`, while its §5.1 and §5.6 required the arithmetic to be testable with no display — and it is paid with the file. → `tst_snap`. **Since S6 · T12 (D6-14, D6-39(f)) POINT TARGETS come first**: `SnapPoint` -- a path's endpoint, a straight segment's midpoint, where two segments cross (not where they only meet end to end -- S6 guide I-19), and a point on a path -- resolved before the per-axis kinds and fixing both coordinates, the nearest winning and the kind on a tie, a point on a path only where none of the others is in reach: **object point, object edge, guide, grid**. `PageGeometry` holds one page's segments with a 128-cell grid index (a query costs the segments near the pointer) and the 100,000-segment bound; `snapPointsNear` offers the targets within the threshold. → `tst_snap` (`[A-89]`, `[A-91]`, `[I-19]`). | `core/Geometry.h`; used by `ui/viewer/GridGuideLayer` |
| `core/Measure.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S6 · T12)** — the plan's ⚠️ row, added with the file | **What a measurement measures, and how it is written down** (D6-14, D6-39(a)-(e)): nine units by their symbols, exact in points; the scale as ONE ratio and a unit, its words derived (`scaleString`: "1 in = 4 ft") and its `/Measure` (`measureDictionaryFor`); `calibratedRatio`; `polylineLength`, `selfIntersects` and `polygonArea` (a figure that crosses itself refused by name, `error.measure.selfIntersecting`); `measured` -- a measurement's reading from its geometry and its own first number formats, displayed only as `RL`, one `/X`, no `/Y`, one plain decimal format -- and `measureAt` for a tool's running reading; `newMeasurement` (a Line, a PolyLine or a Polygon with its `/IT`, arrows, caption, leaders and `/Measure`) and `withFreshReading`; WHERE its parts are drawn (`dimensionGeometry`, `measureLabel`, `captionAbove`), which the builder, the hit test and `fittedShape` read. Pure. → `tests/unit/tst_measure` (A-87, A-88, A-90's arithmetic, A-92). | `core/doc/Annotation`, `core/doc/TypedText`; **read by** `pdf/ops/AppearanceStreamBuilder`, `pdf/ops/MutationSinkImpl`, `core/doc/AnnotationGeometry`, `core/doc/DrawnShapes`, `ui/viewer/tools/MeasureTool` |
| `core/Geometry.h`, `core/Units.h` | ✅ (S0 · T10) | PDF-space vs device-space point/rect types that do not implicitly convert, and the unit conversions for rulers and measurement. Keeping these distinct is what prevents a whole class of coordinate bugs. **S4 · T8 gave `Unit` its first product callers** — the rulers and `units/rulerUnit` — and `unitFromSuffix`, so the five unit SUFFIXES are also the tokens the settings file holds: "mm" in the INI, "mm" on the ruler, "12mm" in a field, rather than a second table of enum names. | `core/render/*`, `ui/viewer/*`, `core/doc/Page` |

### 3.9 `pdf/cos/` — first-party PDF object layer (we own this outright)

| File | Status | Role | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|
| `pdf/cos/CosObject.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S2 · T6) | The object variant: null, bool, number, string, name, array, dict, stream, reference. **Identity-preserving round-trip is the property this file exists for, and it is carried by a SPAN**: every object parsed from a buffer records the exact byte range it was written as, so Stage 3's incremental save rewrites an untouched object by COPYING rather than by re-serializing — which is what keeps `(a+b)` from becoming `(a+b)`, `<</A 1>>` from being re-spaced and a signature applied at Stage 10 from being invalidated by a later unrelated save. The corollary is enforced rather than documented: a CONSTRUCTED object has no span, and so does one that has been changed — `append`, `insert` and `setStreamData` each clear it. A stream's payload is an offset and a length into the caller's buffer and is never copied, which is how A-21's *no unbounded allocation* clause is satisfied structurally: `/Length 4294967295` cannot reserve anything. Dictionary order is preserved and duplicate keys are KEPT, with lookup taking the last. Equality is semantic and ignores spans, because byte equality is the different question `raw()` asks. Guide case **A-16**. | All of `pdf/cos/`, `pdf/ops/` |
| `pdf/cos/Lexer.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S2 · T5) | Tokenizer over the byte stream: delimiters, literal and hex strings, numbers, names, comments. **Tolerant by design, and the design is three named guarantees rather than a disposition**: it never fails (there is no `Result` here — a malformed construct yields a token carrying a `LexNote` and lexing continues, because a reader that stops at the first bad byte cannot reconstruct anything and `XrefRepair` is what needs it to keep going); it always makes progress (every `next()` below the end advances at least one byte — the invariant that makes the fuzz target a bounded program rather than a hang detector, and the one only an explicit assertion finds); and it never reads out of bounds, including inside the one-byte-from-the-end escapes `#`, `\` and `<`. **Every token carries its raw byte span AND its decoded text**, which is not redundant: `(a\053b)` and `(a+b)` decode identically and are not the same bytes, and Stage 3 reproduces untouched objects verbatim. std-only, no Qt — `pdf/cos/` is portable by rule and the fuzz driver hands it a `const uint8_t*` that must not become a Qt container once per iteration. Guide case **A-15** (24 cases, two negative controls run). | `pdf/cos/Parser`, `tests/fuzz/` |
| `pdf/cos/Parser.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S2 · T6) | Objects, indirect objects, streams, and object streams. Inherits the lexer's three tolerance guarantees for `parseObject()`, which has no error channel at all, and adds a fourth that is this layer's own because it is the first that can recurse: **it cannot be made to overflow its stack**. `kMaxDepth` (128) bounds the descent and past it the construct is consumed ITERATIVELY, so `corpus/malformed/deep-nesting.pdf`'s 4,000 levels are refused by name rather than crashing *and* the parse resumes at the right place — verified as a negative control: raising the bound turns two cases into a SegFault. **The `/Length` recovery rule is: trust it only when it lands on `endstream`** — absent, non-integer, an unresolvable `n g R`, past the end of the buffer, or simply wrong all take a forward scan, and `CosStreamData` keeps BOTH the declared and the real length because T8's report has to say what it reconstructed. An indirect `/Length` resolves through a CALLBACK rather than an `XrefTable` member, because T7 is built on this file and a member would be a cycle. `core::Result` appears on `parseIndirectObject` and `parseObjectStream` and nowhere else. Object streams take the DECODED payload: Flate is T7's, and reaching for a decoder here would put a decompressor inside the grammar. **Since 2026-10-04 each packed object is read only up to the next larger offset its table names**, each distinct offset once, and a pair repeating a taken offset is dropped as `ObjectStreamOffsetBad`: unbounded, a damaged stream cost the object count times the payload, a `fuzz_cos_roundtrip` soak timeout (S5 guide I-23, A-125). Guide cases **A-16** and **A-17**, and the **primary fuzz target**. | `pdf/cos/Lexer`, `pdf/cos/XrefTable` |
| `pdf/cos/XrefTable.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S2 · T7) | **Since Stage 3 T13 it never places an object number above `kMaxObjectNumber`** (8,388,607, ISO 32000-1 Annex C, declared here and shared with `ObjectAllocator`): a classic subsection or `/Index` range past it is refused or not placed, each check made before `start + index`, and `mergeEntry` refuses one as a backstop; a stream row's field is accumulated unsigned (Stage 3 guide **I-13**, found by `fuzz_cos_roundtrip`'s soak). Classic cross-reference tables and cross-reference streams, the `/Prev` chain across incremental updates, and the trailer. **The stream form is not an exotic path**: every PDF 1.5-or-later file written by a modern producer uses it, and it is Flate-compressed every time — which is why `pdf/cos/filters/FlateFilter` is a dependency of *finding objects* rather than an image feature, and why zlib is what lets the reader open a modern document at all. Also reads the **hybrid** `/XRefStm`, whose absence does not fail loudly but silently loses every object in an object stream — and, since 2026-09-23, marks each entry the classic table won over that stream with `alsoInXrefStm`, which is what lets `IncrementalWriter` hoist the key without handing those numbers back (Stage 3 guide **I-17**). **NEWEST WINS** across the `/Prev` chain — reversing it reads a document as it was two saves ago with no error anywhere — and a repeated offset ends the walk (`circular-prev.pdf`, A-21's *no hang*). `fetch()` follows a type-2 entry into its object stream, caches the container, and **checks that the offset actually held the object it named** (`xref-offsets-short.pdf`), because returning the wrong object silently is the failure nobody notices until a page renders as a font descriptor. Guide case **A-20**. | `pdf/cos/Parser`, `pdf/cos/filters/*`, `pdf/cos/XrefRepair`, `pdf/cos/IncrementalWriter` |
| `pdf/cos/XrefRepair.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S2 · T8) | Full-file object scan when the xref is broken, rebuilding the table and emitting a `RepairReport`. This file is why damaged files open instead of failing, and **the Stage 2 exit criterion (A-21) lives here**. **It repairs as little as possible, and that is the design**: try the file's own structures and VERIFY them, scan only if that fails, keep every entry that still verifies, and rebuild the trailer only if there isn't one. The obvious *if the xref is broken, rebuild everything* throws away the good half of a file whose damage is confined to its tail — which is what most damaged files are — and makes the report useless, since every repaired document would read as *reconstructed the entire xref*. `openWithRepair()` is deliberately the **only** entry point, so opening a document is one code path whether or not it is damaged; two paths is how the repaired case ends up untested. It also **declines an encrypted file by name before repairing** (D2-6), with `Unsupported` rather than `ParseFailure` — *this file is fine and this build cannot read it* is a different message from *this file is damaged*. **Since Stage 3 T13 it never recovers an object numbered 0, nor one above `kMaxObjectNumber`** (I-13 — an object stream's pair table naming `LLONG_MAX` overflowed the rebuilt `/Size`) — from a header, from an object stream or from a failed table — because object 0 heads the free list and no section can place it, so a repaired table that did saved as a file needing repair again (Stage 3 guide **I-12**, found by `fuzz_cos_roundtrip`). Guide cases **A-21**, **A-22**, **A-23**. | `core/doc/RepairReport`, `pdf/cos/Parser`, `pdf/cos/XrefTable`, `pdf/cos/filters/*` |
| `pdf/cos/filters/*.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S2 · T7), **`Jbig2Filter` 🟡 declared-and-refusing (I-06)** | The stream-filter family, one file each: `FlateFilter`, `LZWFilter`, `Ascii85Filter`, `AsciiHexFilter`, `RunLengthFilter`, `DctFilter`, `JpxFilter`, `CcittFilter`, `Jbig2Filter`, `CryptFilter`. **Nine of these are Stage 2; `CryptFilter` is Stage 10**, with the `StandardSecurityHandler` whose key it needs — a crypt filter built without one cannot be tested (Stage 2 D2-4). `Filter.{h,cpp}` beside them is the registry, the `DecodeParms` and the PNG/TIFF predictor Flate and LZW share; it is **not** a tenth filter, and guide case A-19 asserts the filter LIST rather than counting files here. **"A pure `decode`/`encode` pair" is true of five of them and no more, which is a correction rather than a shortfall**: `encode`→`decode` returning the input exactly is not a property JPEG or JPEG 2000 *has*, and CCITT and JBIG2 are decode-only at a stage D2-4 makes a READER — so `isLossless(FilterKind)` carries the distinction and A-18 is asserted over it. **Three are first-party for measured reasons**: LZW's `EarlyChange` boundary was determined against `corpus/pdfbuild.py`'s encoder rather than derived (the decoder runs one table entry behind the encoder, so the two conditions look different while being one rule); CCITT is ours because libtiff's fax decoder is reachable only through a `TIFF*` handle, so fabricating a TIFF around every PDF image costs more than writing the decoder; and **JBIG2 has no permissible library at all** — `jbig2dec` is AGPL-3.0, which D4 forbids — so it is declared and **refuses by name** rather than returning a blank image (I-06). | zlib, libjpeg-turbo, OpenJPEG; used by `pdf/cos/XrefTable`, `pdf/cos/XrefRepair` and the writers |
| `pdf/cos/StandardSecurityHandler.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S10) | Password-based encryption: RC4 40/128, AESV2, AESV3; key derivation, permission bits, and the metadata/attachment coverage modes. Pure → `tst_crypt` against known vectors. | OpenSSL, `core/security/EncryptionPolicy` |
| `pdf/cos/PublicKeySecurityHandler.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S10) | Certificate-based encryption: per-recipient key envelopes and per-recipient permissions. | OpenSSL, `platform::ICertificateStore` |
| `pdf/cos/IncrementalWriter.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S3 · T2; **S6 · T6 `ObjectDelta::reserve`/`define`**) | **Since S6 · T6 a new object's number can be had before its value** (`reserve`, then `define` once): two new objects that name each other -- a note and its pop-up -- cannot both be added first (D6-33(a)). **Appends** changed objects, a new xref section, and a `/Prev`-chained trailer, leaving the original bytes untouched. The reason a signature can survive a later edit. **It returns the UPDATE, never the file**: it takes the original read-only and gives back the bytes to write after it, so the prefix property is structural and `pdf/cos/` stays std-only for the portable jobs (a deviation from `Implementation-Plan.md`'s `append(QIODevice&, …)`, recorded in its T2 row). D3-5's shape: exactly the delta's objects, each an ordinary object even if it lived in an `/ObjStm`; a section matching the newest one's kind (classic after classic or hybrid, a Flate xref stream after a stream, its number from `ObjectAllocator`); `/Size` never reduced; `/Root` and `/Info` carried; `/Prev`; `/ID[0]` kept and `/ID[1]` an MD5 of the update, so output is deterministic. `appendComplete()` is D3-6's forced-Save-As shape — every verified object, no `/Prev`. Refuses by catalog key, producing nothing: encrypted, no revision, no `/Root`, empty delta, unknown or duplicate object, stream without data, a table over another buffer. **Serializes from VALUES and ignores spans**, because a value inside a packed object has a span into a buffer that no longer exists (Stage 3 guide I-04). Also holds `serializeObject()`. **`protectOriginal()` (S3 · T13, Stage 3 guide I-12)** runs before every append and keeps an append from changing what the original bytes MEAN: an object whose parse runs to the end of the file is re-written into the update as it was read, and the delta's allocator is raised past the largest number any object references, so a dangling reference never names a new object. It walks every placed object once — found by `fuzz_cos_roundtrip`'s first run, on repaired files' complete sections. **After a hybrid file it also re-lists what hoisting `/XRefStm` would take back** (S3 · T13's successor fix, Stage 3 guide **I-17**, found by the nightly soak): naming the stream in the new trailer puts it in FRONT of the section at `/Prev`, so every number both define gets a row of its own — which is the difference between a hybrid file that can be saved twice and one that can be saved once. → `tst_incrementalwriter` (guide A-19 … A-25, A-27, A-28), `tst_roundtrip`'s I-12 cases. | `pdf/cos/XrefTable`, `pdf/cos/ObjectAllocator`, `pdf/cos/filters/FlateFilter`, `pdf/ops/MutationSinkImpl` (T3) |
| `pdf/cos/FullWriter.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S8) | Save-As: renumber, drop unreachable objects, compact, choose object-stream packing, and target a PDF version. | `pdf/cos/ObjectAllocator`, `pdf/ops/OptimizeOps` |
| `pdf/cos/Linearizer.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S8) | Fast-web-view output: first-page object ordering, hint streams. | `pdf/cos/FullWriter` |
| `pdf/cos/ObjectAllocator.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S3 · T2) | Object-number allocation for objects an update ADDS. **Never reuses a number** (D3-5): it starts at the larger of the newest `/Size` and one past the largest object present — because `/Size` is a claim `malformed/size-too-small.pdf` lies about — so free-list entries below that point are never touched (guide A-23). **`reserveThrough()` (S3 · T13)** raises that floor past a number the file references without defining, capped at ISO 32000-1's 8,388,607 (Stage 3 guide I-12). Maintaining a free list for writing is a full rewrite's concern, and `FullWriter` (S8) decides whether it shares this class. → `tst_objectallocator`. | `pdf/cos/IncrementalWriter`, `pdf/cos/XrefTable` |
| `pdf/cos/ContentStreamParser.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S10) — **re-tagged from (S5) on 2026-09-29** (`Stage5-TextSearch-Plan.md` D5-19) | Content-stream operators into a token stream with graphics-state tracking — the substrate for redaction and object-level editing. Pure → `tst_contentstream`. **Not the substrate for text extraction**, which is PDFium's (`PdfiumTextExtractor`, D5-2) — the row once said it was, and a Stage 5 build would have had no caller but a test. | `pdf/ops/RedactionOps` (its first caller), the Stage 13 object editor |
| `pdf/cos/ContentStreamBuilder.{h,cpp}` | 🟡 (**S6 · T3** graphics state, colour and paths, and the re-read; **S6 · T6 line cap and join**; **S6 · T7 the text operators** -- `BT`/`ET`, `Tf`, `Tm`, `Tj` over a hexadecimal string -- and `W`, and `check()` refusing `q`/`Q` inside a text object) | Emits well-formed content streams for new appearances, watermarks, header/footer text, and composed reflow pages. **At T3:** one method per operator, numbers spelled in ONE place (fixed, at most four decimals, never an exponent, never `-0`), and **`check()`** -- the re-read D6-6 requires of every generated stream: tokenized by `pdf/cos/Lexer`, every operator in ISO 32000-1 Annex A's table less the inline-image triple, `q`/`Q` balanced and at most 28 deep, `BT`/`ET` balanced and never nested, no token the lexer had to tolerate. Proves well-formed, never right. → `tst_annotationops`. | `pdf/ops/AppearanceStreamBuilder`, `core/reflow/ReflowToFixedComposer` |

### 3.10 `pdf/render/` — the PDFium facade (read and raster only)

PDFium is linked as a **DLL through its `FPDF_*` C API**, which is what makes it consumable from our
GCC build regardless of how PDFium itself was compiled ([`Tech-Stack-Plan.md` A6](./Tech-Stack-Plan.md#33-assumptions-that-are-not-yet-verified)).
Every file below therefore obeys one extra rule on top of the layering ones: **no C++ object, no
exception, no `FILE*`, and no heap-ownership transfer crosses the PDFium boundary** — buffers and
callbacks only. That was already the facade's design; it is now also an ABI requirement.

| File | Status | Role | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|
| `pdf/render/PdfiumInit.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S2 · T2; S5 · T3 the fair engine lock) | **Since S5 · T3 the engine lock is an `EngineMutex`: a first-come, first-served ticket lock that records its owner** — `std::mutex` let a thread that took it in pieces win it back ahead of the waiting render worker (S5 guide I-02), and `requireEngineLock()` counts any facade reader entered without it (I-01). Library init/teardown and **the one place PDFium's global state is touched** (D2-9). `ensure()` is idempotent and thread-safe; `initializationCount()` makes *entered once* a fact a test asserts rather than a property nobody measures. **Teardown is deliberately not automatic** — a static destructor runs during process exit in an order nothing controls, after workers may have been suspended mid-render, which is how a clean shutdown becomes a crash report from inside the engine; `shutdown()` exists for the one caller that wants it and the product lets the process end. It is a **mutex and a flag rather than `std::call_once`**, because call_once is a one-way latch: after a shutdown a second `ensure()` would silently do nothing and hand every caller a library that is not there. `version()` is the **build-time pin** `cmake/PdfiumAssert.cmake` verified — the `FPDF_` C API has no version call — and `--version` says so. The memory and font-hook configuration arrives with `FontResolverBridge` at T16. **Its only caller until T9 is `tests/render/tst_pdfiuminit.cpp`**, said out loud here for the reason Stage 1's I-46 taught. | PDFium; `tests/render/`; `PdfiumDocument` (S2 · T9) |
| `pdf/render/PdfiumDocument.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S2 · T9; S5 · T3 locking; S5 · T8 search units; S5 · T9 `Opening`; **S6 · T2 `annotationFacts()`** -- each page's `/Annots` entry by entry as the engine reads it, taken in the page walk while the page is loaded, at no measurable cost: `annots-900.pdf` opens in 102 ms as `pages-900.pdf` does; **S6 · T4 the reload**) | **Since S6 · T4 the RELOAD** (D6-2): `show(base, update)` queues a revision; the next engine call for the document swaps it in UNDER THE ENGINE LOCK -- between jobs, on the render thread -- through `FPDF_LoadCustomDocument` reading across the two buffers, the original shared with the backend rather than copied; a newer `show` coalesces; a load the engine refuses keeps the document it had; the page tree and the readers are not re-read; `reloadStats()`. **Since S5 · T9 the open is a staged `Opening`** -- the load with its password loop, the form environment, the page count, the page walk in pieces BOUNDED BY TIME (pages until 1 ms has passed, at least one; a count of eight measured up to 3.5 ms), the readers, and the first-party repair reading with no engine -- and `openBytes` runs it to its end, so a window's open and a folder search's are one. **Since S5 · T8 `searchUnits(part, descriptions, into)`** (D5-12): a page's MARKUP annotations' `/Contents` and `/Subj` and its widgets' qualified field names and values, each with its `/Rect`, under one lock for the page; the outline's titles from what open read; and each attachment's name, its description (the first-party reading, joined by position and confirmed by name) and -- for one that is a PDF -- its pages' text, opened from bytes by `openBytes` with no password callback and one level deep, the one long part. **Since S5 · T3 every engine call at open is under the engine lock** — the outline, destination and Info readers had none from S4 · T4 (S5 guide I-01) — **and the page walk takes it per page**, so another thread gets the engine between two pages. The walk loads every page, ~145 ms for 900: a folder search's open is T9's to split (D5-13 as amended). Opens a document (including password-protected), exposes the page count and page tree, and hydrates `core/doc/Document` from it — the last of those at Stage 3, since `Document` is Stage 3's. **The `FPDF_DOCUMENT` never leaves this object.** The file is read through `QFile` and handed to `FPDF_LoadMemDocument64` as BYTES rather than as a path: what encoding PDFium's `const char*` path is depends on the platform, and convention 1 exists because this machine's code page 950 turned an emoji filename into `report-?????.pdf`. The cost is recorded rather than absorbed — a 400 MB document occupies 400 MB, and `FPDF_LoadCustomDocument` is the upgrade path. **`FPDF_GetPageCount` returns `/Count` VERBATIM**, so `corpus/malformed/pages-count-wrong.pdf` opens through PDFium claiming 97 pages over three real kids; the trailing run of unloadable pages is dropped, because the tree is authoritative and a 97-page scrollbar on a 3-page file is what believing the number produces. It also runs the first-party COS reader over the same bytes for a `RepairReport`, since **PDFium repairs silently and reports nothing**. **Corrected 2026-09-13 (Stage 2 I-31, I-32):** a document whose page tree is EMPTY is refused with `error.open.noPages` rather than opened as a tab with nothing in it, and a refusal of bytes that carry a `%PDF-` header says `error.open.damaged` — PDFium reports the same `FPDF_ERR_FORMAT` for a damaged PDF and a spreadsheet, and calling the first *not a PDF* was false. **S4 · T4 adds three readers, all run ONCE at open** (D4-3, D4-4): the outline as a tree with each node's destination and the file's own open/closed request, named destinations from both PDF tables as one list with unresolved entries kept, and the Info dictionary plus the header's version. The outline walk is bounded by depth and node count — `/First` and `/Next` are references and a file may point them in a circle, which does not fail a reader, it hangs one. Reading at open rather than on demand is what keeps the `FPDF_DOCUMENT` inside this directory: a lazy reader would have to leave the handle reachable from `core/`. **S4 · T12 adds the CONTENT BOX, which is the exception to that and says why** (D4-14): the union of one page's object bounds through `FPDFPage_CountObjects` / `FPDFPage_GetObject` / `FPDFPageObj_GetBounds`, intersected with the CropBox, computed for the page asked about and cached. Per page is the reason it cannot be an open-time read; the handle still never leaves, because the call comes IN rather than the handle going out. An object whose bounds the engine declines to report is SKIPPED and not treated as a zero rectangle at the origin, which one such object would otherwise drag the whole box down to. The cache has **its own mutex, not the engine lock**: the engine lock serializes PDFium, and two threads asking about two different pages would still race on the map — and the engine is called with that mutex released, so the map never waits behind whatever the render thread is doing. `std::nullopt` in the map means *asked, and the page is empty*, so a second ask for an empty page walks nothing. → `tst_contentbox`. **Since S6 · T12** `pageGeometry` (D6-39(g)): `FPDFPath_*` over every path object that strokes or fills, its `FPDFPageObj_GetMatrix` composed with its form's, `FPDFFormObj_*` eight deep, a Bezier's three segments flattened into eight `curve` pieces and its two ends kept as anchors; the whole page under the engine lock; past the bound, `tooManySegments` and one log line. | PDFium, `core/doc/PageTree`, `core/doc/{Outline,Destinations,Metadata}`, `pdf/cos/XrefRepair`, `pdf/DocumentBackendImpl` |
| `pdf/render/PdfiumRasterizer.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S2 · T9; **S6 · T4 a reloaded `/Rotate`**) | **Since S6 · T4** it reads each loaded page's `/Rotate` and corrects for its difference from the tree a request's unsaved-rotation delta is relative to -- after a reload the engine holds the composed rotation. Implements `IPageRasterizer`: renders one `RenderRequest` into a bitmap at the requested device resolution, honouring the render flags. ⚠️ **NOT thread-confined per worker — that half of D2-9 was measured FALSE at T3 (I-05)**; every entry into the engine is serialized on `PdfiumInit::engineLock()`, held across `LoadPage`, the bitmap creation, the render AND the destroy, because those race on PDFium's allocator too. **The tile arithmetic is the part most likely to be wrong**: `FPDF_RenderPageBitmap` has no region parameter, so a tile is rendered by passing the WHOLE scaled page's size with a NEGATIVE origin — passing the tile's size instead puts a thumbnail of the entire page into every tile. The bitmap is built over OUR buffer via `FPDFBitmap_CreateEx`, so the pixels are never copied out, and the result is a `core::RasterImage` with an explicit stride rather than a `QImage` (I-03). | PDFium, `core/render/RenderRequest`, `pdf/render/PdfiumInit` |
| `pdf/render/PdfiumTextExtractor.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S5 · T2; T4 ruling lines)** | **Since T4 it also records every thin top-level path object as a rule** (at most 10,000), for the analyzer's ruled tables. Implements `ITextExtractor` over PDFium's text page, **inside the engine lock**: per character the Unicode, loose box, origin, angle, size, and the generated/hyphen/unmap-error flags, one font lookup per text object; surrogate pairs joined. **It corrects two measured engine habits** — a line-end hyphen arrives as U+0002 and is stored as U+002D; a base-14 weight of 0 is stored as −1 — and **never calls `FPDFText_GetText` or `FPDFText_Find*`** (D5-2). One per document, knowing its handle: another is refused with `InvalidArgument`, and on any failure the model is untouched → `tst_extract`. | PDFium, `core/text/PageTextModel` |
| `pdf/render/PdfiumFormFill.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S9) | PDFium's form-fill environment for rendering live field appearances and widget states. **The APPEARANCE-STREAM half is already done and does not live here** (S2, issue I-21): `PdfiumDocument` creates a read-only `FPDFDOC_InitFormFillEnvironment` handle and `PdfiumRasterizer` calls `FPDF_FFLDraw`, because PDFium will not draw a `/Subtype /Widget` annotation from `FPDF_RenderPageBitmap` however the flags are set and a form would otherwise render as an empty page. What is left for this file is the INTERACTION this row names — focus, typing, validation, the calculation cascade — and it inherits the handle rather than making a second one. | PDFium, `core/doc/FormModel`, `ui/viewer/FormOverlay` |
| `pdf/render/FontResolverBridge.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S2 · T16) | The `FPDF_SYSFONTINFO` hook, **version 2** so PDFium skips its own font enumeration and asks per request. Translates the engine's ask into a `platform::IFontResolver::Request` — the charset becomes a BCP-47 hint, which is what decides Han disambiguation — and hands back the face's BYTES through `GlyphCache`. **Static, because `FPDF_SetSystemFontInfo` writes PROCESS-GLOBAL state**: one engine, one hook, and an instance per document would be a dangling callback the moment a tab closed. Every mapping is recorded with one of **four outcomes**, not a boolean: an empty `chosen` means *declined* or *nothing installed* and a support reader must be able to tell those apart. **It DECLINES the base 14 (issue I-13)** — PDFium asks about standard faces too, and the first run of its control case caught the product substituting Segoe UI for Helvetica on every page of every document. `app/CompositionRoot` installs it after the platform bundle and uninstalls it **after the scheduler is joined and before the bundle dies**; either half alone is a crash. | PDFium, `platform::IFontResolver`, `core/render/FontSubstitution`, `pdf/render/GlyphCache` |
| `pdf/render/GlyphCache.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S2 · T16) | **NOT a cache of rasterized glyphs, and its header says so before its name misleads anybody.** PDFium rasterizes and caches glyphs, in the process-global state D2-9 confines to `PdfiumInit`; nothing in `pdf/render/` ever sees a glyph bitmap, so a class here that claimed to count them would be measuring a number it invented. What this owns is the step before: the face's FILE BYTES, read once and served to every later ask — across tiles, across pages, across documents. LRU with a **byte** cap rather than a face count, because CONSOLA.TTF is 100 KB and a CJK collection is 18 MB. This is the correction to guide case **A-67**, recorded the way T7 corrected A-18 and T15 corrected A-65. | `pdf/render/FontResolverBridge` |

### 3.11 `pdf/ops/` — mutation realization (the engine side of every command)

| File | Status | Role | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|
| `pdf/ops/MutationSinkImpl.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S3 · T3; **S6 · T3 the annotation listings; S6 · T5 the edits**) **S6 · T11 the name tree** (D6-38(a), (c)): the tree as it should be, written flat into the names dictionary or the catalog, a removed pair put back where it was, an attached file compressed once and verified byte for byte (`reconcileFiles`); a created `FileAttachment`'s `/FS`; `attachmentData` | Implements `MutationSink`: receives **intent** from a command and turns it into COS object edits queued for the next save. The single funnel every mutation passes through on the engine side. **Desired state, not a log** (D3-5): it keeps each page's target rotation and `delta()` diffs it against the **newest revision on disk**, so an undo before a save writes nothing (A-31) and, after `adopt()` moves the comparison onto the revision just saved, an undo writes the page back (A-32). Normalizes −90 → 270 and 450 → 90 and refuses anything else, and any page index outside the document, without changing state (A-33). Owns its `XrefTable`, not the bytes; opens through `openWithRepair()`; declines an encrypted file by name. → `tst_mutationsink`. **Since S6 · T5 (D6-5) the third intent, `replaceAnnotations`**: per annotation the file holds, the model as on disk and as it should be, only where they differ; `delta()` rewrites it from its dictionary on disk (`AnnotationOps::editedDictionary`), redrawing `/AP` only where what it draws changed -- a move or resize carries the producer's appearance (`core::isMappedCopy`), and a change this build cannot draw is refused when asked for; a DIRECT dictionary first edited becomes an object its page lists (D6-4); **an undo returns a dictionary the file held, verbatim** (guide I-08); `verify` reads every edit back -- subtype, rectangle, a redrawn `/AP`. → `tst_annotedit`. **Since S6 · T3 (D6-1, D6-4, D6-5) the annotation intents**, `insertAnnotations` and `removeAnnotations`, all or nothing over a set of placements: it keeps each page's DESIRED `/Annots` listing -- references the file holds, direct entries as written, annotations RawPage created -- only where it differs from the disk, so add-then-undo writes nothing (A-25). `delta()` writes each such page's listing into its own indirect array object when no other page lists it, else into the page dictionary (written once, with any rotation), the key REMOVED when nothing is left; each created annotation's dictionary and `/AP` stream, numbered past every number the file references (`IncrementalWriter::largestReference`); and nothing else -- a deleted annotation's object stays, unlisted, and an undone delete lists that object again. A removed direct entry is kept by its open-time name; `adopt()` finds where a save put each created annotation by `/NM`, and `reconcile()` -- shared with `RevisionWriter::verify` -- holds every listing written to the one composed. → `tst_annotationops`. | `core/backend.h`, all of `pdf/ops/*`, `pdf/cos/IncrementalWriter` |
| `pdf/ops/RevisionWriter.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S3 · T8) | **A row the file map did not have**, added with the file. The backend's half of Save, portable in `rawpage_ops`: **compose** the newest revision on disk copied verbatim plus the sink's update (D3-6's complete section for a repaired document; the original bytes alone when there is nothing to write), **write** it to the temporary path, **read it back from that path** and **verify** — the prefix identical, the file opening through its own table with nothing that requires Save-As, the page count, and every page's rotation as the sink says — removing the file on any failure; and **adopt** the file now at the target, returning its bytes and repair report. Both `pdf/DocumentBackendImpl` and the headless `tests/unit/CosBackend.h` call it, so the round trip runs under `portable-asan`. `setComposeFaultForTesting` is a named seam for A-54, and for the fuzz body's three controls (S3 · T13). **Since T13 `compose` calls `IncrementalWriter::protectOriginal` whenever anything will be appended** (Stage 3 guide I-12). **Since S6 · T3 `verify` also holds every page's `/Annots` to the sink's desired listing** (`MutationSinkImpl::reconcile`, strict): a created annotation a dictionary with its `/NM`, its `/Subtype`, its page as `/P` and an unfiltered `/AP /N` that passes `ContentStreamBuilder::check`; every page nobody changed unchanged (Stage 6 guide A-30). | `pdf/ops/MutationSinkImpl`, `pdf/cos/IncrementalWriter`, `pdf/cos/XrefRepair`, `pdf/DocumentBackendImpl` |
| `pdf/ops/PageOps.{h,cpp}` | 🟡 (**S3 rotate ✅** · T3, S7 rest) | Page-tree object edits: rotation, insert, delete, move, duplicate, extract, box changes, scaling. **At Stage 3, the page-object walk and rotation only.** The walk is written to agree with PDFium — `/Count` never read, a `/Kids` array marks an intermediate node, a revisited node skipped and noted, iterative with `kMaxDepth` 256, inherited `/Rotate` and `/MediaBox` passed DOWN the path taken — and `tst_pageopsengine` holds it to the engine over 55 corpus files and 1,016 pages, with one measured disagreement (`pages-loop.pdf`, Stage 3 guide I-07). `rotatedPage()` writes the page's OWN dictionary, never an ancestor's, and drops a `/Rotate` equal to what the page would inherit (D3-5 as refined at T3). `/Rotate` normalized as PDFium does: truncated to quarter turns. **A page that is a loose stream stays a stream, its data copied** (S3 · T13, Stage 3 guide I-12): the walk calls any dictionary without `/Kids` a page, so a damaged file can make an object stream's container a page, and rewriting it as a dictionary dropped every object packed inside. → `tst_pageops`, `tst_pageopsengine`, `tst_roundtrip`'s I-12 cases. | `pdf/cos/*`, `MutationSinkImpl` |
| `pdf/ops/EmbeddedFileReader.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S5 · T8)** — **a row the file manifest did not name**, added with the file **S6 · T11 (D6-17): the model's reading** -- `entries` (the tree's pairs as written, for the sink's rewrite), `attachments` and `fileOf` (what a file specification says: name, `/Desc`, MIME, `/Params`), and `dataOf` (the bytes through the stream's filters, refused by key when they disagree with `/Params /Size`) | **The second read in the product that does not go through PDFium**, after `TransitionReader`, and for D4-3's same *unless PDFium cannot*: each embedded file's `/Desc`, which the pinned engine's attachment API never reads (its `FPDFAttachment_GetStringValue` reads the embedded file's PARAMS dictionary). Walks the catalog's `/Names /EmbeddedFiles` name tree -- bounded in depth and nodes, because `/Kids` can loop -- off the first-party walk `DocumentBackendImpl::open` already runs, so it costs no second parse and one lookup for a document with none; names are read only to MATCH the engine's list by position. `decodeTextString()` decodes a PDF text string: UTF-16BE or UTF-8 behind its mark, else PDFDocEncoding, which is not Latin-1 in 0x18-0x1F and 0x80-0xA0. An encrypted document is not read first-party (D2-6), so its descriptions are not searched. | `pdf/cos/XrefTable`, `pdf/DocumentBackendImpl`, `pdf/render/PdfiumDocument` (`searchUnits`) |
| `pdf/ops/AttachmentFiles.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S6 · T11)** — **a row the file manifest did not name**, added with the file | **The one place an attachment's bytes are written to disk** (D6-17, D6-38(d)): `write` puts them at a path through `QSaveFile` -- its folder made, a file there refused unless asked to replace, nothing left at the path on any failure -- for an extract and for the session folder an open writes into; `pdf/` may write, so neither needs a waiver and the census stays fifteen. `read` is the backend's reading for a document it holds NO sink for, over a table loaded for the call. Shared by `pdf/DocumentBackendImpl` and `tests/unit/CosBackend.h`, so the product and the headless suite write an extract the same way. Portable, in `rawpage_ops`. | `pdf/DocumentBackendImpl`, `pdf/ops/EmbeddedFileReader` |
| `pdf/ops/FdfCodec.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S6 · T13)** — the plan's ⚠️ row, added with the file | **FDF read and written, and the one door an exchange FILE goes through** (D6-20, D6-40(d), (e)): an FDF is PDF syntax with no cross-reference table, so it is opened with `pdf/cos/XrefRepair::openWithRepair` and each annotation read by `AnnotationReader::parseDictionary` -- the first-party reader's own code -- `/Page` an index and `/IRT`, `/Popup`, `/Parent` objects of the FDF turned into names -- and an `/IRT` that is a STRING, the parent's `/NM` as Acrobat 26.2 writes it (S6 guide I-28), taken as a name -- a file attachment's bytes from its `/EF`; written from `AnnotationOps::exchangeDictionary`, a file attachment's file an `/EmbeddedFile` stream, no table. `readFile` (bounded, either syntax told by its first bytes -- XFDF is `core/doc/XfdfCodec`'s), `writeFile` (through `AttachmentFiles::write`, so no waiver) and `carryFiles` (an export's file attachments given their bytes from the document). → `tests/unit/tst_fdf`, `fuzz_annot_pipeline`. | `pdf/cos/XrefRepair`, `pdf/ops/AnnotationReader`, `pdf/ops/AnnotationOps`, `pdf/ops/AttachmentFiles`, `core/doc/XfdfCodec`; used by `pdf/DocumentBackendImpl`, `tests/unit/CosBackend.h` |
| `pdf/ops/AnnotationReader.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S6 · T2)** — **a row the file manifest marked ⚠️**, added with the file | **The third first-party read**, and the first that exists not because PDFium CANNOT read the subject but because editing needs what its API does not give: each annotation's OBJECT NUMBER and its dictionary as written (D6-3, D6-4). Off the walk `pdf/DocumentBackendImpl` already runs, so no second parse: every page's `/Annots` -- resolved through references, an entry that is not a dictionary kept as a listing entry with no annotation, `/Rect` read **exactly as PDFium reads it** (four entries or the empty rectangle, measured entry by entry against the engine over ten awkward forms, guide A-18) -- into `AnnotationStore::Reading`. **Tolerant and bounded**: nothing fails, every oddity is a reported `AnnotationNote`, no annotation keeps more than 200,000 coordinates, and an annotation reached only through `/IRT`, `/Popup` or `/Parent` and listed by no page is reported and ignored. Costs **27 ms on `annots-900.pdf`** (9,000 annotations) and 0.24 ms on a 900-page file with none (release, medians of 7, T2). **Since S6 · T12** it reads `/LL`, `/LLE`, `/LLO`, `/Cap`, `/CP`, `/CO` and `/Measure` -- each value out of range read as ISO's default, chains of at most sixteen formats, a unit past 64 characters or a separator past 8 cut there and marked not plain, any finite factor held: exactly what a command accepts (S6 guide I-18's lesson). **S6 · T13:** `parseDictionary` -- one dictionary read as `read` reads each, for an FDF's annotations, which are the same dictionaries listed by `/FDF /Annots` rather than by a page. | `pdf/cos/XrefTable`, `pdf/ops/PageOps` (the walk), `pdf/ops/EmbeddedFileReader` (text strings), `core/doc/AnnotationStore`; **called by** `pdf/DocumentBackendImpl::assemble`, `tests/unit/CosBackend.h`, `tests/fuzz/AnnotPipelineFuzzBody.h` |

| `pdf/ops/AppearanceStreamBuilder.{h,cpp}` | 🟡 (**S6 · T3 Highlight**; **S6 · T6 underline, strikeout, squiggly, caret, note**; **S6 · T7 free text -- a box, a callout, a typewriter**; **S6 · T8 line, square, circle, polygon (and cloud), polyline, ink, every line ending, dashes and blends**; **S6 · T10 stamps -- standard, dynamic and image, any turn**; **S6 · T11 the file attachment's icons**; **S6 · T12 measurements' leaders and readings**; **S6 · T14 a redaction mark's outline and its `/RO` overlay, and a sound's speaker and microphone -- every kind Stage 6 creates**; widgets S9) | Generates `/AP` normal, rollover, and down appearance streams for every annotation and widget kind — so annotations look identical in other viewers. **At T3:** a highlight as a form XObject -- a STREAM, whose `/BBox` is the annotation's `/Rect` -- each quadrilateral filled in `/C` (yellow by default) through an ExtGState with `/BM /Multiply` and the opacity, its corners drawn around their centre whatever order the file wrote them in; `supports()` answers which subtypes it draws, and the sink asks it at intent time; every stream passes `ContentStreamBuilder::check` before it is returned. Its oracles are not itself (D6-6): golden rasters and PDFium's own generation at T8, `qpdf` in CI (A-31), Acrobat and Firefox (M-02). **At T6:** the three text lines in each quadrilateral's OWN frame (bottom edge read from the corner order -- Acrobat's edges run the same way, ISO's opposite -- so a vertical line is underlined along its side); the underline PDFium's, the strikeout at 42 % of the box and the squiggle between 7 % and 16 % -- where A-50 measured the glyphs, not where PDFium draws them (D6-32, guide I-09); a caret Acrobat's notched wedge; a note the `Comment` bubble or the `Note` page, any other `/Name` refused by key; `drawsNothing()` for a `Popup`, written with no `/AP`. **At T7:** free text set by `core::layoutTypedText` in the frame `core::freeTextFrame` gives -- `/IC` filled, an outline in `/C`, a callout's leader with an `OpenArrow` or `ClosedArrow`, the text in `/DA`'s size and colour, each line placed with its own `Tm`, clipped -- its fonts NOT embedded and written BESIDE the form through `AppearanceObjects`, keyed so a compose writes each once (`fontObject`: Helvetica with its `/Widths`; a CJK `Type0` over a `CIDFontType0`, `/DW 1000`, an indirect descriptor); a character no face can show, a `/DA` face it does not draw and a callout ending it does not draw refused by key. → `tst_annotfreetext`, `tests/render/tst_typedtext`. **Since S6 · T12** (D6-39(d)) a measurement draws its reading: a Line its dimension line moved by `/LL`, its leaders, its endings at the drawn ends and its CAPTION -- the reading, else `/Contents`' first line -- in Helvetica 9, on top or in a gap; a polyline's reading over its longest segment, a polygon's on its centroid. A measure this build does not display is refused by key, a polygon that crosses itself by name. `MutationSinkImpl`'s `drawsUnmodelled` narrowed to what the model still cannot draw. Two appearance baselines, `measure-distance` and `measure-perimeter-area`. | `pdf/cos/ContentStreamBuilder`, `core/doc/Annotation`, `core/doc/TypedText` **At T8** (D6-15, D6-35): a Line with `/LE`'s two endings, a Square and a Circle inset by half their stroke inside the rectangle less `/RD` (a cloudy Square's bumps round that box), a Polygon -- cloudy by `/BE` -- and a PolyLine with its endings, Ink as the centripetal curve through its samples; `/BS /S /D` dashed (`ContentStreamBuilder::dash`), endings drawn solid and filled with `/IC`, ALL TEN endings for a callout too; `/BM` in the graphics state where the annotation names a blend (a highlight's own `Multiply` otherwise); a measurement refused by key until T12. The `Comment` note redrawn on PDFium's proportions after A-65 measured T6's at IoU 0.877 (guide I-12). Its geometry is `core/doc/DrawnShapes`'. → `tests/unit/tst_annotdrawing` (A-66), `tests/render/tst_appearance` (A-65), the golden suite's appearance cases (A-64). |

| `pdf/DocumentBackendImpl.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S2 · T9 · extended S3 · T4, T8; S5 · T2 the extractor; **S6 · T2 the annotation store and its per-page agreement, assessed and logged at open**; S5 · T9 the open in pieces; **S6 · T4 `showEdits`**); **S6 · T11 the attachments** -- read off the first-party walk, their bytes through the sink or a table loaded for the call, the extract through `pdf/ops/AttachmentFiles`, and `openBytes`: an attached PDF, never editable (`error.edit.attachmentCopy`), its sink kept so a Save As writes it unchanged | **Since S6 · T4 `showEdits`** -- `RevisionWriter::compose` and `verify`, Save's own, the whole composition transient and only the update kept, then each engine's `show` -- and `reloadRecord()` (reloads, compose and verify times, the thread, the buffer identity) for the cases and the bench. **Since S5 · T9 `open()` IS the open in pieces run in one call** (D5-13 clarified): `beginOpen` records the path, and each `continueOpen` is one piece -- READ the file (no engine), the engine's own pieces (`PdfiumDocument::Opening`), and ASSEMBLE (no engine): the identity, the first-party walk with its sink, transitions and attachment descriptions, the permissions and the handle; a refusal drops the opening, and `abandonOpen` drops one between pieces. **Since S5 · T2 it hands out a `PdfiumTextExtractor` per document**, made once the handle exists, under the same map lock as the rasterizer. Implements `IDocumentBackend`: open, close, hydrate the model, hand out the rasterizer / extractor / sink / signer. **From Stage 3 T8 it saves**: `writeRevision` and `adoptRevision` through `pdf/ops/RevisionWriter`, D3-6's complete section chosen for a document whose report `requiresSaveAs()`; after an adopt it holds the saved file's bytes, repair report and identity, while the ENGINE goes on drawing what `showEdits` last handed it (S6 T4, D6-2) and its page tree stays the one read at open (D3-15). The map's lock is held only to find the entry, so a long save never stalls the render thread's `rasterizer()`. **After Stage 2 the implemented set is `{IDocumentBackend, IPageRasterizer}`; from Stage 3 T4 it adds `MutationSink`** — a `pdf/ops/MutationSinkImpl` built over the engine's own bytes at `open()`, handed out only for a document `Permissions::assess()` allows, which `open()` also runs and stores beside the `DocumentId` it takes — and the other four accessors are **null rather than empty stubs** (convention 6). `DocumentHandle` is an opaque `std::uint64_t` and **handles are never reused**, so a token held across a close MISSES rather than silently naming a different document. **`pdf::createBackend()` moved from `rawpage_cos` to `rawpage_pdfium` in this task**: a backend that opens documents is the engine, and `rawpage_assert_headless()` forbids the headless suite from linking it by name (D2-2). The cost is stated rather than absorbed — the shipped backend's census is no longer asserted under ASan; `core/backend.h` itself still is, over a test backend defined in the headless suite. | `pdf/render/*`, `pdf/ops/*`, `core/backend.h` |
| `pdf/BackendFactory.h`, `pdf/render/BackendFactory.cpp` | ✅ (S2 · T1; **the .cpp MOVED to `pdf/render/` at T9**) | `pdf::createBackend()` — the one exported symbol `app/` calls. Nothing else in the program names a `pdf/` type. **Returned a backend that implemented none of the seven and said so** until T9: seven falses from `implementedInterfaces()`, `notImplementedError` from every method, null from every sub-interface accessor, and a census logged at construction (`backend: assembled -- 0 of 7 interfaces implemented`). That is the honest form of *declared, not built yet*, and it is deliberately not a null pointer — a `no backend` branch in `app/` that no later build exercises is a code path that rots. **T9 replaces what it returns**, not the factory. It lives in `rawpage_cos` (§5.1 of the stage plan), which is why it names no PDFium type and compiles on the portable presets. | `app/CompositionRoot` **T9 replaced that with `pdf/DocumentBackendImpl` (2 of 7) and MOVED the definition into `rawpage_pdfium`.** Both are the same fact: a backend that opens documents IS the engine, and `tests/unit` links `rawpage_cos` under `rawpage_assert_headless()`, which forbids `rawpage_pdfium` by name (D2-2). The HEADER stays in `pdf/` because that is what `app/CompositionRoot` includes and it names only `core/` types. On the portable presets there is now no `createBackend()` at all, which is correct: `app/` is not built there. The factory still logs its own interface census — it briefly stopped when the null backend was deleted, and that was caught by a smoke run rather than by a test. |

### 3.12 `platform/` — the OS abstraction

| File | Status | Role | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|
| `platform/platform.h` | ✅ (S1 · T7 · extended S3 · T7) | **Interfaces only**: the **eighteen** — seventeen until Stage 3 T7 added `IDurableFiles` (D3-4) — of [`Tech-Stack-Plan.md` §4.3](./Tech-Stack-Plan.md#43-what-qt-gives-us-for-free-versus-what-needs-platform-code), plus the `Platform` bundle struct, the name census, and `createPlatform()`. No implementation and **no Win32 header — enforced, not asked for**: `tools/layer-lint/rules.json` declares this ONE FILE as its own layer with an OS-header deny list, with a fixture proving the rule fires and a control proving `platform/win/` may still include the OS freely. An unimplemented interface is a **null member**, never a stub that silently succeeds, and `unimplementedInterfaces()` names them so "not built yet" is printable rather than discovered by dereferencing. Every handle crosses as an opaque integer. | Implemented by `platform/win/*`; consumed by `core/`, `ui/`, `app/` |
| `platform/win/WinSingleInstanceBus.{h,cpp}` | ✅ | Named-object instance detection, file-list handoff, and raise-to-foreground (D1-11). A `Local\` mutex decides who is primary; a length-prefixed UTF-8 message over a named pipe carries the file list; the **secondary** calls `AllowSetForegroundWindow` with the primary's pid — read from the pipe rather than from the payload — before it exits, because Windows refuses foreground activation to a process that received no input. **The pipe name carries the session id explicitly**: `Local\` does not apply to pipes, so without it two users' correctly-separated mutexes would still meet on one machine-wide pipe. The handler is delivered by queued invocation onto the thread that called `listen()`. | `app/SingleInstance` |
| `platform/win/WinDisplayTopology.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S2 · T12) | Monitor enumeration with per-monitor DPI, and change notification on topology or scale changes. **The effective DPI and the RAW DPI are reported separately** — the first over 96 is the OS scale factor, the second is the dots per inch of glass, and they are not derivable from each other: the P-1 rig's 4K panel measures **163 DPI at 150 %** where the nominal answer is 144. `processIsPerMonitorAware()` says whether this process is being told the truth at all, because a DPI-unaware caller is handed virtualized figures that are self-consistent and wrong. `changed` fires on a real DIFFERENCE rather than per message: one scale change in the Settings app produces several notifications, and `WM_SETTINGCHANGE` arrives for things that are not displays. | `ui/viewer/PageLayoutEngine`, `core/render/DpiPolicy`, `core/settings/SessionState` |
| `platform/win/WinAcceleratedSurface.{h,cpp}` | ○ **(NOT BUILT — answered at Stage 4 T2, 2026-09-19)** | Accelerated presentation of rendered tiles, with a software fallback when no device is available. **This row deferred to *"Stage 4, if A2 says it is needed"* from Stage 2 T16 until T2 measured it, and it now says what was measured rather than what would decide it.** A2 at Stage 4 (`Tech-Stack-Plan.md` §3.3) is `go, with named custom work`: under the workload this stage creates — a split view plus a thumbnails pane streaming at `Speculative`, one scheduler, one worker, one cache — the viewport's p95 is **10.59–10.74 ms against a 16.667 ms budget**, and the pane costs **+0.62–0.74 ms** of it. **The reason it is not built is structural, not just that the number fits:** an accelerated surface PRESENTS finished pixels and does not rasterize them, while every millisecond measured is PDFium rasterizing behind I-05's engine lock — re-measured the same day, 1/2/4/8 workers give p95 13.199/14.078/14.093/13.808 ms, so more threads buy nothing and a GPU path would move the part that is not the bottleneck. The named work is **T11's** (an unfocused window enqueues at `Prefetch`), not a device. The member stays null, which is the vocabulary `platform.h` defines for *not built* (convention 6), and the interface census asserts it. | `ui/viewer/TilePresenter` **CONFIRMED AT T14, 2026-09-23**, which re-read the same quantity through the shipped panes and found the verdict a plain `go`: the viewport's p95 under D4-16's workload is **7.03–8.56 ms** against a 16.667 ms budget, and the two-window column that had withheld the plain verdict is **4.01–4.75 ms** now that a background window queues at `Prefetch`. The structural argument is unchanged and is the load-bearing half: a surface PRESENTS finished pixels and does not rasterize them, and every millisecond measured is PDFium behind the engine lock. |
| `platform/win/WinFontResolver.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S2 · T16) | **Answers *what is installed*, and deliberately not *what to prefer*** — `platform/win/` makes no policy decisions, so the ordered candidate list is `core/render/FontSubstitution`'s and this walks it against DirectWrite's system font collection. **DirectWrite rather than GDI or `QFontDatabase`**, for two separate reasons: `FontMatch::filePath` is the load-bearing field (PDFium's hook is handed bytes, and GDI cannot name the FILE a face came from), and `QFontDatabase` is QtGui, which `platform/platform.h` may never name. The collection is **refreshed on every query** — a font installed while the application runs is the ordinary case for anyone fixing a document. Family names are read back in **en-us**, because on a Traditional-Chinese Windows the same family also has a Chinese name and a PDF names its fonts in Latin. | `pdf/render/FontResolverBridge`, `core/render/FontSubstitution`, `core/reflow/Shaper` |
| `platform/win/WinShellIntegration.{h,cpp}` | 🟡 (S1 assoc ✅, S15 rest) | **Real as of T1… T7:** per-user ProgID registration under `HKCU\Software\Classes`, the `.pdf` `OpenWithProgids` entry, association query, and `showDefaultAppsUi()`. That last one is **not a setter and cannot be**: since Windows 8 the association is protected by a hash the OS computes, so an app can register a ProgID but only the user can make it default — modelling it as `setDefaultHandler()` would be an API whose contract cannot be honoured. The registry root is injectable, so the write path is tested for real against a scratch key rather than against the association a person is using. **Still owed:** context-menu verbs, thumbnail provider, preview handler, index filter, jump list, taskbar progress — all Stage 15, each a separate COM server registration. | `core/settings/RecentFiles`, `ui/shell/MainWindow` |
| `platform/win/WinRichClipboard.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S12 paste path) — **re-tagged from (S5) on 2026-09-29** (`Stage5-TextSearch-Plan.md` D5-10) | Clipboard flavours beyond Qt's. **Stage 5 needs none of them**: Qt publishes plain text, HTML (as CF_HTML) and bitmaps itself, so copy and Snapshot go through `QClipboard`. What is left is the **vector metafile** — reading one is Stage 12's *create from clipboard*, and **writing one from page content is `DW-2`** (`Deferred-Work.md`), because it would need PDFium's `HDC` render path. | `core/create/DocumentBuilder` (Stage 12) |
| `platform/win/WinCertificateStore.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S10 recipient lookup, S11 full) | Enumerates digital IDs and root certificates from the OS store; builds chains; resolves certificate-encryption recipients. | `core/security/SignatureValidator`, `pdf/cos/PublicKeySecurityHandler`, `ui/dialogs/DigitalIdManager` |
| `platform/win/WinCryptoProvider.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S11) | Signs a hash through the OS crypto provider, including smart cards and HSMs; verifies; fetches OCSP and CRL. | `pdf/ops/SignatureOps`, `core/security/SignatureValidator` |
| `platform/win/WinSecretStore.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S11) | OS-key-protected storage for signing-ID passwords and service tokens. | `core/security/SignatureRequest`, `core/settings/Settings` |
| `platform/win/WinPrinting.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S8) | Printer enumeration and job submission with duplex, tray, and page-range control beyond `QPrinter`. | `ui/dialogs/PrintDialog` |
| `platform/win/WinPrinterDriverService.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S16) | Installs, services, and configures the **virtual PDF printer**, and receives its spooled jobs. | `core/batch/BatchRunner`, the installer |
