| `platform/win/WinScanner.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S16) | Imaging-device enumeration and page acquisition with ADF and duplex control. | `ui/dialogs/ScanDialog` |
| ~~`platform/win/WinSpeech.{h,cpp}`~~ | ⤵ | **Withdrawn 2026-08-23** with Read Aloud and the rest of the accessibility surface — see [`Features.md` → *Removed from scope*](./Features.md#removed-from-scope--accessibility-2026-08-23). Its interface `platform::ISpeech` goes with it, taking the platform count from sixteen to fifteen. | — |
| `platform/win/WinOfficeBridge.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S8 export, S12 import, S16 add-ins) | Drives the installed office suite for high-fidelity conversion in both directions; hosts the add-ins' entry points. Which direction uses it versus a first-party writer is open decision O1. | `pdf/ops/ExportOps`, `core/create/DocumentBuilder`, `core/batch/BatchRunner` |
| `platform/win/WinBrowserBridge.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S16) | Native-messaging host for the browser extensions. | `app/CommandLine` |
| `platform/win/WinRightsManagement.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S16) | Applies and reads enterprise information-protection labels; tracks and revokes access. | `core/security/SecurityScheme` |
| `platform/win/WinProcessSandbox.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S10) | Launches a restricted child process for protected-view rendering of untrusted files. | `core/security/TrustPolicy`, `app/CompositionRoot` |
| `platform/win/WinPowerSession.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S2 · T12) | Sleep/resume, battery state, and session lock/unlock notifications, plus the presentation-mode keep-awake. **"No battery" is a distinct answer from "on mains"** — a desktop cannot exercise a throttling policy, and `hasBattery()` is what lets guide case A-62 report *not applicable* explicitly rather than pass vacuously. A UPS (`PBT_POWERSETTINGCHANGE` source 2) reports as MAINS: throttling the renderer for the length of a power blip is a visible stutter bought for nothing. **The throttle WIRE is T15's**, with `performance/throttleOnBattery`; this file is what it will be made of. | `core/render/RenderScheduler`, `core/settings/Settings` |
| `platform/win/WinMessageWindow.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S2 · T12) | **A row this stage's file manifest did not list**, added with the file on the precedent T11 set for `core/render/DiskTileStore`. A hidden top-level window, shared by the two classes above, because on Windows a notification IS a window message and neither of them may own a `QWidget` — `platform/` may not name one. **Top-level and not `HWND_MESSAGE`**, which is the load-bearing detail: message-only windows do not receive broadcasts, so a display watcher built on the obvious choice would register successfully and never fire. | `platform/win/WinDisplayTopology`, `platform/win/WinPowerSession` |
| `platform/win/WinDurableFiles.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S3 · T7; **the replace rebuilt 2026-09-27, S4 guide I-31**) | **A row the file map did not have**, added by `Stage3-Mutation-Plan.md` D3-4. `IDurableFiles` on Windows: `syncToDisk` is `FlushFileBuffers` on a handle opened `OPEN_EXISTING` for write; `replaceFile` **carries the target's identity onto the replacement, then makes ONE atomic rename over the target** — its named streams (the `Zone.Identifier` a plain rename throws away) copied and flushed, its DACL with its inheritance protection, its creation time and hidden/system/archive/not-indexed attributes, and then `SetFileInformationByHandle` renames with POSIX semantics (which succeeds past a reader sharing delete), falling back to the classic rename where the OS or volume has none. **Until 2026-09-27 it was `ReplaceFileW` with a backup name, and that was an S1**: `ReplaceFileW` is two renames inside the calling process, and a process killed between them left NO file at the target — found by `tst_recoverykill` once its kills stopped being tick-locked (I-30). Not carried, and carried by `ReplaceFileW`: the short name, the object id, compression and encryption, and the owner — the header says why each does not matter. A new target is `MoveFileExW(MOVEFILE_WRITE_THROUGH)`, never over an existing file and never across volumes. A read-only target is refused by its own key (`error.file.readOnly`) before the OS is asked; the OS would refuse it anyway, as `ERROR_ACCESS_DENIED`. **From T8** also `removeFile` (an absent file is success) and `processExists` (a handle to an exited process reads as gone). Four `layer-lint` waivers, one per call spelling, each behind one helper — `CreateFileW`, `MoveFileExW`, `DeleteFileW` and, since I-31, `SetFileInformationByHandle`, which joined the gate's list with it; `forceClassicRenameForTesting` lets `tst_durablefiles` run the fallback this machine never takes. Decides nothing: whether and where to save is `core/doc/SaveCoordinator`'s. → `tst_durablefiles`. | `core/doc/SaveCoordinator`, `core/cmd/RecoveryJournal`, `platform/win/PlatformFactory` |
| `platform/win/PlatformFactory.{h,cpp}` | ✅ | `platform::createPlatform()` — assembles the Windows bundle, and the only exported symbol from this layer. **Four** of seventeen slots are non-null as of Stage 2 T12 — `ISingleInstanceBus` and `IShellIntegration` from Stage 1, `IDisplayTopology` and `IPowerSession` from T12; the count of what is *not* implemented is logged at assembly rather than left implicit. The executable path is passed IN rather than resolved with `GetModuleFileNameW`: `app/` already knows where it launched from, and a test must be able to assemble a bundle without registering anything. | `app/CompositionRoot` |

### 3.13 `ui/shell/` — the application frame

| File | Status | Role | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ui/shell/MainWindow.{h,cpp}` | 🟡 (S1 T12) | The frame: ribbon, QAT, document tab bar, sidebar dock, status bar, message bar. Owns the open-document set. Contains **no** document logic — it dispatches `ActionId`s. **Real as of T9:** the frame, the sidebar with its edge strips, the status bar, the theme, `openFiles()` under D1-2 (existence and `.pdf` suffix, **not one byte read** — case A-58), `raiseToForeground()` for flow B's handoff, and **four** registered actions at that point (`view.toggleSidebar`, `view.fullScreen`, `file.quit`, and `view.toggleRibbon` at T10) — what the window could genuinely service then, with every other id left declared and unregistered, which is the honest state at any count. Its **destructor disconnects the sidebar explicitly** (issue I-12): a lambda bound to a child's signal outlives the derived object, and `~QWidget` closing the window re-entered a `MainWindow` that no longer existed. **Real as of T10:** the ribbon, hosted through `setMenuWidget` so no docked pane can land above it, built from `res/ribbon.json` before the central surface and **after** `registerActions()` — the factory reads registration state as it builds each button, so the order is load-bearing rather than cosmetic. `showEvent` runs the D1-7(b) wiring self-check, asserting in debug and logging in release. **Real as of T11:** the menu-widget slot holds a COLUMN — the Quick Access Toolbar above or below the ribbon, both inside the one slot `QMainWindow` puts above the dock areas, so no docked pane can land between them — plus the tool finder in the tab strip's corner and the customization that is saved on every change rather than at shutdown. **Real as of T12:** the document tab strip over a two-page body (the placeholder, and the documents), `openFiles()` opening a stub tab per accepted path (D1-2), the window title and status bar following the current document, and **seventeen registered commands**. **And the rule T12 forced:** there can now be more than one of these — tearing a tab out makes a second one — while `ActionRegistry` is a single shared object whose registration is deliberately not idempotent. So **registration happens once per registry and no handler captures `this`**: each resolves `MainWindow::activeShell()` at dispatch time, which is what makes a Global command mean *the window I am looking at* and what stops a closed window's handler from being dispatched into. **Real as of T13:** the window owns a `ShortcutBinder`, created before the action factory because the factory asks it which context a dispatch happened in and released FIRST in the destructor because it filters the application's events — so `Ctrl+O`, `F4`, `F9`, `F11`, `Ctrl+W`, `Ctrl+Tab` and the rest of §8's Global table now reach the same commands the ribbon reaches. **Still owed:** the message bar (S2), and everything a real document brings at Stage 2. **Corrected 2026-09-13 (Stage 2 I-30):** a single-instance handoff goes through `openHandoff()`, which RAISES and then opens — the reverse order left an encrypted file's password prompt to a background process that Windows would not bring forward, so the window came up only after somebody found the prompt. **And (Stage 2 I-32):** `openFiles()` gathers every refusal of one call into a single window-level `OpenFailed` banner rather than a status-bar line alone, and a cancelled prompt raises none. **S3 · T10 — the mutation UI.** `registerMutationActions()` registers `edit.undo`, `edit.redo`, `page.rotate`, `file.save` and `file.saveAs` (53 registered of 361, `--list-actions`), each resolving the document in front at dispatch; undo's and redo's tooltips name the step from its `command.*` entry, and rotate's disabled tooltip is the document's own `Permissions` refusal. `rotatePages` runs a `RotatePagesDialog` choice as one command or one group (D3-13). `saveDocument` shows WHY before a forced Save-As (a `SaveAsRequired` banner, then `saveDocumentAs`), and a failure is a `SaveFailed` banner the document keeps. **`closeDocument(index, disposition)` returns a `Status`** (D3-14): a dirty document asks `UnsavedChangesDialog`, and with nobody to ask the close is REFUSED with `Unavailable`; `closeEvent` puts every window close, `file.quit` and a torn-off window's close through it, and a refusal keeps the window. `updateDocumentTitles()` puts `shell.document.dirtyTitle`'s marker on the tab and the title, and a mutation calls it rather than `updateDocumentChrome()` so a dismissed banner is not raised again by the next command. The Save As path has a parameterized twin, `setSaveAsAnswerForTesting` (convention 16). **S3 · T11 — recovery.** `recoverDocument(RecoveryCandidate)` opens the candidate's source through the backend and builds a `DocumentWindow` that replays the journal: under the source's own path when the source still matches and the document was that file, otherwise **untitled**. It raises a `Recovered` banner — the steps restored, *"step k could not be recovered: why"*, a last change still being made — and then removes the crashed session's directory. A refusal (damaged, source gone, the file already open here, an open refused, a journal that replays nothing) opens nothing, keeps the directory, and says so in a window-scoped `OpenFailed` line. **`closeDocument` now finds the tab by its widget**, not by its path: two untitled documents both answer to the empty path. A Save As proposes `DocumentWindow::suggestedSavePath()`. **S3 · T12:** with `documents/warnOnRepairedSave` off, a REPAIRED document's Save skips the `SaveAsRequired` explanation and still goes to Save As; every other reason still explains. **S4 · T5 — pane hosting.** The window builds the sidebar's document panes and owns them: **one `ThumbnailsPane` per window, re-pointed** by `updateDocumentPanes()` from `updateDocumentChrome()`, so a tab switch, an open and a close take one path and a pane is never rebuilt for a tab. A mutation hands the pane the new **revision** rather than re-pointing it, which is what keeps a rotation on page 40 from sending the pane back to page 1 while still re-keying every request (D2-12). `updateViewChrome()` sets the pane's selection **silently**, which is what makes the click→view→selection round terminate. **Four more registered commands (57 of 361, `--list-actions` 2026-09-20):** `pane.thumbnails` — which shows, selects and FOCUSES, because a command that did only the first reads as doing nothing from an auto-hidden sidebar and one that skipped the third leaves §8's Page Thumbnails table dead — plus `page.goToSelected`, `page.thumbnailsLarger` and `page.thumbnailsSmaller`. `page.rotate` is in that table too and is **not** a fifth: the pane reaches Stage 3's registration by declaring its context (D4-1). **S4 · T6 adds the other two panes and seven more commands (64 of 361, `--list-actions` 2026-09-21):** `pane.bookmarks`, `pane.destinations`, and the five §8 Bookmarks rows that navigate. `updateDocumentPanes()` hands both panes the backend's own `outline()` and `destinations()` — pointer fetches into models hydrated once at open (D4-3), which is what makes re-pointing on every tab change free and is asserted by a case that shows the panes five times and reads neither model again. **One `goToDestination` closure serves both panes**, so a bookmark and a named destination naming the same target cannot land in different places. **S4 · T8 registers eight more (366 declared / 74 registered / 292 unregistered, `--list-actions` 2026-09-21):** the three §8 chords that toggle a `units/*` row — `Ctrl+U`, `Ctrl+'`, `Ctrl+;` — plus `view.toggleGuides` and `view.invertColors`, both declared since Stage 1 and chordless, and the three ids T8 declares for the guide menu, `view.lockGuide`, `view.deleteGuide` and `view.clearGuides`. **A toggle's handler writes the SETTING and nothing else**, and its `checked` predicate reads it back: every `DocumentWindow` subscribes to the group, so the state reaches every tab and survives a restart, where a handler reaching into the view in front would make the toggle mean *this tab* on Monday and *the preference* on Tuesday. **Convention 14 cost a crash here before it cost a comment** — the first version captured `this` in those handlers, the registry outlived the window that registered them, and the second `MainWindow` of `tst_recoverydialog` read a destroyed object's `Settings` while building its ribbon. `showGuideMenu()` builds the ruler's and the guide's context menu through `ActionFactory` (D1-7(a)), offering the two that need a guide only over one; the TARGET is recorded on the view's `GridGuideLayer` when the menu is raised, which is Stage 1's I-50 in a second place. **`view.pageBackground` stays unregistered**: substituting a page colour needs a colour pair no declared key holds. **S4 · T11 — the windows (373 declared / 92 registered / 281 unregistered, `--list-actions` 2026-09-21).** `registerArrangeActions()` registers `window.tileHorizontal`, `window.tileVertical` and `window.cascade` — declared since Stage 1 and registered by nothing until now — plus the two ids T11 mints, `window.merge` and `window.list`; the first four are DISABLED with one window open, by `shell.tooltip.needsWindows`, and the list is not, because a list of one is a true statement about the application. `showWindowList()` builds that popup through **`ActionFactory::addNonCommandMenuItem`**, the ribbon overflow's precedent: a window has no `ActionId` and there is nothing for `ActionRegistry` to route, and what IS a command is opening the list. **`window.new` stopped meaning *a new empty window*** and became `openSecondView()` — the tab in front opened a SECOND time through the backend into a new window whose tab is a companion of this one (D4-13's exception); with no document in front it still opens an empty window, and an untitled recovered document is refused by `error.window.untitled`, because its bytes came from a source it chose not to write into. **`window.syncScroll` became one switch with two subjects**: `Features.md` lists synchronized scrolling under both split views (§1.2) and document windows (§1.1), and to the person holding the mouse they are one idea — a split takes precedence when there is one, and with no split it frees the windows. **And the window in front decides a render lane** (A2's named work): `refreshWindowFocusState()` walks the window set from `activeShell()` and tells every tab whether it is in the background. It is driven from `activeShell()` rather than from `isActiveWindow()` for the reason `raiseToForeground` already records — under `offscreen` the activation event never arrives — and with one window it is a no-op by construction, so nothing in the product changes until there are two. **S4 · T12 — reading mode, Document Properties and the labels (373 declared / 94 registered / 279 unregistered, `--list-actions` 2026-09-23).** Two ids already declared gain handlers: `view.readingMode` (`Ctrl+L`), which hides the header and the docks and **restores what it hid rather than a default** — which panes were open, the sidebar's width as the WIDGET reports it (a person who has just dragged the splitter has a width the dock has not written down) and which edge it was docked to, recorded before anything is hidden; and `file.documentProperties` (`Ctrl+D`), DISABLED where there is nobody to ask (convention 16). **Reading mode keeps the status bar**: `Features.md` §1.2 calls the mode *maximum page area*, not *no information at all*, and a person who has hidden the ribbon has no other way to find out where they are. It is NOT `F11` — that is the window state and is `FullScreenController`'s level 1 (D4-12) — and the two compose. `documentProperties()` assembles the dialog's facts from `metadata()`, `documentId()`, `repairReport()` and the VIEW's page tree, the last so an unsaved rotation is reflected in the page size reported (D3-15). It also installs the status bar's page-label resolver, resolving against the tab in front at the moment Enter is pressed rather than holding a tree (convention 14). **Since S6 · T12** `askMeasurementScale` -- `MeasurementScaleDialog` asked, from two lengths or to calibrate, the three `measuring/*` keys written; `annot.measurementScale` (disabled where nobody can be asked) and `annot.measureCalibrate` registered; a reading and the notices said in the status bar; `setMeasurementScaleAnswerForTesting`. **S6 · T13:** comments in and out -- `importComments`, `exportComments`, `migrateComments`, the annotations' `copyAnnotations`, `cutAnnotations`, `pasteAnnotations` and the four ids (`comment.import`, `comment.export`, `comment.migrate` minted, `edit.cut`), defined in `ui/shell/MainWindowExchange.cpp`; `edit.copy` takes selected annotations first and `edit.paste` RawPage's own clipboard type first. | `ui/shell/*`, `ui/shell/DocumentWindow`, `ui/panes/{ThumbnailsPane,BookmarksPane,DestinationsPane}`, `core/cmd/ActionRegistry`, `core/cmd/RecoveryJournal`, `app/Services` **S4 · T13:** `paneLayoutState()` / `applyPaneLayoutState()`, the sidebar as one hand-readable `key=value` string for `core::SessionWindow::paneLayout` — encoded here because a dock edge, a width and a pane id are Qt facts, and **an EMPTY string is a layout meaning *no panes* rather than an absence**, which is what `general/rememberPaneLayout` off restores onto a window that is built with the sidebar open. **S5 · T5:** `edit.copy` and `edit.selectAll` registered at §8's Common layer, each reaching the tab in front's selection (96 registered of 373), and the selection's copy worded in the status bar -- progress while a copy reads its pages, then what reached the clipboard, or why nothing did. **S5 · T13:** `compareDocuments` -- the dialog's choice written to the four `documents/compare*` keys, both files opened again through the backend as the comparison's OWN handles (a refusal closes what was already opened and is said on the status and message bars), and ONE `ui/viewer/ComparisonView` owned per window, replacing the one open and closed with the window; `compare.documents` (disabled where nobody can answer the dialog, convention 16), `nav.nextDifference` and `nav.prevDifference` registered -- **110 of 373**, no id minted, `compare.showReport` left to Stage 8. **S6 guide I-24 (2026-10-20):** `followTool()` -- each active pane's `toolChanged` re-asks every action, so a tool put away after its use or by `Esc`, with no command run, is SHOWN put away; disconnected in the destructor (convention 10), because a note's editor commits, and puts the tool away, when the focus leaves it. |
| `ui/shell/MainWindowExchange.cpp` | ✅ **(S6 · T13)** — **a row the file manifest did not name**, added with the file | **`MainWindow`'s comments in and out, in a file of their own** (D6-20, D6-21, D6-22, D6-40(h), (i)): which file -- asked of the person, or a case's `ExchangeAnswers`, and disabled where nobody can be asked (convention 16) -- handed to the backend; the ONE command the merge, the migration or the paste plans run through the tab's journal; and what it did worded on the status bar and, when anything was not brought over, in `ExchangeReportDialog`. A paste lands on the page under the middle of what is shown, each annotation a new `/NM`; a cut is the copy and the overlay's one delete. It decides nothing of what an import means: that is `core/doc/Fdf`'s and `core/doc/CommentMigrator`'s. → `tests/ui/tst_exchange`, `tests/composition/tst_exchangeproduct`. | `ui/shell/MainWindow`, `ui/shell/CommentMigration`, `ui/dialogs/ExchangeReportDialog`, `core/doc/Fdf` |
| `ui/shell/MainWindowAudio.cpp` | ✅ **(S6 · T14)** — **a row the file manifest did not name**, added with the file | **`MainWindow`'s half of a sound comment, in a file of its own** (D6-18, D6-41(i)): the recorder asked (`AudioRecorderDialog`) and the WAV `annot.attachAudio` reads asked -- each answering nothing where nobody can be asked (convention 16), or a case's `AudioAnswers` -- through hooks every document's overlay is given; and a sound PLAYED on a double click, its samples carried by the command that made it or read from its `/Sound` stream (`IDocumentBackend::soundSamples`), changing no byte; `lastPlayed()` for a case. → `tests/composition/tst_audioproduct`. |
| `ui/shell/CommentMigration.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S6 · T13)** — **a row the file manifest did not name**, added with the file | **One migration, run without holding the UI thread** (D6-21, D6-40(h)): the comparison window's pipeline reused -- both documents' text from `core/text/DocumentIndex` at `Background`, their alignment from `core/text/ComparisonRun` at text only on its own thread -- then the older revision's comments exported and handed to `core/doc/CommentMigrator`. It owns the older revision's handle (opened for it) and closes it with the three-step close; the revision is the tab's handle, never closed here. It changes no document: the outcome is the window's to merge. | `core/text/DocumentIndex`, `core/text/DocumentComparer`, `core/doc/CommentMigrator`; used by `ui/shell/MainWindowExchange.cpp` |
| `ui/shell/Ribbon.{h,cpp}` | 🟡 (S1 T10, T11; S5 guide I-16, 2026-10-02, the key tips' dismissal) | The ribbon widget: tabs, groups, sizing behaviour, collapse, contextual-tab show/hide, key tips. **Since S5 guide I-16 the key tips last while the ribbon has the focus**: losing it dismisses them and leaves the focus where it went, and a mouse press anywhere -- an application filter installed only while they are up, mouse events only -- dismisses them and gives the focus back first. **Risk A3 was proven by the T2 spike and is productionized here**, and the file carries all three pieces of the named custom work the verdict rests on: `RibbonPageLayout`, a custom `QLayout`, because collapse changes what a child *is* rather than how much space it gets; a chevron overflow over `QTabBar`, whose own overflow is scroll arrows; and `restoreTabOrder()`, which re-chains `Tab` over the groups in **visual** order whenever the collapsed set changes (issue **I-05** — collapse re-parents, and re-parenting appends to the end of the focus chain). Hysteresis on the un-collapse direction only, so a slow drag across the threshold cannot oscillate. **Two width facts, both found by asking a real ribbon to be narrow (I-19):** the page layout's minimum is **one** collapsed group rather than the sum of them, and the tab bar's size policy is `Ignored` — together they were holding the whole main window open at 1044 px and making the chevron unreachable. **I-19 was closed at T11**: the body now sits in a `QScrollArea`, so `RibbonPageLayout::minimumSize()` can go back to the honest content minimum — the SUM of the collapsed groups — without that number reaching the main window. Collapse is still exhausted first and the row scrolls only below the width every group collapsed needs; the scrollbar's height is reserved at every width, because a ribbon whose height depends on its width is R-4's flicker wearing a different hat. **Corrected 2026-08-31 (I-42):** key tips are now *reachable* — `AppRibbonKeyTips` is registered and toggles them, the follow-up letter arrives in `Ribbon::keyPressEvent` because `showKeyTips()` takes focus and returns it, and each tab's letter is **catalog data** (`ribbon.keytip.<tabId>`, resolved by `RibbonTabBuilder`, which reports a missing one as a problem) rather than the first character of its title, which was untypable in `zh-TW` and collided there. Drawn since T10, raisable by nobody until now. **Corrected 2026-08-31 (I-50):** a right-click on a **disabled** control now names its command too — Qt hands the event to the nearest enabled ancestor, so `Ribbon::contextMenuEvent` resolves the position with `childAt()`, which does return disabled children. Before, 338 of 361 commands could not be added to the toolbar at all. **Corrected 2026-08-30 (I-36):** that `Ignored` policy also meant the tab bar had **no width preference for the layout to honour**, and beside a separate `addStretch(1)` the spacer took the entire strip — so the `QTabBar` was laid out **0 px wide from T10 until a person went looking for the View tab**, and no ribbon tab was ever drawn. The tab bar now carries the stretch itself; the corner widget is held right by an expanding tab bar rather than by a spacer. **Still owed:** contextual tabs beyond the one that proves the mechanism (per feature, Stages 5–13). | `ui/shell/RibbonTabBuilder`, `ui/shell/ActionFactory`, `core/cmd/ActionRegistry` |
| `ui/shell/RibbonTabBuilder.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S1 T10) | Builds the ribbon from a declarative layout resource plus the `ActionRegistry`, so adding a command never means writing widget code. **It makes no decision the resource could have expressed** — not the tab, not the group, not the button size, not the collapse order — which is the only thing that keeps D1-8's promise true: a command added in Stage 9 is a registry row plus a JSON line. Asserted from both directions by `tst_ribbonbuild`: everything in the resource is in the widget tree, **and no control exists that the resource did not declare**. | `res/ribbon.json`, `ui/shell/RibbonModel`, `core/cmd/ActionRegistry` |
| `ui/shell/RibbonModel.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S1 T10) | The ribbon resource's schema and its **one** parser: `QByteArray` → tabs/groups/controls plus diagnostics, naming no widget at all. That last part is what makes it shareable — `tools/ribbon-validate` **compiles this translation unit in** rather than reimplementing the schema, so the build-time check (D1-8) and the runtime build cannot disagree about what the file means. A validator with its own parser can certify a file the runtime reads differently, which is worse than no validator. Same technique `tests/unit` uses for `app/CommandLine.cpp`. Every problem is collected rather than the first returned, and each carries a **line number** resolved from the raw bytes. | `core/cmd/ActionId.h`, `res/ribbon.json` |
| `ui/shell/QuickAccessToolbar.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S1 T11) | Add/remove/reorder commands, above-or-below placement, reset — all five of `Features.md` §1.1's operations. **It holds `ActionId`s and rebuilds its buttons from that list**: the list is what gets serialized, restored and compared against the factory default, so making the LAYOUT the state would put the source of truth in the wrong place. Every operation is a command (`ui.qatAdd`, `ui.qatRemove`, `ui.qatPlacement`, `ui.resetCustomization`) rather than a context-menu lambda, which is what keeps them reachable from the Stage 14 batch runner. **`ui.qatAdd` and `ui.qatRemove` are the product's first PARAMETERIZED commands** and `ActionRegistry::dispatch` carries no argument — the surface that raised the menu records the target in `pendingTarget()` and the handler reads it. That is the Stage 1 answer and not a general one; open decision **O11**. **Changed 2026-08-31: the row reorders by DRAG.** A press, a move past the platform's start-drag distance, and the widget follows the pointer inside the layout — `commands_` is committed once on release, so nothing rebuilds mid-drag and deletes the button being held. A **lock** (`ui/qatLocked`, unlocked by default) refuses the gesture; `ui.qatMoveLeft` / `ui.qatMoveRight` stay registered but leave the menu, since a gesture reaches no batch runner. Two index spaces meet here and confusing them was **I-52**: a slot among the buttons is not a layout index, and the layout holds the trailing stretch. **Corrected 2026-08-31 (I-49): a rebuild now SHOWS what it creates.** A widget built with an already-visible parent starts hidden and is an empty layout item, so every customization after the window appeared produced invisible buttons and a 4-pixel row — reset included, which made restarting the only recovery. **Corrected 2026-08-31 (I-43):** this row, the class's own header and `CLAUDE.md` all said reordering was among the five operations, and for a person it was among none of them — `moveCommand` was public, the order was persisted, and no menu item, drag or `ActionId` could reach it, so the only caller was a test. It was fixed with `ui.qatMoveLeft` / `ui.qatMoveRight` in the context menu, each **disabled at the end it cannot move towards** — and **superseded hours later by the drag recorded above**, which took both items back out of the menu at the user's request and left them registered and unlisted, reachable from the tool finder and flow K. The case that covers them still asserts the ROUTE as well as the dispatch; it now asserts the menu does **not** offer them. | `ui/shell/ActionFactory`, `ui/shell/UiCustomization` |
| `ui/shell/UiCustomization.{h,cpp}` | 🟡 (S1 T11) | Serializes the QAT and shortcut customizations for export and team deployment. **Widget-free**, so the Stage 14 batch runner can read and write a customization without a window. **D1-16 in both directions:** the blob carries its own `version` because a file that leaves this machine arrives at a build with a different idea of the format, an older one is migrated and re-stamped, and a **newer** one is read for what we understand with its unknown members carried through verbatim (`extras`) — the downgrade half, which is the one that loses a user's layout during a rollback. Commands are stored as **action string ids**, resolved through the alias table, never as enum values. **Fixed 2026-09-14 (Stage 2 I-40):** `applyToSettings` reported the shortcuts it KEPT as the ones it dropped — `KeyBindingStore::importOverrides` returns the dropped count, and this read it as accepted, Stage 1 I-51's misreading in a second place; nothing called it yet, and `tst_qat` now asserts both directions. **Still owed:** the ribbon half (custom groups are Stage 14's customize dialog), and the file picker — this class converts to and from BYTES because `layer-lint` allows a filesystem write in `pdf/` and `core/settings/` only, and a `ui/` class that writes files is one that will eventually write a document. | `core/settings/Settings`, `core/cmd/KeyBindingStore` |
| `ui/shell/ShortcutBinder.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S1 T13; S4 D4-22, 2026-09-27, the lone `Alt` on release; S5 guide I-04, 2026-10-01, read-only text keeps Copy and Select All) | Watches focus to compute the active `KeyContext`, resolves a chord through `KeyBindingStore`, and dispatches. **A lone `Alt` — §8's key-tips row — is answered on its RELEASE since S4 D4-22**, and only when no other key, mouse button, wheel or loss of focus came while it was held (`loneAltHeld_`), so `Alt`+wheel on the Loupe never pops the key tips; every other chord still dispatches on its press, through one shared `resolveKey`. **The file that makes one chord mean two things** — and the file that keeps that possible, because Qt's own shortcut system cannot express §8's *innermost context, then `Common`, then `Global`* and would have made a user's remap a shadow copy inside 358 actions. Three separable parts: `activeContext()`, a pure walk up `parentWidget()` from the focus to the innermost widget that DECLARES a context (D1-5, `tst_context` over synthetic chains); `chordFor()`, the QtGui boundary `core/cmd/Chord`'s header promised — one table read both ways, so `unreachableBindings()` can answer *which §8 row names a key no keyboard can produce* from the same rows that produce them — **plus `chordCandidatesFor()` since Stage 2 I-33**, because with Shift held Qt reports the character a punctuation key types (`Ctrl+Shift+=` arrives as `Key_Plus`), and §8 spells some rows by the key and some by the character, so a press is tried as reported, then on the unshifted key of a US keyboard, then without Shift; and `activate()`, which resolves and dispatches. **Five outcomes, and only two consume the key**: a dispatch and an `Unavailable` refusal consume, while a `NotFound` (most of §8 at Stage 1, where seventeen of 358 ids are registered), a `WidgetNative` row and an unbound chord are left for whoever else wants them. **The focused widget gets first refusal through Qt's own `ShortcutOverride` handshake**, so a `QLineEdit` keeps `Ctrl+A` without this file maintaining a list of widgets that edit text. **Plus one rule since Stage 5 T5 (S5 guide I-04):** a READ-ONLY text view accepts the handshake for nothing, so `selectableTextOwnsKey()` leaves Copy and Select All to any focused widget whose own `textInteractionFlags` property says its text can be selected — read from the property, never the type — which is what keeps the log pane's and Document Properties' `Ctrl+C` and `Ctrl+A` once `edit.copy` and `edit.selectAll` are registered at Common. **One binder per window, filtering `qApp`** and answering only for its own window, because a second `MainWindow` would otherwise dispatch the same chord twice. **Nothing in Stage 1 declares a context**: the Viewer surface is Stage 2, the panes Stage 4, Write mode Stage 13 — so `activeContext()` answers `Global` throughout the shell today, which is the honest answer and is exactly what makes every §8 Global default work. `shortcutComplaints()` is T13's half of D1-7: an action carrying its own key sequence, or a free-standing `QShortcut`, is a chord that bypassed the context stack and the user's remap on its way in. **The keypad's `+` under `Ctrl` also offers `Ctrl+=`** (S4 guide **I-21**, 2026-09-27): it arrives as `Key_Plus` without Shift, measured through `QWindowsKeyMapper`, so the reported chord is `Ctrl++`, which nothing binds; the fallback is tried only when that binds nothing and never without `Ctrl`, so the bare keypad `+` stays §8's `+`. → `tst_viewercommands` (A-92). | `core/cmd/KeyBindingStore`, `core/cmd/KeyContext`, `core/cmd/ActionRegistry` |
| `ui/shell/DocumentTabBar.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S1 T12) | Tabs, reorder, tear-off to a new window, re-dock, middle-click close, overflow. **It reports gestures and moves nothing** — a tear-off has to choose between a new window and another window's strip, and that choice is not available to a widget that knows only its own tabs, so `MainWindow` owns it. **Unlike the ribbon it uses `QTabBar`'s own overflow**: a ribbon has a small fixed tab set and shows a chevron, a document strip has an unbounded user-driven one and every product with one shrinks and then scrolls. The gestures themselves are manual case **M-03**; everything they end in — reorder, release, adopt, detach, close — is asserted in `tests/ui/tst_documenttabs.cpp`. **A tear-off is reported on the RELEASE, not when the drag crosses the 40 px threshold** (issue **I-34**, found by the first M-03 walk): reporting at the crossing carried the position from that one move event, which left the drop-onto-another-strip branch correct and unreachable by hand. The widget carries the tab in `tearingTab_` from the crossing to the button-up and swallows the moves between, so Qt cannot restart a drag it was told had ended. **A window emptied by a move closes itself** (**I-35**, `MainWindow::closeIfEmptied`), while one emptied by a close stays; only a `WA_DeleteOnClose` window can go, because the primary window is a stack object in `app/main.cpp`. | `ui/shell/MainWindow`, `ui/shell/DocumentWindow` |
| `ui/shell/DocumentWindow.{h,cpp}` | 🟡 (S1 T12 stub, S2 · T13 the viewer, S3 · T10 the journal, S3 · T11 recovery, S4 · T8 the rulers, S4 · T9 the split; S4 · T12 page labels — the "coordinate readout" once credited here was never built, and is the rulers' since I-17; S5 · T11 Reflow view; 2026-10-03 the rulers' corner box a button; **S6 · T4 the show step; S6 · T5 the annotation overlay and the properties bar**) | **Since S6 · T5** it owns ONE `ui/viewer/AnnotationOverlay` -- handed to every pane, given the journal's store and `journal->run` as its runner, its refusals emitted in words (`annotationRefused`, the status bar's) -- and the `ui/viewer/PropertiesBar` under the find bar, hidden until `Ctrl+E`, refreshed from the selection or the armed tool. **Since S6 · T4** it sets the journal's show step for its handle and every companion's, shows a replayed session once, shows a companion the leader's unsaved edits when it attaches and puts a detached one back on its file, and hands the scheduler the engine's tree with each scope. Per-document container. **Since 2026-10-03 the rulers' corner box is a button**, on the user's request (`Features.md` §1.3): a press dispatches `view.cycleRulerUnit`, which steps `units/rulerUnit` to the next unit. The box holds no state, and `applyGuideSettings` sets its text from the row. It is mouse-only (`Qt::NoFocus`). It draws its own text with no margin, because a stock `QToolButton` elided "mm" to "…" in the 22-pixel box on the `windows` platform (S5 guide A-115). **Since S5 · T11 it holds the tab's Reflow view** beside the page surface (D5-16): `setReflowing` hides the split and its rulers for `ui/viewer/ReflowView` -- entering at the block at the active pane's top edge, leaving with the reflow's top block at that pane's top edge -- and a find or bookmark hit leaves reflow first, the focus kept where it was unless the reflow had it. **The D1-2 stub is gone as of S2 T13**: it owns the open `DocumentHandle`, the document's `Revision` and a `PageView`. **As of S3 · T10 it owns the document's `CommandJournal` — the only holder of a mutable `Document` (D3-10) — its `RecoveryJournal` (only for an editable document, under `Services::recoveryRoot`) and its `SaveCoordinator`.** Every mutation tells `RenderScheduler` the scope and revision, then `PageView::modelChanged` lays the view out again from the MODEL's tree before any tile moves, then `mutated()` is emitted; the view is handed the engine's tree too, for D3-15's delta. A recovery journal that stops recording is said ONCE (`recoveryUnavailable`). The document's own banners — a failed save, a forced Save-As's reason, recovery off — are kept here as conditions so a tab switch shows them again. A clean close discards the recovery directory; a dirty one keeps it unless a Discard removed it. **S3 · T11: a second constructor takes a `Recovery`** — the crashed session's directory, the source path, and whether the document is untitled — and replays that journal into the freshly hydrated `CommandJournal` BEFORE a recorder or a subscription is attached, then opens its own recovery journal SEEDED with the lines replay applied; `recoveryReport()` or `recoveryFailure()` says what happened. **An untitled document** has an empty `path()` and a `sourcePath()`; its Save is `SaveAsRequired` by `error.save.untitled` before `SaveCoordinator` is asked, its recovery meta says untitled, and `suggestedSavePath()` proposes *"<name> (recovered).pdf"* beside the source. **S4 · T8 gives it the two rulers**, in a grid with the page surface so a ruler shares the view's column and row exactly — a ruler one pixel wider than the surface it measures reads the wrong number everywhere. It reads the eleven `units/*` keys in `applyGuideSettings()` and subscribes to the `UnitsAndGuides` group beside the two it already had, recomputes both `RulerWidget::Mapping`s on every `viewChanged`, and maps a guide dragged off a ruler — reported in global coordinates, because Qt keeps the grab on the ruler — onto the page surface, making no guide when the drop is not over it. **The mapping guard asks `isHidden()`, not `isVisible()`:** this runs during `build()`, before the tab is in the window, and a widget whose ancestors are not on screen is not *visible* — asking the wrong question left every ruler blank until something else changed the view. **S4 · T9 gives it the split container** (D4-13), which is the last of the two things the D1-2 stub note said it did not hold. Its page surface is a `ui/viewer/SplitViewContainer` rather than a bare `PageView` — one pane when nobody has split the window, and a document that is never split costs one widget for it. Three things that named a single view now FAN OUT over `split()->views()`: the eleven `pageDisplay/` keys, the eleven `units/*` keys, and every mutation's `modelChanged`, the last of which is guide case A-54 — a pane that was not told the new revision would go on showing the un-rotated page with no error and no failing test, because the revision is in every tile's key. One thing NARROWS to the active pane: the rulers, the cursor readout and the guide menu, rebound on `activeViewChanged` and unbound when a pane is retired, because the layer is a CHILD `QObject` of the view and disconnecting the view alone would leave the guide connection standing (convention 10). `view()` now means *the active view*, which is what kept every caller of it — the status bar, `MainWindow::currentView()` and all twenty-five §8 Viewer commands — working unchanged. A pane a split adds is positioned from the primary rather than left where `setDocument` starts it, or half the window would jump back to page 1; and the guides are MIRRORED across panes, because a guide is a page fact in PDF points (D4-7) and what is per-view is the drag. **It is what CLOSES the handle**, in a three-step protocol the destructor writes out: cancel this document's render jobs and drain the scheduler, drop its tiles from the cache, then close — a worker inside PDFium on a document about to be destroyed is a use-after-free, and `IDocumentBackend::close()` states the protocol because no mutex can express it. **S4 · T11 gives it a third constructor: a COMPANION**, the second view of one document in a second window (`window.new`, D4-13's stated exception). It has its OWN `DocumentHandle` — which two windows need, because each runs the close protocol above — and **no journal, no recovery journal and no save coordinator**, so there is one undo stack and one Save for the file; two journals over one file would be the *changed on disk since it was opened* path D3-16 exists to refuse, manufactured by the product itself. It is therefore not editable and never dirty, which a null journal already answered correctly. What it SHARES is the only thing that makes it a view of the same document: it lays out from the LEADER'S model page tree at the leader's revision while rasterizing through its own handle — D3-15's split one window further out — so a rotation in the leader appears here, unsaved, with no second copy of anything. **When the leader closes the companion DETACHES rather than following it**, and that is correct rather than a fallback: the leader's disposition has just been settled, the model tree it was reading is gone, and the file its own handle rasterizes is still there. The leader's destructor detaches every companion BEFORE its `Document` goes, because a companion still pointing at that tree would lay out over freed memory on its next paint. It also forwards `SplitViewContainer::scrolled` as its own `scrolled`, which `ui/shell/WindowArranger` mirrors: a tab may not name another window. **Since S6 · T12** it owns the tab's `SnapTargets`, hands it to every pane, keeps the session's measurement history (`recordMeasurement`) and forwards a calibration and the measuring tools' notices (`measureNotice`); `applyGuideSettings` reads `units/snapToObjects` and `measuring/snapToPaths`. | `core/backend.h`, `ui/viewer/PageView`, `core/render/RenderScheduler`, `core/render/RenderCache` **It is what reads the viewer's preferences** (S2 · T15) and re-reads them on every change to `PageDisplay` **or `PerformanceAndCache`** — two groups, because `performance/prefetchPages` is a performance key that a viewer obeys, and the first version subscribed to one group and left that preference reaching nothing. The subscription is released in the destructor: `Settings` belongs to the composition root and outlives every tab. **S4 · T13:** `splitState()` / `applySplitState()`, the split as one string for `core::SessionDocument::splitState` — the mode by NAME and never by the enumerator's number, so inserting a fifth mode cannot silently re-read an old session as a different split. **S5 · T5:** it makes ONE `ui/viewer/SelectionOverlay` and hands it to every pane `handDocumentTo` reaches (D5-8), forwards its copy reports as its own signals so `MainWindow`'s one `disconnect(document, …)` covers them, and deletes it before the three-step close -- every pane let go of it first -- because it holds text tickets on this handle. **S5 · T6:** it ATTACHES its handle to `core/text/DocumentIndex` under the backend's `DocumentId` for it -- so `window.new`'s companion lands on its leader's entry (guide A-66) -- hands the selection that entry, gives every mutation's `Scope` to the index beside the scheduler (A-64), and DETACHES after the selection has let go and before the scheduler's jobs on the handle are cancelled, which is what lets the index re-send a request a companion is waiting on through the companion's own handle. `indexEntry()` is what its find bar searches. **S5 · T7:** it makes ONE `ui/write/FindReplaceBar` above the panes, hands it the index entry and the selection overlay, reveals the bar's current hit in the active pane, gives the page the focus back when the bar closes, and deletes the bar before the overlay it paints through. |
| `ui/shell/SidebarDock.{h,cpp}` | 🟡 (S1 T9; S4 guide I-24 and I-25, 2026-09-27, re-docking and the closed pane's strip; S5 guide A-124, 2026-10-04, the edge facing the page, `ui/shell/DockEdge`) | Pane hosting: dock left/right, float, auto-hide tab strip, width memory, per-document open-pane state. **Re-docking re-plugs (S4 guide I-24):** Qt 6.11.1's `setFloating(false)` clears the flag and leaves the dock out of the window's layout — measured on a bare `QMainWindow` too — and after the floating pane has been DRAGGED the window's layout is frozen — `restoreDockWidget` returns true and moves nothing — so re-docking is ours: `toggleFloating()`, taken by the float button and by a double-click on the title (`event()` answers it before `QDockWidget::event`), does `setFloating(false)`, `restoreDockWidget` or else `addDockWidget` on the last docked side, then a `restoreState(saveState())` round trip that unfreezes the layout. A drag onto an edge stays Qt's own plug. **And the edge strip of a CLOSED pane opens it on a click (S4 guide I-25)** through `view.toggleSidebar`, where from Stage 1 T9 it only peeked, which a pane that is not auto-hidden ignores; a hover still only peeks. **Per-document open-pane state, which this row listed as owed from Stage 1, is NOT built, by decision** — `Stage4-ViewingSurface-Plan.md` **D4-19**, recorded 2026-09-27 when the user's M-10 walk judged one pane per window, kept in front across a tab switch, the expected behaviour. **Real as of T9:** dock-left/right, float and re-dock come from `QDockWidget` and are not reimplemented; **auto-hide is ours**, because Qt has no notion of it — the pane collapses and the window shows an edge strip, and `peek()`/`unpeek()` reveal it *without* pinning it. Width memory is captured on **resize**, not on hide, because a collapsing splitter reports width 0 on the way out; a zero is never recorded. Pane chrome (pin, float, dock side) is deliberately **not** given `ActionId`s — the same call T4 made for its fifteen `WidgetNative` bindings — while the close button dispatches `view.toggleSidebar` through the registry. **Changed 2026-08-30 (I-37):** the strip peeks when the pointer **arrives on it**, not only when it is clicked — `MainWindow` filters the two strips for `QEvent::Enter`, since a `QToolButton` has no pointer-arrived signal; the click reveals as well, because a strip that looks like a button and ignores a press is its own defect. **And the strip goes while the pane is out (I-39)** — `isAutoHidden()` stays true through a peek by design, so `MainWindow::updateAutoHideStrips()` asks whether the pane is *peeking* as well; showing the stand-in beside the thing it stands in for had been the behaviour since T9 and was unseeable until a peek became performable. **S4 · T5 pays the pane debt (D4-2):** it holds a **pane registry keyed by `ActionId`** — the panes' own permanent public names, so the chooser dispatches `pane.thumbnails` through the registry rather than reaching into the dock, which is the call the close button made at T9 — over a `QStackedWidget`, and **the list holds exactly the panes that exist**: no greyed row, no *coming soon*, which is convention 6's *null member, never a stub* read across to a list a person chooses from and Stage 2's **I-11** read as a warning. The chooser is hidden while there is one pane and **visible from T6, where the list is three** — Thumbnails, Bookmarks, Destinations, in registration order; the Stage 1 placeholder survives as page 0 and is what a shell with NO panes registered shows. **Still owed:** per-document open-pane state and width memory across a restart, which are `SessionState`'s at **T13**. | `ui/panes/*`, `core/settings/SessionState` |
| `ui/shell/DockEdge.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(the user's requests, 2026-10-03 and 2026-10-04; S5 guide A-123, A-124)** — **a row the FileMap did not have**, added with the file | **The line on the side of a docked pane that faces the page.** The main window's dock separator is painted in the window's own colour by the native style, so nothing showed where a pane began or where to grab it. A child strip of the dock, stacked ABOVE its contents and transparent to the mouse, ONE LOGICAL PIXEL wide in `QPalette::Mid` (the theme's `Border` token): left when docked right, right when docked left, top when docked at the bottom, bottom when docked at the top, hidden when floating; it follows `dockLocationChanged`, `topLevelChanged` and the dock's resizes and added children. A child because the sidebar's contents reach its edge and would cover a line the dock painted; a strip and not a hairline because at 150 % a dock's edge can fall between device pixels and a hairline on it is clipped away (A-123's first version). Used by the search pane, the sidebar and the log pane. | `ui/panes/SearchPane`, `ui/shell/SidebarDock`, `ui/panes/LogPane` |
| `ui/shell/StatusBar.{h,cpp}` | 🟡 (S1 T9) | Page-number box, page controls, zoom controls, view-mode buttons, cursor coordinates, background-task progress. **Real as of T9:** the message area, the page box, the page total, the zoom label and the task indicator — **all built and disabled**, because there is no document to be on page 3 of. That is the point rather than a shortcut: "1 of 1 at 100 %" with nothing open is a lie that becomes very hard to see once there IS a document to compare against. The task indicator is hidden when idle. **Real as of T12:** there IS a document now, so `setDocumentPresent(true)` runs for the first time and the page box, page total and zoom label come alive with the current tab — and the page total still reads the em dash, because a stub tab has no pages and "1 of 1" would be inventing one. **Still owed:** everything that needs a `ViewportController` and a `RenderScheduler`, at Stage 2. | `ui/viewer/ViewportController`, `core/render/RenderScheduler` **REAL NUMBERS SINCE S2 · T14.** The page total was an em dash from T9 because there was no measurement to report, and it is a count now — the dash comes back when the last tab closes rather than the previous document's figure staying on screen. The page box, an input with no consumer since T9, now navigates: a number inside the document scrolls there, and one outside it puts the current page back rather than leaving the box holding a value nothing acted on. **One-based on screen and zero-based everywhere else**, converted here and in `MainWindow` and nowhere further in — a one-based index leaking into the model is an off-by-one that reaches the tile cache. **S4 · T12 — LOGICAL PAGE LABELS.** The box shows the page's `/PageLabels` label where the document has one ("iv", "A-3"), because a reader on the page printed 1 must see 1 and not 4; the total then carries the physical index beside it, since a document with roman front matter has two page 1s and the label alone cannot say which is on screen — and it does not when the label IS the physical number, where "of 8 (page 8)" would be noise. *Go to Page* resolves a typed LABEL through a resolver `MainWindow` installs, because the labels live on the backend's page tree and a status bar holding one would be a widget holding a document. **The label wins over the physical number**, which is a decision with a cost stated at the declaration: the box DISPLAYS the label, so typing what was just read must land where it was read, and the physical page whose number collides with a label is then reachable only by its label. An unknown label is refused without moving the view, exactly as an out-of-range number always was. |
| `ui/shell/Catalog.{h,cpp}` + `ShellKeys.def` | ✅ (S1 T9) | Resolving a catalog key to text — the one place `ui/` turns `shell.window.title` into words. **The context is the key's own namespace**, the part before the first dot, and NOT the enclosing class name `QObject::tr()` would use: the key is the source string in this project (T6), and a label built by `RibbonTabBuilder` has to resolve identically to the same label shown by `ToolFinder`. `ShellKeys.def` is the shell's key table — one file, two artifacts, the same shape as `ActionId.def` — so the constants the widgets use and the list `--dump-ui-strings` prints cannot drift, which is the only thing that makes guide case A-72 worth running. | `i18n/*.ts`, `core/cmd/ActionId.h` |
| `ui/shell/MessageBar.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S2 · T14) | The document-condition banner: form detected, signed, protected, repaired, in Write mode — each with its action button. **Stage 2 raises two of the five**, `Repaired` and `Protected`; the other three are declared now for `platform/platform.h`'s reason, so the vocabulary does not grow one enumerator at a time by whoever needed one. It says something true about the FILE that the file cannot say, which is why it is a persistent bar rather than a status message that scrolls away — a repaired document stays repaired, and **Stage 3 forces its first save to Save-As on exactly that statement**, so a banner the user missed is a banner that let them overwrite an original. One banner per kind, worst-first, and **cleared on every tab change**: the condition belongs to the tab, and a "this was repaired" banner left over the document that replaced it is a false statement about the file in front of the user. Its detail view **localizes the frame and prints `RepairKind` as a CODE** — those names are declared ASCII and non-localized by their own header, they are what the log holds, and translating thirteen xref-internals codes would put a different string in front of every bug reporter. **Corrected 2026-09-13 (Stage 2 I-29):** the banner used to fill from `QPalette::AlternateBase`, which `ThemeManager` sets to the window colour — so it was the desk's own grey, about 1.3 CIE76 units from the background around the page, and a person could not find it. It now paints from the `MessageBackground`/`MessageText` tokens, pushed in by `MainWindow` and re-pushed on every theme change, because no palette role means *a banner*. **Extended 2026-09-13 (Stage 2 I-31, I-32):** a sixth kind, `OpenFailed`, and the only one that is not a document condition — a refused file has no tab — so it is **window-scoped**: `clearDocumentConditions()`, which a tab change calls, leaves it, and it stays until dismissed. Painted red from `ErrorBackground`/`ErrorText`, so a refusal cannot be read as a condition of the document in front. **Extended at S3 · T10:** three document-scoped kinds — `SaveFailed` (painted in the failure colours, like `OpenFailed`), `SaveAsRequired` (the reason a Save became a Save-As: repaired, or changed on disk) and `RecoveryUnavailable` — stored on `DocumentWindow` as conditions and re-shown on a tab change. **S3 · T11:** `Recovered`, a document-scoped kind whose text is one line per fact of a replay, cleared by the document's first successful Save As or Save. | `core/doc/RepairReport`, `core/doc/Permissions`, `core/doc/FormModel`, `ui/shell/ThemeManager` |
| `ui/shell/ToolFinder.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S1 T11) | Fuzzy command search over the `ActionRegistry`, showing each match's chord and running it. **The ranking is a free function naming no widget** — `toolfinder::rank()` — which is what lets guide case **A-56** assert the ORDER (exact, then prefix, then word-start, then subsequence) without a window, the same split `RibbonModel` made at T10. **A declared-but-unregistered command ranks, appears, and is marked** rather than being hidden: the ribbon shows those commands greyed and a search that cannot find a button the user is looking at is a search they stop trusting. Each hit carries the chord that would actually fire, read from `KeyBindingStore` — defaults plus the user's remaps — and never from a list here. **Corrected 2026-08-31 (I-44):** the result list is a `Qt::Popup` window, which holds the mouse grab for the whole application while it is shown — so it must always be dismissible, and it was not. `Esc`, a press outside it, a failed `Enter`, `FocusOut` and `WindowDeactivate` all put it away now; before, a query matching nothing showed the *nothing matched* row, and every click in the application went to a popup that ignored it. **And the first version of that fix (I-45) dismissed on any `FocusOut`, which closed the list in the same turn it opened** — showing a popup IS a focus change — so `PopupFocusReason` is now excluded and `WindowDeactivate` is honoured only on the popup itself. Neither half is visible offscreen, which is why both are asserted as rules and re-walked by hand. **Corrected 2026-08-31 again (I-54), and this is the row's third visit from the same popup:** opening a `Qt::Popup` takes **two** things from the widget behind it and each has its own symptom. It takes the **keyboard**, so from the second character onwards every keystroke went to the `QListWidget` and was spent on its own search — the query stopped growing. And it takes the **window activation**, so `QLineEdit` stopped painting its caret, which is what the box looked like it had lost. `setFocusPolicy(Qt::NoFocus)` addressed neither and had carried a comment since T11 claiming it addressed the first. The list now forwards every key that is not its own (Up/Down/PageUp/PageDown/Enter) to the line edit, and carries `WA_ShowWithoutActivating` plus a focus proxy back to the box — **all three of the things `QCompleter` does with its popup**, which is what this widget is in all but name. Fixed in two rounds: the first did the keys only, and the user found the caret still missing by using the repaired build. | `core/cmd/ActionRegistry`, `core/cmd/KeyBindingStore` |
| `ui/shell/ActionFactory.{h,cpp}` + `RawPageAction.h` | ✅ (S1 T10) | **The only place in RawPage that constructs a `QAction`** — decision D1-7. `layer-lint`'s non-escapable `bare-qaction` rule allows the construction here and in `RawPageAction.h` (the subclass definition) and nowhere else, across four spellings with two controls (case A-12). The factory resolves the label from `ActionId.def` through the catalog, builds a declared-but-unregistered id **disabled with a tooltip saying so** rather than omitting it, and makes exactly one `triggered` connection — into `ActionRegistry::dispatch`. `wiringComplaints()` is the runtime half (A-54): it names a bare `QAction` and a second `connect` **separately**, because the lint catches the first at the moment it is written and structurally cannot see the second. Two things the first real run taught it (**I-18**, **I-20**): Qt manufactures intrinsic actions of its own — `QMenu::menuAction()`, `QDockWidget::toggleViewAction()` — and `QToolButton::setDefaultAction` adds a receiver, so the invariant is *every receiver accounted for*, not *exactly one*. **T11 taught it three more things.** A `QLineEdit`'s clear button is a QAction that is NOT in `QWidget::actions()` and so cannot be recognised by identity at all — `adoptWidgetActions()` lets the widget's owner stamp it at the instant the property is turned on (**I-22**). The factory had never called `setCheckable`, so its own promise that a toggle is checkable had gone untested for want of a toggle (**I-21**); it now asks `ActionRegistry::isToggle()`, and reads the registration's `tooltipKey` as well. And `addToMenu()`/`addMenuSeparator()` exist because `addAction(` is in the lint's pattern list with no exception for the overload that takes a built action: a caller asks here, one line instead of two, and the rule stays absolute with a single allowed file. | `core/cmd/ActionRegistry`, `ui/shell/Catalog` |
| `ui/shell/ActionToolButton.h` | ✅ (S2 · I-35) | **The button that shows a command's action — the ribbon's and the QAT's — without gaining a signal connection each time that action changes.** Qt 6.11.1's `QToolButton` re-applies its default action on every `ActionChanged` and connects `changed()` again with no check for the one it holds, measured at one connection per change for ever and 56 per document opened across the ribbon. Header-only and deliberately not `Q_OBJECT`: its meta-object, style rules and `findChildren<QToolButton*>()` are the base class's. Before Qt re-applies, it drops the button's own `changed()` connections, so each change leaves exactly the one Qt wants. Convention 23 (`Implementation-Plan.md` §1.5) makes it binding; guide case A-90 keeps a stock button as its control, so a Qt that stops re-connecting shows up as a failing CHECK and this file as removable. | `ui/shell/RibbonTabBuilder`, `ui/shell/QuickAccessToolbar`, `ui/shell/RawPageAction.h` |
| `ui/shell/ThemeManager.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S1 T9) | Light, dark, follow-system; palette tokens; the UI font scale. *(OS high-contrast deference removed 2026-08-23 with decision D1-15.)* **Fifteen closed colour ROLES** (eleven until the message-bar pair, Stage 2 I-29, and the error pair, I-32), resolved by one switch over the whole enum with no `default` — so under `ui/`'s `-Werror` a token added without a value in both themes does not compile, which is `ActionId.def`'s trick applied to colour. `FollowSystem` is a **request**, not a state: it resolves to Light or Dark through `QStyleHints::colorScheme()` and re-resolves live when the OS flips, which is what stops "the setting does nothing" being the first bug filed. The font scale is applied to a base point size captured once, so repeated changes do not compound. **Corrected 2026-09-27 (Stage 4 guide I-29):** "once" was once per `ThemeManager`, and every `MainWindow` owns one — so a second window at a scale of 1.5 took the first window's scaled font as its base (20.25 pt over 9) and 1.0 then left 13.5; the unscaled base is now recorded once per PROCESS, as a property on the application object, by the first `ThemeManager` constructed. **Corrected 2026-08-30 (I-40):** `disabledText` was chosen against a light mock and had never been judged in dark, where it measured 3.05 against the window and 2.62 against a pane — unreadable, and it is *most* of this shell's text while 339 of 358 commands are unregistered. Both halves raised (light measured 2.54 and was the worse of the two), and `tst_theme` now measures every foreground token against the background it is painted over, in both themes, as a **ratio with a floor** rather than as expected hex values. **Corrected again 2026-08-31 (I-41):** a palette was never enough — the native `windows11` style paints control text from the colour scheme it derives from Windows and ignores the application palette, so `apply()` also calls `QStyleHints::setColorScheme()`, with `Unknown` for follow-system and *before* `systemTheme()` reads the scheme back. Without it the theme reached every `QLabel` and no `QToolButton`. **Extended 2026-09-13 (Stage 2 I-29):** `MessageBackground` and `MessageText`, and a second kind of measurement beside the ratio — a BACKGROUND beside a background is judged by colour distance (CIE76, floor 10, `tests/ui/ColorDistance.h`), because a pale tint and a pale grey share a luminance and a contrast ratio cannot tell them apart. | `core/settings/Settings` |
| ~~`ui/shell/ReadAloudBar.{h,cpp}`~~ | ⤵ | **Withdrawn 2026-08-23** with Read Aloud — see [`Features.md` → *Removed from scope*](./Features.md#removed-from-scope--accessibility-2026-08-23). | — |
| `ui/shell/WindowArranger.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S4 · T11; S4 guide I-23, 2026-09-27, the cell is the frame)** | Tile horizontally/vertically, cascade, merge windows, the window list, and synchronized scrolling between two views of one document. **A cell is where a window's FRAME goes** (`placeFrame`, S4 guide **I-23**): `setGeometry` places a top-level window's contents and the title bar sits above them, so the margins `frameGeometry()` reports are removed from the cell first, after a maximized window is restored — and the cascade steps by the window's own title bar from the same margins rather than the style's metric. Until M-09's walk the cell was handed over as it was and the top window's title bar went off the screen; the one-logical-pixel border Windows 11 draws outside Qt's frame is bounded by guide case A-94, not removed. **It adds no `platform/` interface** (D4-17): placing our own top-level windows is `QWidget::setGeometry` over `QScreen::availableGeometry()`, which Qt does everywhere, and `Tech-Stack-Plan.md` §4.3's rule is that what Qt does cross-platform gets none. **Its arithmetic is pure and its operations are three lines each**, which is what lets guide case A-64 judge tiling and cascading over a synthetic work area rather than over whatever the offscreen platform reports for its one screen: `tileGeometry(workArea, count, orientation)` returns an exact, non-overlapping cover — the sizes are shared out with the remainder spread a pixel at a time, because edges computed as `i * total / parts` lose a pixel wherever the division is not whole and a one-pixel gap between tiled windows is visible across the room — and `cascadeGeometry` steps diagonally and restarts the run before a window would leave the work area. **`Orientation::Horizontal` is a horizontal DIVIDER** — full-width strips, one above another — the same coin flip `SplitViewContainer::Mode` records and the same answer, because two spellings of one word meaning opposite things inside one product is how a feature ships backwards and looks like it works. **The minimum forces a GRID rather than a refusal**: strips are added until one would fall below `kMinTileHeight`/`kMinTileWidth` and the surplus is laid across them; a work area too small to hold `count` cells at that size gets smaller cells, stated at the case rather than left to be discovered. Tiling and cascading run **per monitor**, over each screen's own `availableGeometry()`, so a person who put two windows on the second screen and pressed Tile gets them tiled there; both return early with fewer than two windows, which is A-64's control asserted on the geometry as well as on the command's refusal. **`mergeWindows()` targets the FIRST window in `MainWindow::shellWindows()`**, not the active one: *"five tabs in their original order"* only means something if the target is fixed, and creation order is also the one window `app/main.cpp` owns as a stack object and can never close. Each other window is emptied from the front by `releaseDocument`/`adoptDocument` — a MOVE, so every tab keeps its handle, journal and scroll position — and then `MainWindow::closeIfEmptied()` decides whether it goes. `mirrorScroll` mirrors between two views of one document in two windows, in **PDF points** exactly as the split's own mirror does, behind a file-scope echo guard — two windows each following the other is a loop with no fixed point, and unlike the split there is no single object on the path to hang a member on. → `tst_arranger`. | `ui/shell/MainWindow`, `ui/shell/DocumentWindow`, `ui/viewer/PageView`, `core/settings/SessionState` (T13) |

### 3.14 `ui/viewer/` — the page surface

| File | Status | Role | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ui/viewer/PageView.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S2 · T13, T14, T15; **S4 · T7 the tools, T8 the grid, the guides and the inversion; S4 guide I-11, 2026-09-26, the fit anchor and the reader's place; S4 guide I-19 and I-20, 2026-09-27, the frozen pane; S4 guide I-27, 2026-09-27, the modifier's release; S5 · T10 the snapshot; S5 · T11 `putPageRectAtTop` and `depthBelowTop`, S5 guide I-06; S5 · T13 the marks; S5 guide I-14, 2026-10-02, the double click; S5 guide I-15, 2026-10-02, the selection's tint**) | **Since S5 guide I-15** the selection is a constant blue MULTIPLIED onto the page (`selectionColour`, `selectionCompositionMode`) and SCREENED onto an inverted one, so the paper is tinted and the glyphs keep their ink -- the palette's translucent `Highlight` it replaced was 1.12:1 from white paper in the light theme. **Since S5 guide I-14** `mouseDoubleClickEvent` hands Qt's double click to `mousePressEvent`'s click counter -- `QAbstractScrollArea` ignores one, and Qt delivers every second click of a gesture as one, so a double click had selected nothing. **Marks since Stage 5 T13** (D5-18): `setPageMarks` -- rectangles in page space painted over the tiles, under the selection and the hits, each corner through the layout's own mapping and never rasterized (D4-11), the current one outlined -- and `widgetRectOf`, where a page rectangle is drawn; a comparison's differences on each of its two views. **`putPageRectAtTop` and `depthBelowTop` since Stage 5 T11** (S5 guide **I-06**, which replaced T11's `putPageHeightAtTop`): the first puts a page-space rectangle's top AS DRAWN -- its corner highest on screen -- exactly at the viewport's top edge, no history entry, Reflow view's way back to the page (D5-16 clarified (c)); the second says how far below that edge a page point is drawn, from which the way in reads the screen's down. A page-space HEIGHT is the screen's down only on an upright page. **Takes the SNAPSHOT since Stage 5 T10** (D5-11): `snapshotRegionFor(band)` -- the page the band overlaps most, clipped to it, refused when the part on the page is under the click threshold, at `SnapshotTool::resolve`'s scale, the request from `TilePresenter::requestAt` -- asked through its second `LoupeRaster`; the arrival is composited onto white, put on Qt's clipboard as an image, and reported as `snapshotCopied` (size, dpi, reduced and from what) or `snapshotFailed`, which `DocumentWindow` forwards and `MainWindow` words. Nothing it paints over the tiles is in the copy, because none of it is rasterized. `ViewSettings::snapshotDpi` carries the key. **Reads a key RELEASE as well as a press (S4 guide I-27)**, so letting go of the temporary hand's `Shift` restores the armed tool at once, with the released `Shift` counted as up and a pressed one as down whatever the platform reports on its own key. The scrolling page surface: paints tiles, hosts overlays, routes input to the active tool, and owns nothing about parsing. A `QAbstractScrollArea` and deliberately **not** a `QScrollArea`, whose widget-in-a-viewport model would mean laying out a 14-gigapixel widget at 6400 %. **It is the first widget in the product to call `ShortcutBinder::declareContext()`** (D2-8) — until T13, `activeContext()` answered `Global` for every keystroke the product had ever seen, so a regression here looks exactly like the previous fifteen tasks' correct behaviour. `controller()`, not `viewport()`: the base class owns that name. **Since S6 · T12** the measuring tools' host: `snapMeasured` (the page's paths from the window's `SnapTargets` when `units/snapToObjects` is on, then the annotations' sides, the guides and the grid), `measureSettings` (the `measuring/*` keys), `calibrateFrom` and `measurementMade` as signals; `annotationEdges` is the guides' object-edge source; a measuring tool places its points over annotations (`overlayFirst`, D6-39(h)). | `ui/viewer/TilePresenter`, `ui/viewer/ViewportController`, `ui/viewer/PageLayoutEngine`, `ui/shell/ShortcutBinder` **T14 adds the §8 Viewer operations and as much of the TOOL as Stage 2 owns**: `Features.md` §8 binds `H`, `V` and `Z`, and `Implementation-Plan.md` lists all three for this stage, while `ui/viewer/ToolController` and `ui/viewer/tools/` were ○ (S4). So this class held which tool was armed, its cursor and what a drag did. **S4 · T7 replaced all of that**: the controller and four tool classes now own arming, the cursor, the temporary-hand modifier, `Esc` cancellation and auto-scroll, and `PageView` is their `ToolHost` — it implements the four things a tool may reach and routes press/move/release to whichever tool is effective. Its Stage 2 `Tool` enum is GONE rather than aliased to `ToolKind`. Select is still the default and **still selects nothing until Stage 5 gives it text**. `paintEvent` was split into `paintContent` plus an overlay pass, so the same content can go either straight onto the viewport or into the pixmap the loupe magnifies — one painting path, two destinations. **T15 adds `ViewSettings`** — the eleven `pageDisplay/` preferences plus `performance/prefetchPages`, as a struct of plain values rather than a `Settings&`, which is the call `core/render/PresentationOptions` already made for the same reason. **`layoutMode` and `defaultZoom` are applied only on the FIRST call**, which is what makes them defaults rather than commands: a person who has switched this tab to Two-Up and then opens Preferences to change the anti-aliasing must not have their layout yanked back. **It also owns the boundary half of scrolling** (I-24, 2026-09-12): a paged layout holds one page, so the wheel and the arrow keys turn the page when the scroll is already at the edge, landing at the near edge of the new one. That is `Features.md` §1.2 and the §8 arrow row's *"paging at page boundaries"*, and it is the widget's rather than an `ActionId`'s because the arrows are `RAWPAGE_BINDING_NATIVE` rows — `NavNextPage` is the dispatchable form. **S3 · T10:** `setDocument` takes the model's page tree for layout AND the engine's for rendering, and `modelChanged(revision)` re-reads the mutated tree and refreshes — geometry first, then tiles (Stage 1's I-36, guide case A-75). **S4 · T8 gives it a `GridGuideLayer` and the page inversion**, both painted in `paintContent` over the tiles and neither reaching the scheduler (D4-11); the inversion is `RasterOp_NotDestination` over each page's SHEET rather than the viewport, so the desk stays itself and toggling twice restores the exact bytes. **A GUIDE IS PICKED UP BEFORE THE TOOL SEES THE PRESS**, and `Esc` cancels a guide drag before it cancels a tool's: every Stage 4 tool takes a press anywhere on the surface, so a tool-first order would make a guide ungrabbable with anything but the tool that does nothing until Stage 5. It emits `cursorMoved` for the rulers' marker — cleared on `QEvent::Leave`, caught in `viewportEvent` because Leave goes to the viewport — and `guideContextMenuRequested` when a right-click lands ON a guide, while a right-click anywhere else is passed to the base rather than swallowed (Stage 5's and Stage 6's menus want it). **EVERY FIT IS ANCHORED TO THE PAGE IT WAS CHOSEN ON** (`fitAnchorPage_`, S4 guide **I-11**): a fit is re-satisfied on every refresh, and measured against the current page it fed back into the layout that decides which page is current — T12 anchored Fit Visible alone, and on a document of mixed sizes Fit Width then drew the landscape page at twice the window's width. The anchor moves with a navigation and nothing else. **And a zoom COMMAND keeps the reader's place** (`capturePlace`/`restorePlace`: the viewport centre as a fraction of the current page), because `ViewportController::setZoom` keeps the raw device offset and every command but the wheel went through it. → `tst_properties` (A-81). **A FROZEN PANE of a spreadsheet split** (`setFrozen(body)`, S4 guide **I-19**, **I-20**) takes no focus, shows no scrollbar and keeps the body's scrollbar room empty (`setScrollBarReserve`), and its gestures are the BODY's before they move anything: `wheelEvent` re-sends the wheel to the body's viewport, and the tools are hosted by `ToolTarget` — this pane for what a tool measures or paints, the body's controller for what it scrolls or zooms — rather than by a second meaning of `controller()`, which the container and the shell read as this pane's own. → `tst_split` (A-91). **S5 · T5:** it PAINTS the document window's `SelectionOverlay` over the inversion and under the guides, in the theme's translucent highlight, and in the Loupe's sharp lens too; it counts clicks (the platform's double-click interval and drag distance, up to four) for the Selection tool; it answers `ToolHost::pagePointAt()` -- the nearest page, the point held inside it, for a drag over a gap -- and `pagePointOn()`; and `Esc` cancels a pending copy, then clears a selection, and with neither is still NOT consumed. **S5 · T7:** it paints the find bar's hits under the selection, amber and the current one orange -- the PAGE's colours rather than the theme's, because they are drawn on the document's paper in both themes -- and `revealPageRect()` scrolls the least it can to show a page rectangle, a third of the way down when it must move, and not at all when it is on screen. **S6 guide I-25 (2026-10-20):** `iconPointAt()` -- `pagePointAt()` with the icon a click places kept WHOLE on its page (`AnnotationOverlay::keepIconOnPage`); the note, pushpin and sound tools place by it, since a click on the desk arrives at a page's edge. |
| `ui/viewer/HeldRaster.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S4 · T5)** | A `core::RasterImage` as a `QImage` **together with the bytes that image borrows**, which is the whole of the type: `QImage`'s borrowing constructor does not copy, so returning a bare image over a `QByteArray` that then dies is a use-after-free that paints plausible rubbish rather than crashing. Its other rule is that **`bytesPerLine` is passed, never `width * 4`** — a rasterizer may align its rows (`core/backend.h` carries the stride for that reason, and the widget suite's fake pads deliberately so the assumption is never untested) and a padded buffer read as tight draws the page sheared into diagonal garbage. **Extracted from `TilePresenter` at T5 rather than copied into the pane**: both mistakes are silent, and a second copy is the shape convention 8 and D1-6 are about. A deep copy into an owning `QImage` was not taken — it would double the resident bytes of every tile on screen against the one 256 MiB budget **O7** keeps whole. | `core/backend.h`; used by `ui/viewer/TilePresenter` and `ui/panes/ThumbnailsPane` |
