| `ui/viewer/LoupeRaster.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S4 · D4-20, 2026-09-27)** | **The Loupe's SHARP lens** — the scheduler's third client, beside `TilePresenter` and the thumbnails pane: exactly one region of one page, at `presentedScale × LoupeTool::kMagnification`, in the `Visible` lane, **one request at a time** — `aim()` with a new region cancels the pending ticket and drops the held image before it enqueues — so a lens swept across a page can never build a queue in front of the viewport's tiles. No rasterizer, cache or thread of its own (D4-10's rule, met a second time), and the request is built by `TilePresenter::requestAt`, so the cache key is shaped in one place. `PageView` owns it, decides WHEN to ask (after `kLoupeSettleMs` of stillness), and composes the arrived raster with the inversion, grid and guides into the lens. Measured before it was accepted: guide case A-100, `bench_surface`'s `+ loupe` column, inside the budget and indistinguishable from the plain viewer. → `tst_tools` (A-99). **A second instance serves the Snapshot since Stage 5 T10** (D5-11 clarified): the same one-region, one-request shape and lane, constructed with **`CachePolicy::Bypass`** -- a snapshot is consumed once and may be 200 MB, so it never enters the tile budget -- and a **`failed()`** signal, because a snapshot that silently never arrives is a copy the person believes they made (the lens connects nothing to it). | `core/render/RenderScheduler`, `ui/viewer/HeldRaster`, `ui/viewer/TilePresenter`, `ui/viewer/PageView` |
| `ui/viewer/ReflowView.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S5 · T11; S5 guide A-129, 2026-10-04, `Ctrl`+wheel)** — a row added with the file (`Stage5-TextSearch-Plan.md` §4, D5-16) | **Since 2026-10-04 `Ctrl` and the wheel step its text size** (the user's request in M-09's walk, S5 guide A-129): `wheelEvent` turns each notch into a `zoomIn`/`zoomOut`, counted across events; the text browser's own `Ctrl`+wheel had zoomed only its default font, which every run overrides. → `tests/ui/tst_reflow` (A-129). **Reflow view, `Ctrl+4`**: a read-only `QTextBrowser` built with `QTextCursor` from `core/text/ReflowText`'s paragraphs, each text block carrying its (page, block) as user data so the reader's place is read without searching text, drawn in Qt's fonts at the model's size × the reflow's own text scale. **A window of pages** round the place -- two before, six after -- grown by six as the reader scrolls to an edge and by itself while less than a screen follows the place (capped at 48 pages), pages read from `core/text/DocumentIndex` at `Visible` and rebuilt from what has arrived on a 30 ms coalescing timer, the place kept across every rebuild, resize and zoom. **It asks for no raster.** Declares the `Viewer` context, so `Ctrl+4` and the zoom chords reach it (`MainWindow` asks `DocumentWindow::isReflowing()`), and CLAIMS the reading keys through `ShortcutOverride`. Owned by `DocumentWindow`, which hides the page surface for it and back (`setReflowing`, `PageView::depthBelowTop` on the way in, `blockBox` -- the box from the reflow's own text, not the index's -- and `PageView::putPageRectAtTop` on the way back, S5 guide I-06). → `tst_reflow`, `tests/composition/tst_reflowink` (A-92 … A-94). | `core/text/ReflowText`, `core/text/DocumentIndex`, `ui/shell/DocumentWindow`, `ui/shell/ShortcutBinder` |
| `ui/viewer/TilePresenter.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S2 · T13; S4 · D4-20, `requestAt`; **S6 · T5 the hold**) | **Since S6 · T5 (D6-2's *nothing flashes*) a new revision of the same document HOLDS what is on screen**: every held tile becomes stale rather than dropped, and is painted under the fresh ones while its twin -- the same tile at the new revision -- is on its way; it goes when the twin arrives or is no longer wanted, and a stale tile whose twin is never asked for (a page a rotation resized) is never painted. Until then an edit dropped every tile and the page was a white sheet until the cache or the engine refilled it; measured at T5, the first paint after an edit is the paint before it to the pixel (`tst_annotink`). `PaintStats::staleDrawn`, `staleTileCount()`. **`requestAt` is public since D4-20**: a request for any rectangle at any device scale, shaped as this presenter's own — revision, flags, view rotation, unsaved-rotation delta — for the Loupe's sharp lens, so there is one request builder rather than two. Asks the scheduler for the visible and prefetch tiles, paints what has arrived, shows the page's own sheet for what has not, and cancels what scrolled away. It was **the only place in the product where a `core::RasterImage` becomes a `QImage`** from T13 until **S4 · T5 gave the engine boundary a second consumer** — `ui/panes/ThumbnailsPane` — at which point the conversion moved into `ui/viewer/HeldRaster` rather than being copied; this class stores the pair that returns and never hands the image out on its own. Eviction is driven by `TileGrid`'s own `evict` set rather than by a heuristic. **`platform::IAcceleratedSurface` is NOT wired** — software presentation only. T16 re-tagged its row ○ (S2) → ○ (S4) rather than building it, and **Stage 4 T2 answered it on 2026-09-19: not built**, because the measured cost is PDFium rasterizing behind the engine lock and a surface presents pixels rather than making them (`Tech-Stack-Plan.md` §3.3, *A2 at Stage 4*). A null there is the honest state and now a settled one. | `core/render/RenderScheduler`, `core/render/TileGrid`, `core/render/RenderCache` **T15 gives it the render flags, the page prefetch and the transparency grid.** The flags are part of `RenderRequest`'s key, so changing them invalidates nothing — the new requests simply miss, and `PageView` drops the now-unreachable tiles explicitly. The prefetch counts PAGES and extends the SLOT list rather than the viewport rectangle: a viewport grown by a page's height covers a different number of pages in a document of mixed page sizes, which `corpus/ordinary/mixed-page-sizes.pdf` exists to be. The grid is a checkerboard UNDER the page — the raster is identical either way and what changes is what is behind it, which is why it is not a render flag. **S3 · T10 (D3-15):** given the engine's tree as well as the model's, every request carries `pageRotationDelta` = the model's rotation minus the engine's, so an unsaved rotation is drawn without the engine being touched. **S4 · T11 — the BACKGROUND LANE, and it is A2's named work** (`Tech-Stack-Plan.md` §3.3, *A2 at Stage 4*): `setBackground(true)` makes this presenter ask for its viewport at `RenderPriority::Prefetch` instead of `Visible`, so a window that is not the one in front can no longer take the single worker away from the one that is. Nothing else changes — the same tiles are asked for and they arrive, the request is byte-identical and so is its cache key — which is why the claim is asserted on `RenderScheduler::Stats`' per-lane counters and can be asserted nowhere else. It is **not** `Speculative`: that lane means *a page the user has not reached* (the thumbnails pane), and a background window's viewport is a page somebody is looking at, merely not the one in front. |
| `ui/viewer/PageLayoutEngine.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S2 · T13) | Realizes `PagePresentation` geometry for the current layout mode and zoom, caches it, and answers the fit questions — a fit needs the page's size and this is the object holding the `PageTree`. **It is where `platform::IDisplayTopology` finally has a consumer**: it resolves the three inputs `DpiPolicy` needs, joining the widget's `QScreen` to a `MonitorInfo` **by NAME**, because the two report geometry in different coordinate spaces on exactly the mixed-DPI desktop the join exists for. No match falls back to Qt's own DPI and then to the nominal `96 * scale`, and `ScreenMetrics::source` says which was used. **S4 · T12 — the fit arithmetic MOVED OUT of this file into `core/render/FitPolicy`** (D4-14), and what stays is what a view must own: supplying the three rectangles, applying the page's `/Rotate` composed with the session's view rotation, and handing the presentation's margin across. The move is what made Stage 2's I-28 assertable — its observable is a number read off a real file, and the only target that can open one links no widgets. It also gains a **content-box source**, a function the shell installs over `IDocumentBackend::contentBox` because `ui/` may never name `pdf/`; unset in a widget case that does not care, which then gets Fit Visible's CropBox fallback rather than a crash. The box is asked for ONLY when a fit wants it — Fit Width does not use the answer, and asking would load a page and walk its objects for nothing. | `core/render/PagePresentation`, `core/render/DpiPolicy`, `platform::IDisplayTopology` |
| `ui/viewer/ViewportController.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S2 · T13) | Zoom (steps, arbitrary, fit-page/width/height/visible, actual size), scroll, page snapping and the view history behind `Alt+←`/`Alt+→`. **It names no widget**, which is the whole reason it is a separate object: zoom-about-the-cursor, the scroll clamp and the history are arithmetic over four numbers, each with a failure invisible in a screenshot and exact in an assertion. A fit is stored as an INTENT and re-satisfied on every resize, never as a remembered number. Auto-scroll is Stage 4's. **S4 · T12 qualifies that for ONE mode**: Fit Visible's rectangle is the page's own ink, so its zoom differs page by page even in a uniform document — and a fit re-satisfied against the DOMINANT page closes a loop, since the zoom decides the layout, the layout decides which page is dominant, and the dominant page decides the zoom. `ui/viewer/PageView` therefore ANCHORS Fit Visible to the page it was set for and moves the anchor only on a navigation. It was measured rather than imagined: a `/FitB` destination to page 1 settled on page 4's fallback zoom. The other three fits are unchanged — on a uniform document every page gives them the same number, and on a mixed one following the reader's page is the point. | `core/render/DpiPolicy`, `ui/viewer/PageView`, `ui/shell/StatusBar` (T14) |

| `ui/viewer/RulerWidget.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S4 · T8; S4 guide I-17, 2026-09-26, the red marker and the cursor readout)** | Horizontal and vertical rulers with the unit selection, cursor tracking, and guide dragging. **The cursor readout is here** (`Features.md` §1.3, on the user's decision of 2026-09-26): a fixed-red, two-pixel (`kMarkerWidth`) marker at the pointer on each ruler with the reading beside it in the ruler's unit — `cursorReadout()`, asserted in both themes by A-88 — where the catalog had said the status bar and nothing had been built (I-17). **As built, and the distinction is the design:** a ruler measures what is on the SCREEN — distance from the page's drawn top-left corner along the screen axis — while a guide is a PAGE fact in PDF points (D4-7). Under a view rotation the page's x axis runs down the screen, and a ruler that insisted on reporting page x along the top would be measuring a direction the person is not pointing in; both are derived from the same `PageSlot`, so they agree. It holds no document, no layout and no controller: `ui/shell/DocumentWindow` recomputes a `Mapping` (logical pixels per point, the page's near edge, the page's extent) on every `viewChanged` — **which a scroll emits too** — and hands it over, so a case can drive a ruler with three numbers. Ticks come from a **1-2-5 ladder in the DISPLAY unit** rather than the traditional inch halving: one rule for five units instead of five tables, and the walk is by tick INDEX rather than by adding a step, because 5000 additions of 0.1 disagree with `readingAt` by the right-hand edge. It is CHROME, so every colour is from `QPalette` (conventions 20 and 21) — the opposite of the grid, which is painted over the page. **The drag off it is reported in GLOBAL coordinates**: Qt keeps the mouse grab on the widget the press landed on, and only the shell knows where the pages are. **A vertical label is rotated about the tick side of its strip, not x = 0** (S4 guide **I-13**, 2026-09-26): a quarter turn in Qt's y-down space runs the text box towards negative x, so anchored at 0 every number on the vertical ruler was drawn off the widget. **ONE pair per document window, measuring the focused pane of a split** — `ui/shell/DocumentWindow` rebinds it; decision **D4-18**. → `tst_rulers`, `tst_split` (A-84). | `core/Units.h`, `ui/viewer/GridGuideLayer`, `ui/shell/DocumentWindow` |

Its `snap()` takes a PAGE INDEX and a `core::PdfPoint` rather than the plan's bare `QPointF`, because a `QPointF` cannot say which space it is in — the single bug `core/Geometry.h` exists to prevent — and because the guides that participate are the ones on that page. **Everything is painted at PRESENTATION, over the tiles** (D4-11): composing a grid into the raster would put its spacing, colour and offset into the cache key, so dragging the spacing slider would re-rasterize the document; guide case A-50 asserts the scheduler's request count does not move. | `ui/viewer/SnapTargets.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S6 · T12)** — **a row the file manifest did not name**, added with the file | **A document's pages' PATHS, for the measuring tools to snap to** (D6-39(g)): the tab's ONE owner of its geometry requests to `core/text/DocumentIndex` -- `geometry(page)` answers what the index holds and asks at `Visible` for a page it does not -- `arrived` repainting the panes, and a page of more than `PageGeometry::kMaxSegments` segments said ONCE per page per document (`tooManySegments`). Owned by `ui/shell/DocumentWindow`, handed to every pane as the selection is. → `tests/ui/tst_measuretool` (`[A-91]`). | `core/text/DocumentIndex`; used by `ui/viewer/PageView` |
| `ui/viewer/SplitViewContainer.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S4 · T9; S4 guide I-12, 2026-09-26, the painted divider; S4 guide I-18, I-19 and I-20, 2026-09-27, the body leads the freeze)** | Horizontal, vertical, and spreadsheet (frozen row/column) splits over one document. **N `PageView`s over ONE `DocumentHandle`, ONE `Document`, ONE `CommandJournal` and ONE revision** (D4-13): it owns views and never a document, and `ui/shell/DocumentWindow` is what hands each pane the document, both settings groups and every new revision — a split that opened a second handle would need a second copy of that class's three-step close protocol and would double the document's memory to show the same pages. **`Mode::Horizontal` is a horizontal DIVIDER** — two panes one above the other, so it builds a `QSplitter(Qt::Vertical)` — which is the coin flip a reader gets backwards and a product that swapped them would look like it worked. Synchronized scrolling is an option on the container and is mirrored in **PDF points rather than device pixels**, so two panes at different zooms stay on the same place in the document; it synchronizes the SCROLL and not the zoom, because an overview beside a detail is the gesture it exists for. The spreadsheet split is a **constrained scroll**: the corner is frozen in both axes, the top pane follows the body in x only and the left pane in y only, all derived from the body rather than held separately. A retired pane is handed an EMPTY document — which cancels its tickets NOW — and then `deleteLater`'d, because `ShortcutBinder` dispatches from an application event filter and `window.removeSplit` runs while a key event is still being delivered to the very pane it destroys. The active pane is the FOCUSED one, which is what let all twenty-five §8 Viewer commands reach a split with no change to any of them. **S4 · T11 adds one signal, `scrolled(PageView*)`**, emitted after the echo guard and BEFORE the one-pane early return: its consumer is not the split but `ui/shell/WindowArranger`, whose commonest subject — two unsplit windows over one document — has one pane each. **EVERY DIVIDER PAINTS ITSELF** (S4 guide **I-12**): a stock handle is `QPalette::Window`, which is also the desk every `PageView` paints, so a split had no visible boundary. Each splitter is a file-local `DividerSplitter` whose `DividerHandle` draws a `Border` band with a `SubduedText` line, and `Accent` under the pointer, from the palette `ThemeManager` writes rather than through the style (convention 21). **IN A SPREADSHEET SPLIT THE BODY LEADS EVERYTHING** (S4 guide **I-18**, **I-19**, M-08's first walk): the frozen panes take the body's zoom — a fit as the NUMBER it produced — layout mode and view rotation on every change of it (`followBody`), the freeze is a `FreezeAnchor` (a page and a PDF point, plus the margin part in device pixels) so a zoom keeps the same header, a frozen pane takes no focus and shows no scrollbar with the body's scrollbar room kept empty beside it (`PageView::setFrozen`, `setScrollBarReserve`), its gestures — the wheel, the hand, `Ctrl`+wheel — are the body's before they move anything (`PageView`, S4 guide **I-20**, which replaced a first fix that measured the frozen pane's movement afterwards), and whatever else moves one is undone (`restoreFrozen`). → `tst_split`, `tst_arranger`. | `ui/viewer/PageView`, `ui/shell/DocumentWindow` |
| `ui/viewer/FullScreenController.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S4 · T10)** | Full-screen and presentation mode: transitions, auto-advance, blank-to-black/white, the laser pointer, and monitor choice. **ONE controller with TWO LEVELS** (D4-12) — level 1 is the window with no chrome, keeping the viewer's tools and `KeyContext::Viewer`; level 2 is its own surface on a chosen monitor, declaring `KeyContext::FullScreen`. Two controllers would mean two answers to *“is the screen saver allowed to start”*, and it is **`IPowerSession::setPresentationModeActive`'s first caller in the product** — declared at S2 T12 and called by nothing until now. **The `false` on exit is structural rather than a matched pair of calls**: `applyPowerState()` has ONE caller, `setLevel()`, which is the only writer of the level and applies the power state BEFORE any work that could throw — so an exception, a window close, a topology change and the destructor all pair it, which is the S1 a presentation most plausibly ships (guide case A-58, asserted as a CALL SEQUENCE because a controller that asked twice and released once ends inactive and is still leaking). The monitor is joined to `QScreen` by **`friendlyName`, never `MonitorInfo::id`** (S2's **I-20**), and a topology change MOVES the presentation to the primary rather than leaving it on a display that is gone — a presentation nobody can see is one nobody can press `Esc` in. Per-page `/Trans` and `/Dur` OVERRIDE `fullScreen/transition` and `fullScreen/autoAdvanceSec`, because a preference cannot know what the author intended for one page. It constructs no `Command` and A-63 asserts the file's hash and the journal after a full pass. **A MOVE HIDES, PLACES AND SHOWS** (`moveSurfaceTo`, S4 guide **I-14**, 2026-09-26): a full-screen surface re-targeted in place onto a monitor of another scale kept its physical size, so `Ctrl+Shift+M` and the topology path now take the first placement's path. **`fullScreen/monitor` governs the PRESENTATION only**; `F11` is the window's own full screen on the monitor it is on. → `tst_presentation` (A-85 needs two monitors at different scales and skips under `ctest`). | `platform::IPowerSession`, `platform::IDisplayTopology`, `core/settings/Settings`, `ui/viewer/PresentationSurface` |
| `ui/viewer/PresentationSurface.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S4 · T10)** | ⚠️ **A row the file manifest did not name**, added with the file on the precedent T8 set for `core/Snap`. The WINDOW a presentation is drawn in, split from the controller because that is a state machine and this is a widget — which is what lets a case drive every level, exit path and power pairing with the window never shown. A `PageView` over the SAME `DocumentHandle` the tab holds (D4-13's *N views, one document*, reaching its second consumer after the split) plus an overlay child for everything painted OVER the page: the blank, the laser, and the transition. The overlay is a child widget rather than a paint inside `PageView` so none of it can reach the scheduler or a tile — D4-11's argument at a third subject after the grid and the inversion. **A transition animates a CAPTURED FRAME** rather than re-rendering both pages every frame, the same argument `LoupeTool` makes, and what that costs is stated at the class: the outgoing page is frozen at the resolution it was drawn at. It is a real top-level `Qt::Window` so it can be placed on another display, and it is PARENTED to the main window so `ShortcutBinder` can see its focus at all. | `ui/viewer/PageView`, `ui/viewer/FullScreenController` |
| `ui/viewer/ComparisonView.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S5 · T13; S5 · T14 the status line; S5 guide I-24, 2026-10-04, the overlay's zoom and scroll; A-128 the same day, its continuous mode)** | **Since 2026-10-04 the overlay zooms and scrolls** (Stage 5 guide I-24, the user's design): its canvas is a scroll area's widget with a fit and a zoom of its own (`Ctrl+0`/`Ctrl+1`/`Ctrl+2`, `Ctrl+=`/`Ctrl+-`, `Ctrl`+wheel about the cursor); the wheel and the reading keys scroll the pair and turn to the next or previous one at its edge, the wheel after a whole notch there; only the region in view, with half a viewport round it, is rasterized, the composite placed at `overlayOrigin()`; a stepped-to difference is brought into view. → `tests/composition/tst_comparisonink` (A-127). **And a *Continuous* switch** (the user's request, the same day, A-128), on the bar and shown only in Overlay: every pair one under another in one scroll, the current pair the one at the top, nothing to turn at an edge. Each pair near the view is an `OverlaySlot` (its two `LoupeRaster`s, its region, its composite), dropped when it leaves; one pair at a time is the same with one pair laid out. **Since S5 · T14 its bar's status line takes no width from its text** (Stage 5 guide I-11): the run's growing progress line had widened the window five times in one comparison of `text-900.pdf`, re-fitting and re-rasterizing both pages each time. Two documents compared (D5-18): **a window of its own, owned by the main window -- not a tab and not a `SplitViewContainer` mode** -- over TWO `DocumentHandle`s it opened and closes by the three-step protocol, two `PageView`s with their `TilePresenter`s, and no journal, so nothing in it can construct a `Command` (A-107). **Side by side, scrolled by ALIGNED page**: either side leading, the other is put at the partner of the page at the leader's top edge, the same fraction down it; a page with none holds the other at the top of the next page that has one; partners by position until the pages are aligned (A-104). **The overlay**: one aligned pair at a time, both pages rasterized at the canvas's scale and composited per pixel as (newer, min(older, newer), older) in grey levels -- shared ink grey, older-only RED, newer-only BLUE, the side-by-side marks' colours (A-105). **The differences** in aligned-pair order, top to bottom, listed in `DifferencesPane` and stepped by `Ctrl+]`/`Ctrl+[` in both views, wrapping with a message (A-106). **What runs where**: the text through `DocumentIndex` at `Background`, its layout analysis on `ComparisonRun`'s thread; at *appearance* the 72 dpi rasters asked FROM that thread, posted here and enqueued at `Background` with `CachePolicy::Bypass` (D5-4). **The keys**: it declares the Viewer context and claims every key through `ShortcutBinder`'s first refusal except the three comparison chords and the Global table -- every other Viewer and Common command acts on the tab in front, so `Ctrl+R` or `Ctrl+Z` here would have reached the document behind it -- implementing the reading keys and the zoom and fit chords itself on both sides. **Its bar IS the four `documents/compare*` keys** (D5-14's rule); a change to any re-runs the comparison over the text already read. No rulers, grid or guides. → `tests/ui/tst_comparison`, `tests/composition/tst_comparisonink`. | `core/text/DocumentComparer`, `core/text/DocumentIndex`, `core/render/RenderScheduler`, `core/settings/Settings`, `ui/viewer/PageView`, `ui/viewer/LoupeRaster` (the overlay's two rasters), `ui/panes/DifferencesPane`, `ui/shell/ShortcutBinder` |

### 3.15 `ui/panes/`, `ui/dialogs/`, `ui/write/`

| File | Status | Role | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ui/panes/ThumbnailsPane.{h,cpp}` | 🟡 **(S4 · T5 navigate ✅, S7 page ops)** | Page thumbnails: navigation, multi-select, drag-reorder, cross-document drag, size control, and page-op context actions. `KeyContext::PageThumbnails` — **and it is the first widget in the product to declare a pane context**, which is how `Ctrl+R` reaches Stage 3's `page.rotate` with nothing in the file mentioning rotation (D4-1: `mutation-lint` still reports one command). **As built at T5: it is a VIEWPORT over the page set, not a view of it** (D4-10) — a 900-page document costs the visible slots plus **one screen of margin**, every request at `RenderPriority::Speculative`, through the shipped `RenderScheduler` and the shared `RenderCache`: **no second rasterizer, no second cache, no second thread**, and this is that priority's first product caller since Stage 2 declared it. A request is the whole page at thumbnail scale with `flags = None` and D3-15's rotation delta; `deviceScale` is in the key, so a size change re-keys rather than scaling what is held. **A pane nobody can see asks for nothing** — an auto-hidden or closed sidebar cancels every ticket. **Selection follows the view and the view does not follow it back:** `activated()` is emitted only by a GESTURE and `setCurrentPage()` emits nothing, so the two cannot chase each other without a flag whose window could be missed. **Single selection, deliberately** (convention 19): §5.2 asks for multi-select and every operation that would read a page SET is Stage 7's, so a selection with nothing to act on would be I-11 built by choice. **Still owed:** multi-select, drag-reorder, cross-document drag and the context actions, all S7 with the commands they need. → `tst_panes`. | `core/doc/PageTree`, `core/render/RenderScheduler`, `ui/viewer/HeldRaster`, `core/cmd/commands/PageCommands` |
| `ui/panes/BookmarksPane.{h,cpp}` | 🟡 **(S4 · T6 navigate ✅, S7 edit)** | Bookmark tree: navigate, rename, re-parent by drag, expand/collapse, and the generators. `KeyContext::Bookmarks`. **As built at T6: navigation only** — of §8's thirteen Bookmarks rows it services **five** (`Enter`, `→`, `←`, `*`, `/`) and the eight editing rows stay unregistered, so each leaves its key UNCONSUMED rather than reaching a handler that does nothing (Stage 2's **I-11** is what the other reading costs). A `QTreeWidget`, because a bookmark tree is an item view — expansion state, keyboard walking, elision — and inline editing is switched OFF explicitly, since a rename that changed only the widget would be a bookmark the file does not have. **The same table drives three different behaviours and none is decided here**: `→`/`←` are registered commands in this context (they are `WidgetNative` in `PageThumbnails`), `↑`/`↓` appear in no Bookmarks row and reach the tree, and a MODIFIED arrow is ignored outright — `Alt+↑`/`Alt+↓` are Stage 7's reorder and `QAbstractItemView` would otherwise walk the selection on them (**I-04**, met here a second time). It shows the file's own open/closed state from `OutlineItem::openByDefault` rather than expanding everything, and a bookmark whose destination is unresolved is shown greyed rather than dropped. **Still owed:** the editing half, S7. → `tst_panes`. | `core/doc/Outline`, `core/cmd/commands/BookmarkCommands` |
| `ui/panes/CommentsPane.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S6 · T9) | The comment list (D6-12, D6-36): **a view of `AnnotationStore::grouped`** through an item model of its own (a row per comment and per reply, each carrying its annotation's IDENTITY -- guide I-13 -- built only for the rows shown, so 9,000 comments regroup in ~26 ms at p95, A-73), grouped by page, author, day, type, status or checkmark, sorted, filtered by author, type, status, the current identity's checkmark and a date range, and searched -- a thread found by any reply's text; threads open. It constructs `ReplyToAnnotationCommand`, `SetAnnotationStatusCommand`, `EditAnnotationCommand` and `DeleteAnnotationCommand` and nothing else, through the runner the window gives it (the tab's journal). §8's Comments table through `KeyContext::Comments`: `Enter` asks for the comment's own editor (the window's properties bar), `F2` and `Ctrl+Enter` an `AnnotationTextEditor` below the list, `Space` the checkmark, `Ctrl+Shift+K` the five states, `Ctrl+F` its search field, `Ctrl+.`/`Ctrl+,` step and wrap, `Delete` the comment with its thread. The page follows a GESTURE -- not the list taking the focus (guide I-15); sequence numbers for the overlay; rebuilt only while shown or numbering. **No summary launcher** -- the summary is Stage 8's (Q4, D6-19). → `tst_commentspane`, `tst_commentscale`. | `core/doc/AnnotationStore`, `core/cmd/commands/AnnotationCommands`, `ui/viewer/AnnotationTextEditor`; wired by `ui/shell/MainWindow` |
| `ui/panes/FieldsPane.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S9) | Field tree, tab-order editing, calculation-order editing, and field selection. `KeyContext::FormsDesign`. | `core/doc/FormModel` |
| `ui/panes/LayersPane.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S7) | Layer tree: visibility, lock, rename, merge, flatten, and per-usage visibility. | `core/doc/LayerTree` |
| `ui/panes/TagsPane.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S7) | Tag tree editing and the logical-order overlay. *(Alt text and the accessibility-check report removed 2026-08-23.)* | `core/doc/TagTree` |
| `ui/panes/AttachmentsPane.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S6 · T11)** | Embedded files: list, open, extract, add, delete, with the trust warning. **As built** (D6-17, D6-38): BOTH kinds in one list -- the document's files, then every listed `FileAttachment`'s with its page -- read from the JOURNAL'S document, so an add or a delete shows at once; its four buttons, a double click and `Enter` ask the window for a COMMAND (`attachment.open`, `.extract`, `.add`, `.delete`, minted chordless), and `MainWindow` asks, refuses and runs -- the executable refusal and the question before a non-PDF opens are the window's, through `MainWindow::AttachmentAnswers` in a case. A find hit selects its file (`selectByName`, D6-11). **Its buttons say one word and give way** (guide I-16): four action labels side by side widened a 1400-pixel window to 1892. | `core/doc/Attachments`, `core/security/TrustPolicy` |
| `ui/panes/SignaturesPane.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S11) | Signature list with status, details, certificate viewer, view-signed-version, and compare-versions. | `core/security/ValidationReport` |
| `ui/panes/SearchPane.{h,cpp}` | 🟡 **(S5 · T9 folder ✅; S5 guide I-18 … I-20, 2026-10-03, the paths, the count and the options; I-21 and I-22 the same day, `Enter`, *Clear* and the refine chain; the edge line, the user's request; S16 catalog index and portfolios)** | Advanced search: scope (document / folder / index / portfolio), all the toggles, the result list, sort, filter, and refine. **Real as of S5 · T9, the FOLDER** (D5-13): a dock of its own along the right, like the log pane, shown by `Ctrl+Shift+F` (`find.advancedSearch`, §8's Global row since D5-15) with the focus in its query; it declares `KeyContext::FindSearch` (the context this row once called `Search`), and with the focus in it `Enter` searches or opens the current result, `F3`/`Shift+F3` step the list and `Esc` hides it -- `MainWindow` routes those four commands to the pane before the find bar. A folder, recursive or not, the query's flags, content classes and bound from `SearchQuery::fromSettings`; the work is `core/text/FolderSearch` and the list `core/text/SearchResults` -- streamed per file, sorted by relevance, page or file, filtered by author, subject and a date range, refined, the files not searched listed after the hits with their reason -- the snippet's match drawn bold. Activating a result asks `MainWindow::openSearchResult`, which opens through `openFiles` (an open file is not opened twice) and shows the file's hits in that tab's find bar. **The export, `exportTo`, holds the stage's one planned filesystem-write waiver** -- a `QFile` opened `WriteOnly | Truncate` on the waived line, because `#include <QSaveFile>` would itself be a second match; the button asks for a path only where somebody can answer. **Since S5 guide I-18 … I-20 (2026-10-03, raised by M-07's walk):** the *File* column cuts a long path in the middle (`PathDelegate`, `Qt::ElideMiddle`), so the drive and the file name stay; the count names the files the listed results are in, with the files searched as a figure of its own (`search.pane.searched`); and the pane shows the find bar's three option buttons, each dispatching its command and following the setting, a changed option re-running the last search and a hidden pane searching when it is shown. **Since S5 guide I-21 and I-22 (the user's design, 2026-10-03):** `Enter` (`jumpToCurrent`) narrows a list that exists -- the filters read, a changed query refining it -- and searches when there is none or the scope changed; it opens a result only from the list, and does nothing on a control; *Clear* empties the list and keeps the fields; the list keeps the chain of queries it was built from, names it on the status line, and opens a result under the last of them. **And at the user's request (2026-10-03, A-123)** the pane draws the edge it is docked against, because the main window's dock separator is drawn in the window's own colour and showed nowhere to grab -- since 2026-10-04 through `ui/shell/DockEdge`, shared with the sidebar and the log pane. **Still owed:** the catalog index and portfolio scopes (S16, R2). | `core/text/FolderSearch`, `core/text/SearchResults`, `core/text/SearchQuery`, `ui/shell/MainWindow` |
| `ui/panes/DestinationsPane.{h,cpp}` | 🟡 **(S4 · T6 read ✅, S7 edit)** — **re-tagged ○ (S7) → ○ (S4 read, S7 edit) on 2026-09-18** (`Stage4-ViewingSurface-Plan.md` D4-3: `Implementation-Plan.md` § Stage 4 had always listed a read-only `DestinationsPane` among this stage's components while this row said S7 — the pane is navigation, and navigation is Stage 4) | The named destinations, listed and navigable. **As built at T6:** a two-column `QTreeWidget` — name and page — **sortable by either, with the page column sorting as a NUMBER** (the default lexicographic compare puts page 10 before page 2, which looks right on a nine-page document and is wrong on every larger one), and an explicit default of **by name, ascending**, because `setSortingEnabled` alone inherits whatever indicator the header carries and because `/Names /Dests` is a name tree and already lexically ordered. **An unresolvable destination is LISTED and marked, never dropped** — `corpus/ordinary/dests-named.pdf` carries one deliberately as T1's control — it is not selectable, it does not activate, and it sorts last rather than as page 0. **It declares no `KeyContext`**, and that is `Features.md` §8's doing rather than an omission: §8 has no Destinations table, `KeyContext.h`'s list is exactly §8's tables, and a context here would be one nothing could bind to — so `Enter` is the widget's. For the same reason **`pane.destinations` has no default chord** and is reached by dispatch. **Still owed:** create, rename, delete and the repair after pages move, all S7. **I-05 — the destination's FIT carried and not applied — was closed at T12** (D4-14): `ui/viewer/PageView::goToDestination` applies `/XYZ`'s point and zoom, `/FitH`'s top, `/FitV`'s left, `/Fit` and `/FitB`, with a null meaning *keep what you have*; `/FitR`'s rectangle is the one thing still unread, because `core::Destination` has no fields for it. → `tst_panes`, `tst_properties`. | `core/doc/Destinations`, `ui/shell/SidebarDock` |
| `ui/panes/DocumentMapPane.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S13) | Heading-outline navigation for Write mode. | `core/reflow/ReflowDocument` |
| `ui/panes/StampPalettePane.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S6 · T10)** | Stamp browsing by category, favourites, and custom-stamp creation. **A dock along the right** (D6-37(h)), as Advanced Search is -- so no pane id: `Ctrl+Alt+S` arms the stamp tool and shows it. One tree: Favourites (built-in or custom), Standard (Table 181's fourteen), Dynamic (five), Signatures and each of the person's categories; a built-in named in the person's language (`stamps.name.<Name>`) beside a preview of the words it draws. Choosing one makes it the stamp tool's (`ui::setCurrentStamp`) and arms the tool -- a custom stamp's image read and decoded ONCE, on the choice; favour, refile and delete write the library at once; *Custom stamp…* asks the window for the dialog (`annot.customStamp`). A window composed with no library lists the built-ins alone. → `tests/ui/tst_stamps`. | `core/settings/ProfileStore`, `ui/viewer/tools/StampTool` |
| `ui/panes/DifferencesPane.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S5 · T13)** | A comparison's differences: one row per difference -- its kind with the header or footer band it is in, the older and the newer page, the words before and after -- under a line counting them by kind. **The summary at Stage 5 is this list** (D5-17, Q4); **the report export the row named is Stage 8's**, which writes a new document, and this pane writes nothing. It shows the order it is given -- `ComparisonView`'s, the order `Ctrl+]` steps -- so a row's index is that difference's: a row CHOSEN (a click, the arrows, `Enter`) says so and the view goes there, and the view going elsewhere selects the row without saying anything back. It lives in the comparison's window, not the sidebar, whose panes are views of the tab in front. → `tests/ui/tst_comparison`, `tests/composition/tst_comparisonink` (A-106). | `core/text/DocumentComparer`, `ui/shell/Catalog` |
| `ui/panes/LogPane.{h,cpp}` | 🟡 (S1 T14; S5 guide A-124, 2026-10-04, the edge facing the page, `ui/shell/DockEdge`) | Filterable in-app log viewer and the "collect diagnostics" action. **Real as of T14:** a bottom dock, hidden at startup and toggled by `pane.log` as a checkable command, showing `core::LogBuffer` in a read-only monospaced text view with a level filter, a subsystem filter, copy and clear. **It is a bottom dock rather than a `SidebarDock` pane** — every other file in this section is a view of a DOCUMENT and the log is a view of the application. Its filters can only **narrow**: it is fed from inside the sink that has already applied `logging/level` and `logging/subsystems`, and it says so on screen so that "the filter is broken" reads as "change the setting". A live record is marshalled to the UI thread, because from Stage 2 records arrive on render workers. **Its read-only view did NOT keep `Ctrl+C` and `Ctrl+A` by being a text widget**, as its header had said since T14: it lost both to the document once Stage 5 T5 registered `edit.copy` and `edit.selectAll` (S5 guide I-04), and `ShortcutBinder` now leaves them to it; `textView()` is the accessor guide A-52 presses them in. **Still owed:** "collect diagnostics", which bundles the log, the settings and the environment — Stage 14, with `diagnostics.bundle`. | `core/Log.h`, `core/LogBuffer` |
| `ui/dialogs/PasswordDialog.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S2 · T14)** — **a row this stage's manifest did not list**, added with the file | The prompt an encrypted document raises, and the `core::PasswordCallback` `IDocumentBackend::open()` takes. **A dialog where the protected banner is a bar**, because they answer different questions at different moments: the prompt is a QUESTION the engine asks mid-open and cannot continue without, so it is modal by necessity rather than by preference; the banner is a STATEMENT about a document that is already open. `attempt` is 1-based (`core/backend.h`) and the second ask says something different, because a prompt that repeats itself reads as one that is not registering the typing. **It refuses to raise itself where there is nobody to ask** — Stage 1's I-23: a modal `exec()` under the offscreen platform is shown to nobody and never returns. An empty password is still an answer and is not the same as cancelling. | `core/backend.h`, `ui/shell/MainWindow` |
| `ui/dialogs/PreferencesDialog.{h,cpp}` | 🟡 (S1 T14 shell, **12 of 26 pages as of S6 · T12** (Identity T6, Commenting T8, Measuring T12), grows per stage) | The preference pages of [`Features.md` §9.1](./Features.md#91-preferences-groups), driven by `SettingsSchema` so a new key needs no new widget code; shows policy locks; includes the Shortcuts page. **Real as of S2 · T15:** a page list over a stack, with **five** of the 26 groups — General, Page Display, Performance and Cache, Logging and Shortcuts (three at T14; Page Display and Performance and Cache cost two lines in `shippedPages()` and no widget code). `shippedPages()` is the one place a page is added; everything else follows from it and from `SettingsKeys.def`. **MODELESS, applying immediately, with Close and Restore Defaults and no OK/Cancel/Apply** — a `Cancel` that must un-apply needs a transaction `core::Settings` deliberately does not have, and modeless is also what lets `file.preferences` dispatch headlessly with no enablement guard (contrast `file.open`, issue I-23). **S3 · T12 adds Documents**, second in the list — `documents/recoveryDir` and `documents/warnOnRepairedSave` (D3-9) beside Stage 1's `recentCount` and `recentPinned` — so six of the 26 groups ship, and the header's page rationale, stale since S2 · T15, was corrected. **S4 · T8 adds Units & Guides** — the group has existed in `SettingsGroup` since Stage 1 with NO keys, and the eleven `units/*` rows fill it; one line in `shippedPages()` and no widget code, so seven of 26 groups shipped. **S4 · T10 adds Full Screen** on the same terms — one line, five `fullScreen/*` rows, no widget code — so **eight of 26 groups ship** (re-derived from `shippedPages()` 2026-09-23). **Still owed:** the other 18 groups, preference search, export/import of the whole set, reset-all, and the policy-lock rendering — nothing locks anything until Stage 15's `PolicyOverlay`, so nothing renders as locked, which is the honest state rather than a stub that greys nothing. | `core/settings/Settings`, `core/settings/SettingsSchema`, `core/cmd/KeyBindingStore` **Eight of 26 groups ship as of S4 · T10**, read back from `shippedPages()` 2026-09-23 — General, Documents, Page Display, Units & Guides, Full Screen, Performance and Cache, Logging, Shortcuts; seven as of S4 · T8, six as of S3 · T12 and five as of S2 · T15. **26 is the group count excluding `SettingsGroup::Internal`**, which is not a page. Two rows in `shippedPages()` and sixteen in `SettingsKeys.def` were the whole change: no widget code per setting, and no second list saying which key belongs to which page. |
| `ui/dialogs/MeasurementScaleDialog.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S6 · T12)** — out of the dialog family's *S6* entry | **The measurement scale** (D6-39(i)): a length on the page in a paper unit and what it stands for in any of the nine units, reduced to the ratio, its words shown as `/R` will write them; or, to CALIBRATE, the length measured and what it is (`core::calibratedRatio`); the decimals. It collects and changes no document -- `MainWindow::askMeasurementScale` writes the three keys. → `tests/ui/tst_measuretool`. | `core/Measure` |
| `ui/dialogs/ExchangeReportDialog.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S6 · T13)** — **a row the file manifest did not name**, added with the file | **What an import or a migration did, comment by comment** (D6-20, D6-21): each comment's type, page, outcome -- added, replaced, kept, not imported; placed, not placed -- and the reason for each one not brought over, worded from its `error.` key. Modeless; it asks nothing and constructs no command. Shown where a person can see it -- never offscreen -- and the window keeps the report it shows (`lastExchangeReport`). → `tests/ui/tst_exchange`, `tests/composition/tst_exchangeproduct`. | `ui/shell/Catalog` |
| `ui/dialogs/ToolDefaultsEditor.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S6 · T8)** — **a row the file manifest did not name**, added with the file | **The Commenting page's editor of each tool's defaults** (D6-13): one tool chosen and an editor for each property, enabled FROM THE TABLE (`toolHasProperty`) so a property `ToolDefaults.def` gives a tool appears with no widget code; each change writes the tool's rows at once, as every page applies immediately; `Reset` takes one tool back to the table and the dialog's *Restore Defaults* every tool. Below the page's generated rows. → `tests/ui/tst_drawtools` (A-61). |
| `ui/dialogs/SettingsPage.{h,cpp}` | ✅ (S1 T14) | **One Preferences page, generated from `core/settings/SettingsKeys.def`.** It `#include`s that table itself with a macro that calls a TEMPLATE per row, so each editor holds the real `constexpr Key<T>` and calls the typed `get`/`set` — the walk is typed rather than over `SettingsSchema::allKeys()` precisely because a descriptor walk would need the string-keyed API D1-10 exists to forbid. Which widget a row wants comes from `SettingsEditorTraits` and what to call it from `settingsLabelKey`, both derived from the key's type and path; adding a preference in a later stage is a `.def` row and no widget code, which guide case A-90 asserts in **both** directions. | `core/settings/Settings`, `core/settings/SettingsSchema`, `ui/shell/Catalog` |
| `ui/dialogs/ShortcutsPage.{h,cpp}` | 🟡 (S1 T14) | The Shortcuts page: a `KeyContext` selector over a table of that context's bindings, a `QKeySequenceEdit` to capture a chord, assign and reset, and the class-1 **shadow** report beneath. It writes to **both** `KeyBindingStore` (so the chord works before the dialog is closed) and `Settings` (so it is there tomorrow); nothing is rebuilt, because `ShortcutBinder` resolves through the store on every press and holds no copy. Shadows are shown, never prevented — §8 ships six by design. **Still owed:** a conflict prompt when the chosen chord is already taken (today the store simply reassigns and the old row's source column says so), and export/import of a shortcut set. | `core/cmd/KeyBindingStore`, `core/settings/Settings`, `ui/shell/ShortcutBinder` |
| `ui/dialogs/RotatePagesDialog.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S3 · T10)** — D3-13 brought it forward from S7's `PageOpsDialogs` | What `Ctrl+R` opens: a direction (90° clockwise, 180°, 90° counter-clockwise) and a page set (the current page, or all pages) — **no range**, which is Stage 7's. It holds no document and no journal: it answers a `Choice`, and the free function `planRotation` turns a choice into one `RotatePageCommand` or one group of *n* (one undo step). Where nobody can answer (offscreen), `canAsk()` is false and `page.rotate` is disabled; `setAnswerForTesting` is the twin a headless case drives. | `core/cmd/commands/PageCommands`, `ui/shell/MainWindow` |
| `ui/dialogs/CustomStampDialog.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S6 · T10)** | **A custom stamp for the person's library** (D6-16, D6-37(i)): from an IMAGE FILE -- its browser lists image types only, and a file that is not an image, a PDF among them, is refused by name before anything can be made (A-78; a PDF page source is Stage 7's) -- a PNG or JPEG kept as given, any other image as a PNG of itself; or initials DRAWN on a pad (`InitialsPad`) or TYPED, each a PNG on a transparent ground filed under Signatures. It constructs NO command -- a library entry is not a document change -- and returns `Made`, which `MainWindow::addCustomStamp` writes to `core::ProfileStore`. → `tests/ui/tst_stamps`. | `core/settings/ProfileStore`, `ui/panes/StampPalettePane`, `ui/shell/MainWindow` |

OK needs two named files; whether each is a PDF is the backend's to say (A-47's rule). **Nobody to ask** (convention 16): `canAsk()` is false offscreen unless a test installed an answer, and `compare.documents` is then DISABLED; `setAnswerForTesting` is the parameterized twin. **Since 2026-10-04 (the user's request, S5 guide A-126) the newer field remembers**: where the older file was compared before, it defaults to the newer file it was last compared against -- `core/settings/ComparePairs`, asked through a function so the dialog holds no settings -- when that file still exists, and follows the older field until a person picks, types or browses to a newer one. → `tests/ui/tst_comparison` (A-103: the chord through the binder with an answer, and the widget built and driven, never executed; A-126: the remembered newer). | `core/text/DocumentComparer` (`CompareOptions`), `core/settings/ComparePairs`, `ui/shell/Catalog` |
| `ui/dialogs/UnsavedChangesDialog.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S3 · T10)** — D3-14 | Save / Don't Save / Cancel for a dirty document being closed; Escape and the close box are Cancel. It returns a `CloseDisposition` and does nothing with it — `MainWindow::closeDocument` does. `canAsk()` is false offscreen unless a test installed an answer, and a close with no answer is refused rather than blocked or silently discarded. | `ui/shell/MainWindow` |
| `ui/dialogs/RecoveryDialog.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S3 · T11)** — D3-11 | What a launch after a crash asks: one row per journal — the document's name, its path, and a sentence for its state — with **Recover** (labelled *Recover to a new file* when the source changed or the document was untitled), **Discard** and **Decide later**, and Continue / Decide Later for all. A source that is gone or a journal that is damaged offers no Recover. Nothing is thrown away by default. It is handed descriptions (`Offer`) and answers a `RecoveryDisposition` per row — no journal, no document, no window — so the report of a replay that stopped at step *k* is the recovered document's banner, because the dialog has closed before any replay starts. `canAsk()` is false offscreen unless a test installed an answer; `setAnswerForTesting` is the twin. | `app/RecoveryPrompt` |
| `ui/dialogs/DocumentPropertiesDialog.{h,cpp}` | ✅ **(S4 · T12)** — D4-4 | What `Ctrl+D` opens: the Info dictionary's eight fields, the PDF version, the page count, the current page's size, whether the document is password protected, whether its cross-reference table had to be reconstructed, and the file's path and size. **It collects nothing and constructs no command**, which is not an exception to the standing dialog rule but the other half of it — **Stage 4 constructs no `Command` at all** (D4-1), and editing the Info dictionary is Stage 8's with the `/ModDate` policy and the XMP writer that must stay consistent with it. Every value is a `QLabel`, selectable and never editable: a read-only `QLineEdit` looks like a field somebody forgot to enable. **No XMP** (D4-4). It holds a `Properties` struct of values assembled by `MainWindow` from the four places the facts live — `metadata()`, `documentId()`, `repairReport()` and the view's page tree — so a case with no document, no engine and no window can drive it; and the size and path are the identity taken at OPEN (D3-16), never a fresh `QFileInfo`, which would describe a file that may have changed since. A date that `Metadata::parseDate` refuses is shown VERBATIM rather than as an empty field or an invented substitute. → `tst_properties`. | `core/doc/Metadata`, `core/doc/DocumentId`, `ui/shell/MainWindow` |
| `ui/dialogs/*.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S2–S16) | The dialog family, one file each. *S2–S3:* ⤵ `RepairReportDialog` — **folded at S2 · T14**: a repair report's details are a `QMessageBox` raised by the message bar's *Details* action (`ui/shell/MessageBar.cpp`), which is all T14's banner needed and no file of its own; `RecoveryDialog` (its S3 · T11 row is above). *S4:* ⤴ `DocumentPropertiesDialog` — **built at S4 · T12 and its own row is above**; listed under S2–S3 until 2026-09-18 and corrected with `Stage4-ViewingSurface-Plan.md` (D4-4), which is where its model arrived; and ⤵ `GoToPageDialog` — **folded at S2 · T14**: *Go to Page* is `MainWindow::promptForPage`, a `QInputDialog` that collects the page number and hands it to the view, per the standing rule that a dialog collects parameters and nothing else. *(Both breadcrumbs recorded 2026-09-14, re-read against `ui/`.)* *S5:* ⤴ `CompareDialog` — **built at S5 · T13 and its own row is above**. *S6:* ⤴ `MeasurementScaleDialog` — **built at S6 · T12 and its own row is above**; ⤴ `CustomStampDialog` — **built at S6 · T10 and its own row is above**; `CommentSummaryDialog` moved to *S8* on 2026-10-04 (`Stage6-Annotations-Plan.md` Q4, D6-19). *S7:* `PageOpsDialogs` (insert/delete/extract/replace/split/crop/renumber/resize, and rotate's ranges — its S3 row is above), `MergeDialog`, `WatermarkDialog`, `HeaderFooterDialog`, `BatesDialog`, `ImpositionDialog`, `LinkPropertiesDialog`. *S8:* `CommentSummaryDialog` (from S6, Q4), `ExportDialog`, `OptimizerDialog`, `ConformanceReportDialog`, `FlattenDialog`, `PrintDialog`. *S9:* `FieldPropertiesDialog`, `TabOrderDialog`, `CalcOrderDialog`, `FormDataDialog`, `DuplicateFieldDialog`. *S10:* `SecurityDialog`, `SecuritySchemeDialog`, `SanitizeDialog`, `RedactionSetupDialog`, `TrustManagerPage`. *S11:* `SignDialog`, `SignatureAppearanceEditor`, `DigitalIdManager`, `TrustedIdentitiesDialog`, `ValidationPreferencesDialog`, `CertificateViewer`, `EnvelopeDialog`. *S12:* `NewDocumentDialog`, `CreateFromFilesDialog`, `ConversionProfileDialog`. *S13:* `ParagraphDialog`, `TabStopsDialog`, `PageSetupDialog`, `ConversionFidelityDialog`, `TableDialog`, `TocDialog`, `CaptionDialog`, `FootnoteDialog`, `ChartDialog`. *S14:* `BatchEditorDialog`, `BatchReportDialog`, `DiagnosticsDialog`. *S15:* `ActivationDialog`, `UpdateDialog`, `AboutDialog`, `ThirdPartyNoticesDialog`. *S16:* `ScanDialog`, `OcrDialog`, `PortfolioDialog`. Each collects parameters and constructs a `Command` or a service call — **none contains document logic**. | `core/cmd/commands/*`, `core/security/*`, `core/batch/*`, `core/create/*` |

| `ui/write/FindReplaceBar.{h,cpp}` | 🟡 **(S5 · T7 find ✅, T8 the content classes ✅, T9 `presentHits` ✅, T14 the collapse ✅; 2026-10-03 the field halved ✅; S13 replace)** | **Since 2026-10-03 the field takes a third of the room it shares with the status line**, half the two thirds it had, on the user's request (S5 guide A-116): the stretches are 1 : 2, the 200-pixel minimum unchanged. **Since S5 · T14 it never widens the window** (Stage 5 guide I-10): the toggles, *Highlight all*, the scope and the range are one container, in the row when the bar has room and RE-PARENTED into a popup behind a *More* button when it does not (the ribbon group's pattern -- the same widgets, so the toggles stay the settings), with `minimumSizeHint` the essentials in both states and a 16-pixel hysteresis; until then its minimum was its whole row and `Ctrl+F` widened a 900-pixel window to 1,102 on the `windows` platform. **Since S5 · T9 `presentHits`** shows hits found elsewhere -- an Advanced Search result opened in this tab -- as the bar's own: the query in its field, every hit highlighted, the chosen one current and revealed, nothing searched again. The find toolbar for both modes, with replace and wildcards available only in Write mode. **Since S5 · T8 it searches the four other content classes** the `search/include*` settings switch on, and a hit navigates as D5-12 says -- a comment or a field to its page and rectangle, a bookmark to its destination (`destinationActivated`, `PageView::goToDestination`), and an attachment hit is selected and opens nothing; the status line says which class the current hit is in. **Real as of S5 · T7, the find half** (D5-14), at this path although the rest of `ui/write/` is Stage 13's -- Write mode's find is this bar with replace switched on. **One per document window**, above the panes and hidden until `Ctrl+F`, searching that tab's `core/text/DocumentIndex` entry through a `SearchSession` and painting through that tab's `SelectionOverlay`, which every pane of a split shares; it declares `KeyContext::FindSearch`. Incremental: a 200 ms debounce, the newer search cancelling the older, `Enter` starting a pending one at once; the current hit is the first at or after the reader's page, and the view goes to it. **Its toggles ARE the settings** -- each button dispatches the `find.toggle*` command `Alt+R`/`Alt+W`/`Alt+C` dispatch, which writes `search/*`, and the bar follows the Search group and keeps no copy. Every button dispatches through `ActionRegistry` and none carries a `QAction` (convention 23). Scope: document, selection or a page range. It says the count, a first search's progress, *no matches*, a wrap, the bound, and always the glyphs it could not search; **an invalid regular expression is the error, and nothing is searched**. **Still owed:** replace and wildcards (Stage 13); hits do not stream while a first search builds the index. | `core/text/SearchEngine`, `core/text/SearchQuery`, `ui/viewer/SelectionOverlay`, `ui/shell/DocumentWindow`, `core/reflow/ReflowDocument` (S13) |
| `ui/write/SpellCheckPane.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S13) | Full-pass spell check with suggestions and dictionary management. | `core/reflow/SpellChecker` |

### 3.16 Later-arriving subsystems

These land in Stages 9–16. They have rows now because the roadmap's *Components* lists name them, and
because declaring their interface early is what keeps them from reshaping the core when they arrive.

| File | Status | Role | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|
| `core/forms/FieldScript.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S9) | The sandboxed Acrobat-compatible form-JavaScript subset: `this.getField`, `event.value`, `AFNumber_Format`, `AFDate_Format`, `app.alert`. **Enumerated and sandboxed** — no filesystem, no network, no process launch. A call outside the subset is blocked and *reported*. Pure → `tst_fieldscript`. | `core/doc/FormModel`, `core/security/TrustPolicy` |
| `core/create/DocumentBuilder.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S12) | Builds a new `Document` from nothing, from source files, from the clipboard, or from a captured region. A **service, not a command** — it creates a document rather than mutating one. | `core/create/ConversionProfile`, `IDocumentBackend`, `platform::IOfficeBridge`, `platform::IRichClipboard` |
| `core/create/ConversionProfile.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S12) | The named bundle of every conversion setting (page setup, image and font handling, PDF version, conformance, bookmark and link generation). Persisted through `ProfileStore`. | `core/settings/ProfileStore`, `pdf/ops/ContentImport` |
| `pdf/ops/ContentImport.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S12) | Turns imported image, text, HTML, and Markdown content into page content streams. First-party layout — no browser engine (excluded by D2's module list). | `pdf/cos/ContentStreamBuilder`, HarfBuzz via `core/reflow/Shaper` |
| `ui/viewer/tools/RegionCaptureTool.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S12) | Drags a screen region and hands the bitmap to `DocumentBuilder`. | `core/create/DocumentBuilder`, `platform::IDisplayTopology` |
| `core/licensing/{Entitlement,ActivationClient,OfflineActivation}.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S15) | Entitlement state (perpetual / subscription / trial), online activation and deactivation, and the offline signed request/response file exchange. | `platform::ISecretStore`, `QtNetwork`, `ui/dialogs/ActivationDialog` |
| `core/update/{UpdateChecker,DeltaUpdater}.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S15) | Scheduled and on-demand update checks, channel selection, background download, signature verification, delta apply with a full-download fallback. | `QtNetwork`, `core/settings/PolicyOverlay`, `ui/dialogs/UpdateDialog` |
| `core/telemetry/{EventSink,EventCatalog}.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S15) | Opt-in, off by default. `EventCatalog` is the **enumerated, inspectable** list of sendable events; `EventSink` refuses anything not in it, which is what makes "no document content, ever" a mechanical guarantee rather than a promise. | `core/settings/Settings`, `core/settings/PolicyOverlay` |
| `installer/` | ○ (S15) | WiX v4 MSI with per-feature selection (printer driver and shell extensions are separately installable), plus a per-user MSIX. Keeps the Qt DLLs replaceable per the LGPL obligation, and ships the GCC runtime DLLs (`libstdc++-6`, `libgcc_s_seh-1`, `libwinpthread-1`) beside them — never statically linked. WiX packages a file tree and does not care which compiler produced it. | `tools/license-audit` output, `platform/win/WinShellIntegration` |
| `core/ocr/OcrPipeline.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S16) | Implements `IOcrEngine`: page image preprocessing, recognition, confidence reporting, and the searchable-image versus editable-text output modes. | Tesseract, Leptonica, `IPageRasterizer`, `MutationSink` |
| `core/portfolio/*.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S16) | Portfolio container model, component-file operations, cover sheet, and portfolio-wide search and security. | `core/doc/Attachments`, `core/text/SearchEngine` |
| `core/media/*.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S16) | Screen-annotation and rendition models, play areas, activation triggers, and alternate renditions. | `QtMultimedia`, `core/security/TrustPolicy`, `MutationSink` |
| `core/three/*.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S16) | 3D annotation model, U3D/PRC loading, part tree, named views, cross-sections, and 3D measurement. Effectively its own renderer — the largest post-v1 item. | `platform::IAcceleratedSurface`, `MutationSink` |
| `core/review/*.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S16) | Shared-review lifecycle, reviewer tracking, comment merging with conflict resolution, and live collaborative sessions. Blocked on open decision O5. | `core/doc/AnnotationStore`, `QtNetwork` |
| `core/ai/*.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S16) | Summarize, ask-with-citations, translate, TOC proposal, and structured extraction, behind a provider interface. Must be disableable with a **provable** no-transmission guarantee and a per-document opt-out. | `core/text/PageTextModel`, `core/settings/Settings`, `QtNetwork` |
| `core/reflow/TableExtractor.{h,cpp}` | ○ (S16) | *Convert to Writable Excel*: reconstructs a spreadsheet model from a table-heavy PDF and composes back. Depends on S13's converter quality. | `core/text/LayoutAnalyzer`, `core/reflow/TableModel`, `platform::IOfficeBridge` |

### 3.17 `tools/` and `tests/` — the gates

| File | Status | Role | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tools/layer-lint/` | ✅ (S0 · T7) | Parses every `#include` and fails on any outward edge; on a PDFium call **or handle** named outside **`pdf/render/`** (narrowed from `pdf/` at S2 · T2); on a filesystem write outside `pdf/`, `core/settings/`, `tools/` and `tests/`; on a path read out of `argv` outside `app/main.cpp`; and on a `QAction` constructed outside `ui/shell/ActionFactory.cpp`. Two single-file layers carry their own deny lists — `platform/platform.h` and `core/backend.h` — because several layers include each of them, and one narrowing is invisible from the layer table: **`pdf/render/` may include `platform/platform.h` and the rest of `pdf/` may not** (S2 · T16). **From S3 · T3 a `pdf-cos` entry makes `pdf/cos/` the bottom of `pdf/`**: it may include itself and `core/` only, so neither `pdf/ops/` nor `pdf/render/` can be included from it (D3-3, guide A-05). `--explain` prints the include graph, the active rules, and every live escape. Rules live in `rules.json` (data, not code) so widening a layer's permissions is a reviewable diff. Exit codes: 0 clean · 1 violations · **2 could-not-run**, distinct so CI never reads a broken gate as a passing one. Standard library only — no Qt, no vcpkg (D0-1). | CI, the `checks` CMake target |
| `tools/mutation-lint/` | ✅ (S3 · T6) | Asserts every `Command` subclass is in `CommandRegistry` and has a serialize round-trip test and a revert test. A new command without tests fails the build. **As built (D3-12):** reads `core/cmd/Commands.def`, `core/cmd/commands/` and `tests/` AS TEXT with the standard library only, so `checks` runs it before compiling. Four rules: `unregistered-command`, `no-serialize-test` and `no-revert-test` for each class deriving from `Command`, and `row-without-class` for a table row whose header column — which the compiler never reads — does not declare its class. A tag counts only inside a `TEST_CASE`'s own arguments, not in a comment or a string elsewhere, and `tests/gates/` is not scanned. **It proves a test exists, not that it tests anything**; `--explain` lists that and the other blind spots — an indirect base, a macro-declared class. Exit 2 for an absent table, directory or unreadable row. | `core/cmd/Commands.def`, `core/cmd/commands/*`, `tests/`, CI |
| `tools/i18n-lint/` | ✅ | The third gate. Four rules: `unwrapped-literal` (a literal reaching one of the **user-facing sinks** named in `rules.json` and not inside `tr()`; the one escapable rule), `missing-key` (a key absent from a locale marked `"shipped": true`), `action-label` (an `ActionId` with no `action.*` entry, read from `ActionId.def` **as text**), and `orphan-key` (**S3 — reported, never blocking**) -- and, since **S5 · T7**, **`wrong-context`** (blocking): a key's first segment must name the `<context>` it sits in, because `ui/` looks a key up in that context and an entry anywhere else is compiled and found by nothing (S5 guide I-05; its first run found Stage 4 T10's `shell.tooltip.needsPresentation` in the `action` context). Sink-driven rather than literal-driven on purpose: a rule that fires on object names, settings paths and format specifiers is a rule that gets waived everywhere and then switched off. **An action's STRING ID is not a catalog key** (S5 · T13, S5 guide I-08): on an `ActionId.def` row only the label column counts as a reference, and a JSON `"action"` member's value names a command and no key -- since the `compare` family (D5-20) shares its namespace with the actions `compare.documents` and `compare.showReport`. Links nothing but the standard library (D0-1) — it reads Qt Linguist `.ts` files **without Qt**. It scans `.def` tables (T9, issue I-16) and, from T10, `res/ribbon.json`: a gate reads the files that **reference** a key, and whether one happens to be compiled is a fact about the build rather than about what the gate is for. | `i18n/*.ts`, `i18n/locales.json`, `core/cmd/ActionId.def`, `res/ribbon.json`, CI |
| `tools/license-audit/` | ✅ (S0 · T8) | Walks the resolved vcpkg manifest plus the Qt module list and the GCC runtime, emits `THIRD-PARTY.md`, and fails on any SPDX **expression** outside the allowlist. Matching is whole-expression, never a `GPL` substring: `GPL-3.0-or-later WITH GCC-exception-3.1` must pass while bare `GPL-3.0-or-later` must fail. Also asserts no non-free GCC plugin was loaded, which is the condition the exception rests on. | `vcpkg.json`, `cmake/toolchain-mingw.cmake`, `ui/dialogs/ThirdPartyNoticesDialog`, CI |
| `tools/golden-update/` | ✅ **(S2 · T17)** | Regenerates `golden/` reference rasters, requiring an explicit reason so a rendering regression cannot be laundered into a new baseline (**D2-3**). **`--reason` is mandatory and the refusal exits 2 with a diagnostic citing the decision**; the reason lands in `golden/captured.json`, in an append-only `golden/UPDATES.md` and in a ready-to-paste commit block — a **reviewed file** rather than only a commit message, which can be amended or squashed away. **`--filter` is repeatable from Stage 3 T1** — a case matches when any filter does — so one decision to add baselines for files that share no substring is one run and one `UPDATES.md` entry. The widget suite (D1-4). **At T9:** theme tokens over both modes, the sidebar's auto-hide and width memory, the status bar's no-document state, `openFiles()` under D1-2, and the string dump's coverage of every shell key and every action label. **T10** added the ribbon build and the action-wiring self-check against planted violations; **T11** the Quick Access Toolbar's operations, its round trip through `Settings`, the customization blob's version handling, and the tool finder's ranking; **T12** the document tabs — reorder, close, step, release and adopt, tear-off, and the window set that makes a Global command act on the window that is active; **T13** the keyboard, over synthetic focus chains and a real shown window; and **T14** the Preferences dialog (asserted against the SCHEMA in both directions, so a hand-written row fails as loudly as a missing one) and the log pane. Ten suites beside its own `main.cpp`, split by subject rather than by task. Links `rawpage_core` + `rawpage_ui` and nothing else; runs under `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen`, set both by CTest and by its own `main()` so that a developer running the binary by hand gets CI's conditions rather than real windows on their desktop. It installs `rawpage_en.qm`, so the widgets are exercised against real text rather than against their own catalog keys. **None of it can judge appearance** — that is the guide's manual half, and a case here claiming to have checked a *look* is a defect in the case. Windows only; never on `portable-asan`. **Arrived at T9 rather than the plan's T15**, on the precedent `tests/platform` set at T7. **Extended at T10** with `tst_ribbonbuild` and `tst_actionwiring`, which between them cover A-50, A-51's runtime half and A-54 — and which found three defects on their first run against the real shown window (**I-18**, **I-19**, **I-20**). The ribbon cases `show()` the widget and activate both layout levels explicitly: offscreen there is no event loop to do the second half, and a bare `resize()` yields stable, plausible, meaningless numbers. **Two cases in this directory link nothing and drive `rawpage.exe` instead**, each because the condition it asserts is a property of a PROCESS: `ScreenMetricsInvariant.cmake` (A-84) varies `QT_SCALE_FACTOR`, which Qt reads once at construction, and `DiagnosticsIgnoreTheBus.ps1` (A-87, 2026-09-13) needs a second process to hold the single-instance bus while a diagnostic answers. The second is PowerShell because CMake cannot start a process and keep it, and the obvious workaround — `taskkill /IM rawpage.exe` — would kill the window of whoever is running the suite; it adopts an instance it finds, stops only what it started, and **skips at 77 if its own control cannot prove the bus was claimed**. **S3 · T10 adds `tst_mutationcommands`** (guide cases A-71 … A-73, A-75 … A-80): the five mutation chords sent as KEY EVENTS to a shown window, a rotated page's layout read before its pixels, the dirty marker in both locales, every close route with each disposition, and the three banners — over `FakeBackend`'s editable mode (a real `Permissions` assessment, a `FakeSink`, saves that write the opened bytes plus a marker) and `ShellFixture`'s `FakeDurableFiles` and temporary `recoveryRoot`. The Unsaved Changes and Save As widgets are answered through their twins and not shown, which the file says at its head. **S3 · T11 adds `tst_recoverydialog`** (guide cases A-67's untitled half, A-82's prompt half), **compiling `app/RecoveryPrompt.cpp` in**, as `tests/unit` compiles three `app/` units — it names `core/` and `ui/` only, so the closure is unchanged. A "crash" there is a `MainWindow` destroyed without closing; the real kill is `tests/composition/`. **S4 · T8 adds `tst_rulers`** (guide cases A-47, A-49 … A-52): a guide dragged off a real ruler by real mouse events, the drag/lock/delete/clear rules, the ruler's reading at a pixel against `core/Units.h`, the three chords as key events, and **two cases whose claim is a NEGATIVE on the scheduler's own counter** — a grid and an inversion that reached the rasterizer would be D4-11 broken and nothing about the picture would look wrong. Its pixel claims are hashes of the rendered viewport rather than counted lines: a case that counted lines would be a copy of the painter. **S4 · T12 adds `tst_properties`** (guide cases A-29, A-68's widget half, A-69, A-70, and the closure of I-05): Fit Visible fitting AND scrolling to a content box the fake backend supplies, with the fallback as its control and the ASK asserted so a fallback that came from never asking is distinguishable; | `tests/fuzz/` | ✅ (S2 · T5, T6 · S3 · T13 · S5 · T4 · **S6 · T2**) | **Five targets since Stage 6 T2: `fuzz_annot_pipeline` (D6-29)** -- bytes through the backend's first-party walk, `pdf/ops/AnnotationReader` and `core::AnnotationStore`, under `AnnotPipelineFuzzBody.h`'s invariants (listing, bounded and finite geometry, normalized rectangles, parent chains that end, the four-way partition, the query, the JSON round trip); T3 adds the appearance builder and T13 the XFDF branch to the same body. **Four targets since Stage 5 T4: `fuzz_text_pipeline` (D5-21)** decodes bytes into a synthetic `PageTextModel` — NaN, the infinities, zero, negative and huge sizes, surrogates, marks, rules — and drives `LayoutAnalyzer` and `TextNormalizer` with their invariants checked — **and since Stage 5 T5 `TextSelection`**: the page's reading, hit-tests at hostile points, word, line and block expansion, a flow and two column copies — **and since T6 `SearchEngine`'s match**: the page made searchable under all four normalizations, every offset inside the reading, and a literal, a whole-word and a regular-expression query finding only non-empty spans inside the page; its body, `TextPipelineFuzzBody.h`, is replayed by `tests/unit/tst_layout.cpp`. **Three targets since Stage 3 T13: `fuzz_cos_roundtrip` (D3-17)** parses any input and, when the first-party reader and sink can edit it, rotates page 1, composes the next revision through `RevisionWriter` with the product's choice of section, and requires every input byte in front, a re-read through the output's own table with nothing forcing Save-As, the rotation read back with every other page unmoved, and `RevisionWriter::verify` agreeing — the body in `RoundtripFuzzBody.h`, replayed by `tst_roundtrip`, compile-checked by `unit.cos.compile.fuzz_entrypoint_roundtrip`. Its body's first local run (a mutation loop over the corpus, 1M inputs) found **Stage 3 guide I-12** — 88 failures, four causes, all on repaired files' complete sections — fixed in the same task. **`tests/fuzz/seeds/`** holds `rawpage_fuzz_seeds`, which keeps the revisions the body composes as seeds (D3-17's *"seeds gain the writer's own outputs"*); it is built on every preset but `gates`, is not a fuzz target, and is `unit.fuzz.roundtrip_seeds`. The count assertion moved to `cmake/RawPageFuzzTargets.cmake`, where `gates.fuzz_count.*` proves it fires. The first two: libFuzzer targets over `pdf/cos/Lexer` and `pdf/cos/Parser`, seeded from `corpus/malformed/` — a good seed set because every file in it is a VALID document with one named fault, so the fuzzer starts from inputs that already reach deep into the reader. `fuzz_cos_lexer` (T5) and `fuzz_cos_parser` (T6), the primary one, **run on Linux only** — 60 s each on every push since Stage 2 T18, and ten minutes in `.github/workflows/fuzz-soak.yml` (run 34781013867, clean; with the round trip, run 35137107236, clean on all three) — there being no clang on the dev box. Two things make targets this machine cannot build still trustworthy: the invariants live in `LexerFuzzBody.h` and `ParserFuzzBody.h` and are replayed over the corpus by `tst_cos_lexer` and `tst_cos_parser` on every Windows run, and each entry point is compile-checked here (`unit.cos.compile.fuzz_entrypoint`, `unit.cos.compile.fuzz_entrypoint_parser`) so a changed signature cannot break it unnoticed. Replaying known files is **not fuzzing** and finds nothing new by construction — the suites say so at the assertion rather than letting a green count imply otherwise. The parser body's three non-sanitizer checks are progress, the depth bound (measured by an ITERATIVE walk, since a recursive one would be the bug it looks for) and span containment, that last being the one whose failure becomes someone else's memory inside a saved PDF at Stage 3. `tests/fuzz/CMakeLists.txt` **fails configure on the wrong target count**, which is guide case A-27's *"zero is a failure, not a pass"* made structural. **Stage 2, not Stage 3** — they can only exist once the lexer and parser do, and `CMakePresets.json`'s `portable-fuzz` description has said *“NO TARGETS EXIST until Stage 2”* since Stage 0. Stage 2 T5 and T6 built them (Stage 2 guide **I-01**). Built by the `portable-fuzz` preset, **not** by the Windows GCC build, which has no libFuzzer; findings are reproduced in `x64-debug` before they count as fixed. **S4 · T12 gave all four targets here the LANGUAGE they had never had** (issue **I-08**): `rawpage_add_fuzz_target` skips `rawpage_strict()` deliberately — `-Werror` breaks on a sanitizer's own warnings — and skipping it also skipped `rawpage_common_flags`, where `CXX_STANDARD 20` is set, so the three drivers had been compiled at clang's default standard since Stage 2 T5 and `rawpage_fuzz_seeds` at GCC's since Stage 3 T13. **Being Linux-only is exactly why it lasted**: no Windows configure builds these, so the first thing to notice was CI run 35799559455's `Portable libFuzzer` job, after the Windows half of the same defect had already been found and fixed. They now call `rawpage_common_flags` and not `rawpage_strict`, which keeps `-Werror` off for the reason it was always off. **S6 · T13:** `fuzz_annot_pipeline`'s EXCHANGE branch (D6-29) -- any bytes read as an exchange file, written back both ways and read again, and merged into a page (`exchangeFuzzOnce`), seeded with `corpus/exchange`. | `pdf/cos/*` |
