At S1 · T14 the count was **66** (59 at T9; T10 added the `bare-qaction` rule across four spellings with two controls, T14 the namespace-reference case for `orphan-key`), of which 14 are `i18n-lint`'s and 4 arrived with T8 and T9 — the `argv-path-read` rule with its entry-point control, and `ui/`'s two permitted interface headers with a control for each direction. `fixtures/layer-lint/README.md` records which spellings of a violation are covered and which are knowingly not. `RunGateCase.cmake` asserts **both** exit code and diagnostic, because CTest's `WILL_FAIL` cannot tell a correct finding (exit 1) from a crashed gate (exit 2), and `PASS_REGULAR_EXPRESSION` ignores the exit code entirely. Includes the negative controls without which every positive case is satisfied by a gate that rejects everything. | `tools/*`, `cmake/ToolchainAssert.cmake`, CI |
| `tools/spike-pdfium/` | ⤵ **deleted (S2 · T2)** | **Throwaway, and it was thrown away.** Answered risk A6 — a PDFium DLL links from `g++` and opens a document — and was deleted at Stage 2 T2 when `pdf/render/PdfiumInit` replaced it, exactly as this row said it would be and exactly as `tools/spike-ribbon/` went at Stage 1 T10. Its durable outputs are the recorded A6 verdict, `scripts/fetch-pdfium.ps1`, and the two findings in `third_party/pdfium/README.md` — that PDFium statically links its own CRT, and that the archive's `LICENSE` is the packager's rather than PDFium's. | `third_party/pdfium/` |
| `tests/bench/` | ✅ **(S6 · T15 `bench_surface --stage6` -- A2 AT STAGE 6 RE-READ THROUGH THE SHIPPED SURFACES (D6-24, guide A-104): a window split in two, the shipped pencil drawing a stroke every 100 ms on page 450 of `pages-900.pdf` in one pane while the other scrolls, graded on the SCROLLING pane's tiles (the probe paired with a ready subscriber to tell the panes apart), edit-to-pixels from the release to a changed tile; registered SHORT as `bench.surface.a2_stage6`, the program's one CTest case -- and `bench_pipeline --stage6`'s edit now §3.1's 200-sample ink, its edit column reading each page's path geometry (D6-14); S5 guide I-12: `bench_pipeline --stage5`'s inversions read from `RenderScheduler::Stats::startedWhileMoreUrgentWaited`, its stamp inference gone, and `bench_surface` printing the same count per Stage 5 column; S5 · T14 `bench_surface`'s Stage 5 columns — A2 at Stage 5 RE-READ THROUGH THE SHIPPED SURFACES (D5-22, guide A-112): over `text-900.pdf`, the find bar typed into, the search pane's own `search()` over a corpus copy, and `MainWindow::compareDocuments`, each graded on the tiles delivered while its work ran and printed beside the viewer alone over the same rounds, the comparison's first fill waited for before the scroll; the English catalog loaded through `app::Application`, so the chrome is laid out in its labels and not in its keys; S5 · T3 `bench_pipeline --stage5`, phase 6 — A2 at Stage 5: the viewport while text shares the engine thread, over an index build, a folder search and a comparison, with the work stamped on the worker (D5-22); S1 · T15; S2 · T3, T18; S3 · T8 `bench_save`; S4 · D4-20, `bench_surface`'s `+ loupe` column — the Loupe's sharp lens swept inside the graded Visible lane, guide A-100; S4 · T2 `bench_pipeline --stage4`, the workload D4-16 grades — a viewer, a split and a thumbnails pane over one scheduler, one worker and one cache, decomposed into four columns so the pane's cost can be read on its own, and the measurement that answered `WinAcceleratedSurface`; S6 · T4 `bench_pipeline --stage6`)** | **S6 · T4 adds `bench_pipeline --stage6`** (D6-24): *A2 at Stage 6*, graded by hand at 30 s a file and registered short as `bench.render.a2_stage6`. The measurements that gate a decision rather than pass or fail. **Real as of T15:** `bench_ribbon`, the A3 criterion **R-9** — a ribbon tab switch inside one 60 Hz frame — reported in three phases and carrying its own **control**, a resize across the collapse threshold, which is what proves the 0.000 ms layout column is a fact about the ribbon rather than a dead instrument. **Real as of S2 · T3:** `bench_render`, risk **A2**'s early probe over raw `FPDF_RenderPageBitmap` (D2-1, first half) — p95 **0.532 ms** against a 16.667 ms budget, and the run that found **I-05**, that PDFium cannot be called from two threads at once. It carries its control the same way: a **throughput** figure beside the percentiles, because per-tile samples are service times and stay flat whether or not the threads are queueing, and a p95 ratio would have read below 1.0 and looked like better-than-perfect scaling. A bench target's exit code is about whether the measurement was *possible*, never about whether the machine was fast. `bench_ribbon` is Windows-only because it links `rawpage_ui`; `bench_render` is Windows-only because it links the engine, and it creates no Qt GUI object at all. **Real as of S2 · T18:** `bench_pipeline`, A2's PRODUCT figure (D2-1, second half) — p95 **14.18 ms** of queued latency against 16.667 ms at T18, an upper bound (Stage 2 I-41; **13.03–13.20 ms** re-measured 2026-09-14), over a 1920×1080 viewport at A2's own 288 dpi, through `pdf::createBackend()` → `app::BackendRasterizer` → `RenderScheduler` → `RenderCache`. **It opens with phase 0, its latency control** (guide case A-69, 2026-09-14): the same instrument over a rasterizer with a known 2.0 ms cost, which exits 1 before any figure if the instrument is dead, quantized to a timer tick (Stage 2 I-41) or not counting the queue. `--dead-instrument` and `--coarse-pump` are its negative controls. **A SECOND BINARY RATHER THAN A REWRITE OF `bench_render`, against this row's own earlier prediction that T18 would replace it**: the probe's figure has to stay reproducible (D2-1 keeps both in `Tech-Stack-Plan.md` §3.3) and `--unsafe-parallel` is I-05's on-demand reproducer, so deleting that program would make a recorded finding un-re-takeable. It **does not link raw `pdfium`**, which is the structural half of the claim: a measurement of the product's render path that could reach past the facade would be measuring something the product does not do. It also produces **O7's** numbers (cross-document evictions at two cache caps) and **D2-14's** (the disk tier, which the measurement says does not earn its place). **Real as of 2026-09-13, added outside any task:** `bench_openclose`, the reproducer for Stage 2 issue **I-35**. It reports live heap, private bytes and handles per round of ten corpus documents opened, rendered and closed through the same backend, scheduler and cache with **no window**. `--no-render` drops the rasterizing, `--bridge` installs the font hook the product installs, and by default the last page's tiles are left undelivered at the close, which is the state guide case A-88 needed. **Its own control is a delivered-tile count per round**, because a render loop that silently delivered nothing would report *flat* for free. It is not a CTest case: it reports a trend over dozens of rounds and no threshold for that trend is decided. **And its window-level partner, the same day:** `bench_openclose_ui` composes `app::CompositionRoot` and `ui::MainWindow` without a person -- offscreen, or `--native` on the real platform and style -- and reports LIVE heap per round with the block sizes and signal connections that grew; guide case **A-92** is its reading. Two instruments live beside them and are not benches: `HeapCensus.{h,cpp}`, the sum of busy blocks over every heap with a histogram by size, whose walk allocates nothing so it cannot count itself; and `AllocationTracker.{h,cpp}`, which replaces the executable's global `operator new` to record the call stack of every allocation still alive -- our code's only, not Qt's or PDFium's, and it says so. All four of I-35's causes were found with these three, after a first diagnosis read from private bytes had pointed at the wrong layer -- and so was its fifth, the classic heap's fragmentation, whose fix (`app/rawpage.manifest`) the product-like benches now embed. **`bench_openclose_ui_classicheap`** is the same harness built WITHOUT that manifest, the control that keeps the fragmentation reproducible without editing a build file. Five benches now, plus that control, and the count is re-measured here rather than carried. | `ui/shell/Ribbon` (S1); `pdf/render/*` (S2 · T3); `core/render/*`, `app/BackendRasterizer.h` (S2 · T18); `platform/win/WinFontResolver` (2026-09-13); `app/CompositionRoot`, `ui/shell/MainWindow` (2026-09-13, I-35) **S4 · T14 adds `bench_surface`** — A2 re-read through the SHIPPED panes (D4-16, guide A-72): `CompositionRoot` and `MainWindow` as `app/main.cpp` assembles them, over `pages-900.pdf`, with the real `ThumbnailsPane`, `SplitViewContainer` and `TilePresenter` issuing every request and the harness issuing none. It is a SEPARATE TARGET from `bench_pipeline --stage4` deliberately: one reports T2's synthetic pattern and the other the product's own, and a flag that could print either under one name would make D2-1's error available again. It reads the queued latency through `RenderScheduler::setLatencyProbe`, the only probe installed anywhere in the tree. |

---

### 3.18 Build system, scripts, and CI

The map owed these rows: Stage 0 built a working build system, and a file map that describes every
`.cpp` but none of the machinery that compiles them is the first place this document starts drifting
from the tree.

| File | Status | Role | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|
| `pdf/ops/CMakeLists.txt` | ✅ (S3 · T3) | **`rawpage_ops`** (decision D3-3): a portable static library linking `rawpage_core` and `rawpage_cos` and no engine, added in `pdf/CMakeLists.txt` **before** the portable-subset return so `portable-asan` and `portable-fuzz` build it — which is the reason it is not part of `rawpage_pdfium`, where Stage 3's page walk and sink would have been out of ASan's reach. `rawpage_tests` links it under the unchanged `rawpage_assert_headless()`; `rawpage_render_tests` links it for the engine comparison. | `pdf/ops/*`, `pdf/cos/`, `cmake/RawPageHeadless.cmake` |
| `CMakeLists.txt` | ✅ (S0 · T2) | Root build definition. Descends into the layer libraries — six since Stage 3 T3: `rawpage_core`, `rawpage_cos`, `rawpage_ops`, `rawpage_pdfium`, `rawpage_platform`, `rawpage_ui` (four when this row was written) — plus `rawpage`, so the **linker itself** catches a class of layering violation before `layer-lint` runs; since Stage 3 T13 it also adds `tests/fuzz/seeds/` on every preset but `gates`. Also holds the three build-shape switches: `RAWPAGE_PORTABLE_SUBSET` (`core/` + `pdf/cos/` + `pdf/ops/`, for the Linux sanitizer and fuzz jobs), `RAWPAGE_GATES_ONLY` (tools/ + tests/gates/ with **no Qt and no vcpkg** — what makes the CI gates job possible), and `RAWPAGE_TARGET_ARCH` (arm64 fails here with an explanation rather than three assertions deep). | every layer, `cmake/*`, CI |
| `CMakePresets.json` | ✅ (S0 · T4) | Six configure presets. vcpkg is the outer toolchain with the pin **chainloaded** via `VCPKG_CHAINLOAD_TOOLCHAIN_FILE` — not the reverse. `hostSystemName` conditions hide the Linux presets on Windows and vice versa. | `cmake/toolchain-mingw.cmake`, CI |
| `cmake/ToolchainAssert.cmake` | ✅ (S0 · T3) | TA-1…TA-9: compiler vendor, exact version, triple, SEH, posix, **msvcrt-not-UCRT**, compiler-inside-the-pinned-root, Qt version/prefix, and no non-free GCC plugin. Separate from the toolchain file because CMake re-reads that file for every `try_compile()`. Each failure names the offending value **and the fix**. | `CMakeLists.txt`, `tests/gates/fixtures/wrong-compiler/` |
| `cmake/RawPageWarnings.cmake` | ✅ (S0 · T2) | The one place flags are decided. `-Werror` for `core/` and `pdf/`; a shrinking grace list for `ui/`; `-g` in **every** configuration because DWARF in the binary is the only symbolization story this toolchain has. Records why `-fanalyzer` is absent — it false-positives on `std::string` with this GCC. **`rawpage_common_flags()` is also where `CXX_STANDARD 20` is set, and that turned out to matter more than it reads** (S4 · T12, issue **I-08**): `rawpage_strict()` is this function plus `-Werror`, so a target that skipped `rawpage_strict()` to avoid `-Werror` — which the `tests/fuzz` drivers do, correctly, because `-Werror` breaks on a sanitizer's own warnings — lost the LANGUAGE as collateral. Four targets were compiled at the compiler's default standard for weeks. `cmake/RawPageLanguageStandard.cmake` is the assertion that closes it. | every target |
| `cmake/RawPageLanguageStandard.cmake` | ✅ **(S4 · T12)** | **`rawpage_assert_language_standard(20)`: every compiled target in this project is C++20, refused at CONFIGURE time.** It walks the real build graph — every directory's `BUILDSYSTEM_TARGETS`, recursively — skips what cannot carry a standard (UTILITY targets like `checks`, interface libraries, anything IMPORTED), and fails naming the target and the call to add. **It reads the graph rather than the CMakeLists text**, for the reason `layer-lint` reads the includes the compiler reads: a check on the build files' source would have to model `add_subdirectory`, functions and conditionals to answer a question CMake answers exactly. Called LAST from the root `CMakeLists.txt`, beside `rawpage_write_qt_module_list()` and for the same reason — an earlier call sees a partial graph and passes by accident. **Why an assertion rather than a corrected comment** (issue **I-08**): the defect had four instances arriving by two different routes, one of them a file whose comment gave a *good* reason for what it was doing, and a fix applied to the known targets is a fix that waits for the fifth. **Two bounds stated at the file:** it checks `CXX_STANDARD` and nothing else of what `rawpage_common_flags` carries — a missing `-Wshadow` loses a warning, a missing `-std=c++20` loses a language — and it walks only the graph the CURRENT preset configures, so a Windows configure walks 32 targets and the three `RAWPAGE_BUILD_FUZZERS` drivers are 35. Its control was run: with the call removed from `rawpage_fuzz_seeds`, configure fails naming it. | every target; `cmake/RawPageWarnings.cmake` |
| `cmake/RawPageQt.cmake` | ✅ (S0 · T2) | `rawpage_link_qt()` — no target links Qt directly. Records the real link closure into `qt-modules.txt` for `license-audit`, and refuses `Qt6::Charts`/`DataVisualization`/`VirtualKeyboard` at configure time with a named GPL-3.0 diagnostic. | `license-audit`, every Qt-using target |
| `cmake/RawPageChecks.cmake` | ✅ (S0 · T9 · extended S1 · T6, S3 · T6) | The `checks` target, running **four** gates since Stage 3 T6, and its `COMMENT` names all four. Depends **only** on the gate executables, never the layer libraries — that is what lets CI run the gates before compiling anything. `i18n-lint`'s three inputs are passed explicitly, so an absent one is exit 2 rather than a silently skipped rule; `mutation-lint` takes only the root, and a root missing any of its inputs is exit 2. | `tools/*`, CI |
| `cmake/RawPageFuzzTargets.cmake` | ✅ (S3 · T13) | **A row the file map did not have**, added with the file. `rawpage_assert_fuzz_target_count(expected)` — the calling directory's own build-system targets, compared with the count `tests/fuzz/CMakeLists.txt` declares (3 since Stage 3 T13), failing configure otherwise: *"ten minutes with no crashes"* is passed by a target that does not exist (Stage 2 guide A-27, Stage 3 guide A-83). Moved out of `tests/fuzz/CMakeLists.txt`, which only `portable-fuzz` reads, so that `tests/gates/fixtures/fuzz-count/` could drive the real function on every preset: before T13 nothing had shown it refuses. A helper in a subdirectory is not counted. | `tests/fuzz/`, `tests/gates/fixtures/fuzz-count/` |
| `cmake/RawPageHeadless.cmake` | ✅ (S2 · T1) | `rawpage_assert_headless()` — the standing proof that `core/` needs no display and no PDF engine, checked against the **resolved link closure** rather than one link line, because a transitive edge is how this actually leaks. Forbidden: `rawpage_ui`, `rawpage_platform` and — since T1 — **`rawpage_pdfium`** (D2-2), which is the load-bearing half of the `pdf/` library split rather than a tidy-up. **It was moved out of `tests/unit/CMakeLists.txt` so a gate fixture could exercise the real function**: a fixture carrying its own copy proves the copy fires (convention 7). Two properties of the walk are deliberate and each was written against a way it had already failed — the forbidden **name** is rejected before the target is resolved, so the rule still holds on the portable presets where `rawpage_pdfium` will never exist; and both `LINK_LIBRARIES` **and** `INTERFACE_LINK_LIBRARIES` are read, because an INTERFACE or IMPORTED target (`Qt6::Core` among them) leaves the first unset and a walk reading one of the two stops at the first edge of the wrong kind and reports a closure it never finished. | `tests/unit/`, `tests/gates/fixtures/headless-assert/` |
| `cmake/PdfiumAssert.cmake` | ✅ (S2 · T2) | **PA-1 … PA-5: the rasterizer's pin.** Reads the fetched `third_party/pdfium/VERSION` and `args.gn` and fails configure on a version mismatch, an unparseable version file, a V8- or XFA-enabled archive, or a missing DLL or GNU import library — each with a named diagnostic carrying **found, expected and the fix**, the shape `ToolchainAssert.cmake` established. **The expected values live here and the found values are read out of the fetched tree, and that asymmetry IS the check**: `third_party/pdfium/` is `.gitignore`d, so a pin stored there would be one the repository does not contain and a mismatched fetch would agree with itself. Why it exists at all (D2-3): a golden baseline is a claim about **one** rasterizer, and an unnoticed engine bump silently reclassifies every rendering regression as a baseline update. Seven `gates.pdfium_pin.*` cases drive it over committed fixture trees, so they run on the bare Linux gates runner with no PDFium fetched. | `CMakeLists.txt`, `pdf/`, `tests/gates/fixtures/pdfium-pin/` |
| `vcpkg.json` | ✅ (S0 · T6) | Every §6.2 dependency, grouped into features so resolution can be **tested now and required later** (D0-3). Only `tests` is built today. | `vcpkg-configuration.json`, `license-audit` |
| `vcpkg-configuration.json` | ✅ (S0 · T5) | Pins the vcpkg baseline commit, so a `git pull` in the vcpkg clone changes nothing and a dependency update is an attributable diff. Registers `tools/ports/` as the overlay directory. | `vcpkg.json` |
| `tools/ports/` | ✅ (S0 · T5) | Empty, deliberately. Where a port goes when vcpkg lacks a working `x64-mingw-dynamic` build — **never** a reason to change the triplet. A5 found no such port. | `vcpkg-configuration.json` |
| `scripts/env.ps1` | ✅ (S0 · T1) | Dot-sourced; puts the pinned toolchain on `PATH` and sets `VCPKG_ROOT` itself, so the build never depends on machine state. Detects being *run* rather than dot-sourced and says so. | every documented command, CI |
| `scripts/bootstrap.ps1` | ✅ (S0 · T1) | Verifies prerequisites and reports what to fix. **Installs and downloads nothing** — a bootstrap that silently mutates a build box is not one to trust on a build box. | `scripts/env.ps1` |
| `scripts/symbolize.ps1` | ✅ (S1 · T1) | Resolves a minidump's faulting frame to a function name, and **refuses a binary whose build id does not match the dump's**. That refusal is the point of the script: the GCC toolchain emits DWARF rather than PDB, so there is no symbol server to arbitrate, and a stack symbolized against the wrong build is worse than an unsymbolized one — it is a plausible, confidently wrong answer. Drives `addr2line` from the pinned binutils. Guide case **A-65**. D1-13 says build this path now rather than at Stage 15, because the first real dump arrives during Stage 2's rendering work. | `app/CrashHandler`, `cmake/RawPageWarnings.cmake` (`-Wl,--build-id`) |
| `scripts/check-single-instance.ps1` | ✅ (S1 · T15) | Guide case **A-61** end to end: `general/singleInstance=false` gives two processes, and the control shows `true` gives one. It is a **script rather than a CTest case**, and the reason is measured rather than assumed — Qt resolves `QStandardPaths::AppDataLocation` through `SHGetKnownFolderPath`, so overriding `APPDATA` for a child process does *not* redirect the settings file, and `main.cpp` exposes no switch for `CompositionRoot::Options::settingsPath`. So the harness edits the real settings file and restores it in a `finally` — which is precisely why it must not be a suite anybody runs casually. It runs **both** directions: a run that only checked `false` would pass against a build whose single-instance code had been deleted entirely (convention 7). The policy half — that `false` never touches the bus at all — *is* a CTest case, in `tests/unit/tst_singleinstance.cpp`. | `app/SingleInstance`, `platform/win/WinSingleInstanceBus` |
| `scripts/fetch-pdfium.ps1` | ✅ (S0 · T12) | Fetches the pinned PDFium, verifies its **SHA-256** and refuses on mismatch, then generates the GNU import library upstream does not ship (`gendef` + `dlltool`). Idempotent. | `third_party/pdfium/` |
| `third_party/pdfium/` | ✅ (S0 · T12) | Fetched, never committed — only its `README.md` is. The reproducibility is in the pin, not in the bytes. | `scripts/fetch-pdfium.ps1` |
| `corpus/make-minimal.py` | ✅ (S0 · T12) | Generates `corpus/minimal.pdf`. A generator rather than a committed blob because a PDF xref stores **absolute byte offsets**: a one-character edit invalidates every one of them. Stage 2 owns the real corpus. | `corpus/` |
| `corpus/make-a2-text-page.py`, `corpus/a2-text-page.pdf` | ✅ (S2 · T3) | The A2 probe's input, and the first file generated under D2-11's all-generated corpus rule. A dense A4 page: 62 text runs, **6,650 glyphs** of 9 pt Helvetica, two hairlines, uncompressed content stream, and it **regenerates byte-identically** — deterministic by construction, with a fixed word list and a fixed LCG rather than `random`, whose stream is an implementation detail of the interpreter. Its docstring states what the page deliberately is **not** — no embedded font, no images, no shading, no transparency, no annotations — because the A2 verdict inherits every one of those limits and a probe's caveats belong with the probe's input. **T4 folded it into the corpus proper with a `PROVENANCE.md` row**, and deliberately did *not* port it onto `corpus/pdfbuild.py`: its bytes are the A2 probe's named input in `Tech-Stack-Plan.md` §3.3, and rewriting it would change bytes that document cites for nothing. | `tests/bench/bench_render.cpp`; `corpus/` (S2 · T4) |
| `corpus/PROVENANCE.md` | ✅ (S2 · T4) | **The record D2-11 requires**: one row per corpus file, naming its generator, what it is for, and — for `malformed/` — exactly what is wrong with it. Also the single place three *limits* are written down rather than left to be inferred: the JPX stream carries one coding pass, the JPEG streams are baseline 4:4:4 only, and the embedded fonts are sixteen rectilinear glyphs. Completeness in both directions is a gate, not a habit (**A-77**): an undocumented file and a row for a file nothing generates each fail `gates.corpus.provenance`. It is the only mechanism in the project that keeps *“nothing is borrowed”* checkable — `license-audit` governs linked dependencies and says nothing about **data**, which is the gap D2-11 names. | `corpus/make-corpus.py`, `corpus/**` |
| `corpus/make-corpus.py` | ✅ (S2 · T4) | The corpus driver, and the home of T4's exit criterion. `--verify` regenerates every file in memory and compares it against the tree — which catches a non-deterministic generator, a corpus file **edited by hand**, a `.gitattributes` regression that lets CRLF injection shift every xref offset, and an **ORPHAN**, a `.pdf` on disk no generator claims. `--check-provenance` is A-77; `--self-test` is **A-78**, the negative control the other two cannot supply for themselves, because a checker that returns success without looking passes both. It also builds twice in one process, which catches a per-run seed that a disk comparison alone would only catch on a machine that had already written the first answer. | `corpus/make-*.py`, `tests/gates/CMakeLists.txt` |
| `corpus/pdfbuild.py` | ✅ (S2 · T4) | Deterministic PDF assembly — the shared spine under every T4 generator: objects, classic xref, **xref streams and object streams**, incremental revisions with a real `/Prev` chain, the filter helpers (`flate`, `ascii85`, `asciihex`, `runlength`, `lzw`, PNG predictors) and page-tree furniture. It does **not** validate: a generator asking for a `/Count` that lies gets one, on purpose, because that is what `corpus/malformed/` is made of. Every `/CreationDate` is a fixed instant and every `/ID` is a hash of the file's own content — a clock or a random id would make the corpus regenerate differently every run and `--verify` meaningless. | `corpus/deflate.py`, `corpus/make-*.py` |
| `corpus/deflate.py` | ✅ (S2 · T4; **S6 · T1** the length limiter, guide I-01) | A DEFLATE/zlib encoder we own, emitting **stored, fixed and dynamic** Huffman blocks so `pdf/cos/filters/Flate` (T7) meets all three decode paths. **Not `zlib.compress`**, and that is the load-bearing choice: zlib's output is a function of the build the interpreter links — match-chain limits, lazy-match thresholds, the dynamic cutoff — so the same script would emit different bytes here and on the Ubuntu runner, turning `gates.corpus.reproducible` red for a reason unrelated to this project. Self-checks every stream through `zlib.decompress`, an independent decoder. | `corpus/pdfbuild.py` |
| `corpus/jpegenc.py` | ✅ (S2 · T4) | Baseline JPEG (ITU-T T.81) for `/DCTDecode`: 8-bit, sequential, Huffman, 4:4:4, the Annex K tables. The forward DCT uses a **hardcoded cosine table** rather than `math.cos`, whose last bit is the platform's libm — one ULP would round one coefficient differently and change the file. No progressive, no subsampling, no arithmetic coding, no restart markers, each named in `PROVENANCE.md` as a limit rather than left to be discovered. | `corpus/make-ordinary.py` |
| `corpus/ccitt.py` | ✅ (S2 · T4) | CCITT Group 3 one-dimensional (T.4) **and** Group 4 (T.6) for `/CCITTFaxDecode` — two genuinely different decoder paths, so the corpus carries both. Ships a **decoder in the same file**, written from the decoding tables rather than by inverting the encoder, and every encode round-trips through it exactly before it reaches a file. Without that the corpus could ship an image only our own decoder agrees with, which would make the filter suite agree with itself (convention 7). | `corpus/make-ordinary.py` |
| `corpus/jpxenc.py` | ✅ (S2 · T4) | JPEG 2000 for `/JPXDecode`: MQ arithmetic coder, EBCOT tier-1 **cleanup pass**, tag trees, packet headers, single tile, **zero decomposition levels** (so the LL band *is* the image and the DWT drops out). **One coding pass**, so a 0/255 source reconstructs to roughly 0/128 — a *truncated* codestream, not a malformed one. It exercises the `/JPXDecode` path and is explicitly **not** a claim about JPEG 2000 fidelity; the limit is stated in the module header, in `PROVENANCE.md` and in the guide's §7 caveats. Verified against OpenJPEG through Pillow when Pillow is importable. | `corpus/make-ordinary.py` |
| `corpus/fontenc.py` | ✅ (S2 · T4) | Synthetic TrueType (`glyf`/`loca`/`cmap`/`hmtx`/`head`/`hhea`/`maxp`/`name`/`post`/`OS/2`) for the embedded-font fixtures — a real typeface would be a licensed third-party file, exactly what D2-11 rules out and the one kind of corpus content `license-audit` cannot govern. Nine hanzi (一 二 三 十 口 日 田 中 目) and seven capitals (E F H I L T), all built from rectangles: real characters correctly drawn, chosen because those glyphs genuinely are straight strokes. What is exercised is the embedding path — `/FontFile2`, CIDFontType2, Identity-H, `/CIDToGIDMap`, `/W` — not typography. | `corpus/make-ordinary.py` |
| `corpus/cryptenc.py` | ✅ (S2 · T4) | The PDF standard security handler, **encrypt side only**, for `corpus/encrypted/`: RC4, AES-128/192/256, revisions 2/3/4/6 — including R6's Algorithm 2.B iterated SHA-2/AES hash, which shares no code with the MD5-based revisions. AES is written out rather than imported because `cryptography` is not a dependency of this project; it is verified against the FIPS-197 known-answer vectors always, and against `cryptography` when that happens to be importable. **Test data only** — `pdf/cos/StandardSecurityHandler` (S10) is the real one and shares no code with it, so nothing here is on a path a user's document takes. | `corpus/make-encrypted.py` |
| `corpus/cmsenc.py` | ✅ (S3 · T1) | **Detached CMS signatures, sign side**, for `corpus/signed/` (D3-2): DER, a **deterministic RSA-2048 key derived from a public seed** — PKCS#1 v1.5 has no salt, so the same key over the same bytes regenerates the same signature and D2-11's byte-identity holds — a self-signed X.509 v3 certificate naming itself a corpus test identity, and an RFC 5652 `SignedData` with `contentType`, `signingTime` (fixed) and `messageDigest`. **No key is stored anywhere**; it is re-derived at generation time. Self-checks by re-doing the RSA public operation, and with `cryptography` when that is importable; the authoritative check is OpenSSL in `gates.corpus.signed`. **Test data only** — it shares no code with Stage 11's signing or with OpenSSL, which is what lets OpenSSL judge it. | `corpus/make-signed.py` |
| `corpus/make-signed.py`, `corpus/signed/**` | ✅ (S3 · T1) | **Four genuinely signed documents** for risk A4: an approval signature over a classic table, the same over an xref stream (page in an `/ObjStm`, signature dictionary kept loose through `pdfbuild`'s `keep_loose`, because `/ByteRange` needs file offsets), a **certification with `/DocMDP /P 1`** — the policy control — and an approval followed by an unsigned incremental update, whose signed range ends at the **first** `%%EOF`. Each file is serialized with a fixed-width `/ByteRange` and zero-filled `/Contents`, filled without moving a byte, then signed. `generate()` asserts the private exponent is in no output. The bound — one generated signer, no timestamp, no PAdES — is in `PROVENANCE.md`. | `corpus/cmsenc.py`, `corpus/pdfbuild.py`, `tests/gates/check-signatures.py` |
| `corpus/make-ordinary.py`, `corpus/ordinary/**` | ✅ (S2 · T4, **35 files from Stage 4 T1**) | **35 well-formed files** — the ones that are supposed to open and render. Between them: DCT (RGB and grey), JPX, CCITT G3 and G4, Flate-with-predictor, indexed/stencil/soft-masked and filter-chained images; axial, radial and function-based shadings; coloured and uncoloured tiling patterns; transparency groups, blend modes and a luminosity soft mask; hairlines and both winding rules; embedded CJK and Latin subsets; a form-heavy page with nine widget types **each carrying a real `/AP`**; nine annotation types; a tagged document whose drawing order is not its reading order; outline and page labels; **900 pages**, an odd count with a cover, an RTL binding, object-stream/xref-stream structure, and **three incremental generations**. Several are deliberately awkward, per D2-11's bar: a construct the corpus never contains is one nothing in this project ever renders. **T16 added the thirtieth, `font-not-embedded.pdf`**, because none of the other 29 could serve guide case A-66: every text fixture used either a base-14 face or an embedded `/FontFile2`, and a font a document NAMES and does not embed is the third thing and the only one that reaches a font resolver. Its two `/Widths` arrays are non-uniform on purpose — with every width equal, PDFium concludes the font is monospaced and the serif bit in `/Flags` becomes unobservable. **Stage 3 T1 added two, for the writer**: `rotate-inherited.pdf` (`/Rotate 90` on the root `/Pages` only, one page overriding with an explicit 0, one inheriting through an intermediate node — D3-5's never-on-an-ancestor rule) and `hybrid-xrefstm.pdf` (packed objects reachable **only** through `/XRefStm`, absent from the classic table — until then no fixture and, it turned out, no test at all exercised that reader path; Stage 3 guide I-03). **Stage 4 T1 added three, for the viewing surface**: `cropbox-margins.pdf` — a `/CropBox` 72 pt inside the `/MediaBox` and ink 72 pt inside that, so sheet, crop and content are **three** rectangles, which no earlier file could be (every other corpus document's CropBox IS its MediaBox, which is why Stage 2's **I-28** could sit open and untestable), plus a second, deliberately EMPTY page carrying the same boxes for D4-14's no-objects fallback; `dests-named.pdf` — both destination forms, a two-level `/Names /Dests` tree with `/Kids` and `/Limits` and the PDF 1.1 flat `/Dests` dictionary, three destination kinds, and a **destination naming object 9999, which does not exist**, so an unresolvable destination has a fixture at last; `transitions.pdf` — three `/Trans` kinds, two `/Dur` values, `/PageMode /FullScreen`, and a fourth page carrying neither key as the control. | `corpus/pdfbuild.py`, the four encoder modules |
| `corpus/make-malformed.py`, `corpus/malformed/**` | ✅ (S2 · T4) | **24 damaged files, one named fault each.** One damage per file is a rule, not a style: a file with two faults cannot tell you which one your repair path handled, and A-21 — *every* file yields a report and never a crash — is only meaningful if a report can be checked against a known cause. Each starts from a valid document built by the same code as `ordinary/` and has exactly one thing done to it, described exactly in `PROVENANCE.md`. Includes the two **hang** cases (`circular-prev`, `pages-loop`) and the two resource cases (`deep-nesting`, `huge-length-claim`) that A-21's *no hang, no unbounded allocation* clauses exist for. `length-lies-short.pdf` **imports** its control's generator rather than rebuilding it, so the pair differs in exactly three bytes and cannot drift. | `corpus/make-ordinary.py` (for the A-17 control), `pdf/cos/XrefRepair` (S2 · T8) |
| `corpus/make-encrypted.py`, `corpus/encrypted/**` | ✅ (S2 · T4) | **Five genuinely enciphered documents** across R2/R3/R4/R6, with fixed public passwords (`user` / `owner`) recorded in `PROVENANCE.md` — a fixture whose password is a secret is a fixture nobody can use. Under **D2-6** these have two readers with two different correct answers: PDFium opens them through its password callback, the COS reader declines them by name until Stage 10. Both halves are only testable against bytes that are actually enciphered. `owner-empty-user.pdf` is the one to know about: an **empty user password**, so it opens with no prompt while still being encrypted — the case a reader mistakes for an unencrypted file. **No public-key encryption** (it would need a private key in the repository). Signed documents are `corpus/signed/`, from Stage 3 T1; until then this row said signing was Stage 10, which is Stage 11. | `corpus/cryptenc.py`, `pdf/render/PdfiumDocument` (S2 · T9) |
| `corpus/make-annots.py`, `corpus/exchange/**`, `corpus/stamps/**`, `corpus/labels/annots-*.json` | ✅ (S6 · T1) | **The corpus Stage 6 is judged on** (`Stage6-Annotations-Plan.md` §5.2, D6-26): eleven annotated PDFs in `ordinary/`, a label each, the migration label, six FDF/XFDF exchange files and two stamp images. **One annotation SPEC writes four things** — the PDF dictionary, its `/AP`, its label entry and its FDF/XFDF form — so a label cannot describe an annotation the file lacks, nor an exchange file drift from its target; `corpus/fixturecheck.py`'s `check_annots` then holds every label entry to the object it names (guide A-15). Reuses `make-text.py`'s Helvetica widths and `make-compare.py`'s page renderer, which gained a line recorder that draws nothing so `annots-report-v1.pdf` can mark words in it and know where `report-v2` put them. What each file carries: `corpus/PROVENANCE.md`. | `corpus/pdfbuild.py`, `corpus/deflate.py`, `corpus/jpegenc.py` |
| `golden/manifest.json` | ✅ (S2 · T17) | **Data, not code, and the DECLARATION half of the golden suite** — hand-authored and reviewed like source; `tools/golden-update` never writes it. Which corpus files are baselined, which pages of each, at which resolutions, which carry a password, which are `machineDependent`, which are **excluded and why**, and the two comparison thresholds D2-13 requires to be *stated*. Every corpus file must be a case or an excluded row, in both directions, checked by the suite and by `--check-manifest`: `corpus/PROVENANCE.md`'s rule that a file with no row is a finding rather than a default. **Which pages** is the one authoring decision that cannot be made without opening the file, which is what `golden-update --survey` is for. | `corpus/**`, `golden/**`, `tests/golden/`, `tools/golden-update/` |
| `golden/captured.json` | ✅ (S2 · T17) | **The MEASURED half**, written by `golden-update` and never by hand: the PDFium version the baselines were made against, the capture timestamp, the `--reason`, and per baseline its size and **the faces it actually resolved**. The split from `manifest.json` is `cmake/PdfiumAssert.cmake`'s — expected values in the file a person edits, found values where the machine put them, and the asymmetry is the check. Two cases read it: one compares the recorded engine with the running one (**A-51**'s runtime half, the part configure cannot see), and one asserts no undeclared baseline resolved a face off this disk (**A-82**, I-13's tripwire). | `golden/manifest.json`, `pdf/render/PdfiumInit`, `pdf/render/FontResolverBridge` |
| `golden/README.md` | ✅ (S2 · T17) | **What a green golden run claims and what it does not**, stated beside the images rather than left in a test comment: *the product rasterizes our own corpus the same way it did when the baselines were captured* — never *correctly*, and never anything about real-world PDFs, since every corpus file is ours (D2-11). Also the three reproducibility hazards (`NoNativeText`, the base-14 decline behind I-13, and a substituted face), the storage decision (8-bit grey where lossless, and why the ENCODER deliberately is not pinned), and the two-number threshold with the reasoning for why one number cannot do the job. | `golden/**`, `tests/golden/` |
| `golden/UPDATES.md` | ✅ (S2 · T17) | **Append-only: every regeneration, its date, its PDFium version and the `--reason` given for it.** D2-3 says the reason *“lands in the commit message”*; a commit message can be amended and a squash can lose it, so it lands in a reviewed file as well, and the reviewer of the next baseline change is the person who most needs to read the last one. | `tools/golden-update/` |
| `golden/**` (231 `.png`) | ✅ (S2 · T17, **+24 at Stage 3 T1, +9 at Stage 4 T1**) | **The reference rasters**: 65 corpus files, 77 pages, at 96 / 144 / 192 DPI — 17.8 MB by `du -cb`, re-measured at Stage 4 T1, which added the baselines of its three corpus files in one filtered capture and regenerated none of the 222 before them. Marked `binary` and `linguist-vendored` by `.gitattributes` — whose `golden/*.json` and `golden/*.md` lines were added in the same change, because `golden/** binary` would have made the four text files above unreadable in a diff, which is precisely the defect T4 found and fixed for `corpus/`. **Never edited by hand and never regenerated except through `golden-update --reason`.** | `tests/golden/`, `tools/golden-update/` |
| `res/ribbon.json` | ✅ (S1 · T10) | **Data, not code — and it is the whole ribbon.** 9 tabs (one contextual), **58 groups, 197 controls** — re-derived from `ribbon-validate` on 2026-09-21, when S4 T9 added the four split controls to the View tab's window group and T10 added `present.start` beside `view.fullScreen`; this row said *49 groups, 192 controls* and had been stale since Stage 2 — every id declared in `ActionId.def`. Tab and group titles are **catalog keys**, not text, because the catalog key is the source string in this project. `collapsePriority` is higher-collapses-first and is deliberately a different axis from visual order — conflating the two is I-05. Validated at build time (`tools/ribbon-validate`) and then **copied beside the executable**, next to `i18n/`, rather than compiled into a Qt resource: the same call T6 made for the `.qm` catalogs, so Stage 15 can ship it replaceable. The validation is what makes that safe, and the runtime parse is the *same* parser, so a hand-edited copy fails with the same diagnostics rather than yielding a quietly thinner ribbon. | `ui/shell/RibbonModel`, `ui/shell/RibbonTabBuilder`, `tools/ribbon-validate`, `tools/i18n-lint` |
| `i18n/locales.json` | ✅ (S1 · T6) | Which locales exist and which of them **ship** (D1-14). The `shipped` flag is what lets `missing-key` fail at all: `zh_CN` and `ja` are declared with `"shipped": false` and a reason, so their absence is *data the gate reads* rather than a case nobody wrote, and `i18n-lint --explain` names them. | `tools/i18n-lint/`, `app/Application` (S1 · T8) |
| `i18n/rawpage_en.ts`, `rawpage_zh_TW.ts` | ✅ (S1 · T6) | **Data, not code.** Qt Linguist catalogs at **955 keys each**, re-counted 2026-10-02 (Stage 5 T14) from the `.ts` files — **373 `action.*`, 141 `prefs.*`, 131 `shell.*`, 129 `error.*`, 70 `ribbon.*`** (58 tab and group titles from `res/ribbon.json`, 3 the ribbon's own code names, and 9 `ribbon.keytip.*` access keys from I-42), **51 `compare.*`** (the comparison's dialog, view and Differences pane, a context of their own, S5 T13), **49 `search.*`** (Stage 5's find bar and Advanced Search, a context of their own since S5 guide I-05; T14's `search.bar.more` the 49th, S5 guide I-10), **9 `panes.*` and 2 `command.*`**. It was *954* at Stage 5 T13, *895* at Stage 5 T12 and *740* on 2026-09-19 (361, 120, 95, 92, 70 and 2, before Stage 5). `zh_TW` carries the same 954 keys with 29 still marked unfinished, which `i18n-lint --explain` reports and which is not a gate failure (`orphan-key` and unfinished counts are S3, reported rather than blocking). **The figure below is Stage 2's and is kept as history rather than corrected into the present**: the families it traces grew through Stages 2, 3 and 4, and what matters about it is why this row carries a DATE at all. The `error.*` family is where Stage 2 has grown: 12 at T5, then +2 at T6 (the parser's two named refusals), +9 at T7 (the filters and the cross-reference table), +6 at T8 (the repair report's four summary keys, the no-objects refusal and the encrypted decline) and +5 at T9 (the password prompt and the rasterizer). It read 538 with 11 `error.*` at S2 · T1; **T2's `error.engine.initFailed` arrived with `PdfiumInit` and no running figure moved with it**, which is why this row now carries a measurement date rather than a remembered total. The `shell.*` group grew by 6 at T11 (the toolbar and the tool finder) and 6 more at T12 (the document tab, the title with a document in it, and the open dialog's own chrome). **The catalog key is the `<source>` and `en` is a real catalog rather than the identity**, because `core/` may hold no user-facing English (`core/Result.h`) and `ActionId.def` already carries one key per action; there is consequently no English in the sources for `lupdate` to extract, and these are hand-maintained with `missing-key` and `action-label` keeping them honest. Each group arrived **with the code that uses it** — `shell.*` at T9 and again at T11 and T12, `ribbon.*` at T10; `prefs.*` arrives the same way at T14 — seeding them earlier would be a hundred `orphan-key` notes and a catalog nobody could review. Compiled to `.qm` **beside the executable** by `i18n/CMakeLists.txt` so Stage 15 can ship them replaceable, and installed as **two** translators at runtime, `en` underneath the active locale: because the key is the source string, an untranslated string falls back to its own key rather than to English unless the source catalog is there to catch it. | `tools/i18n-lint/`, `ui/*` (S1 · T9+) |
| `THIRD-PARTY.md` | ✅ (S0 · T8) | **Generated** by `license-audit`, never hand-edited, and committed per release (§8.2). The About → Third-Party Notices dialog is built from the same data, so a dependency cannot ship without appearing in both. | `tools/license-audit/` |
| `.github/workflows/ci.yml` | ✅ (S0 · T13 · extended S2 · T18, S3 · T13) | **Five jobs** (three when this row was written): `gates`, `windows`, `portable-asan`, `portable-fuzz` — three targets linked and run 60 s each since Stage 3 T13, with `rawpage_fuzz_seeds` writing the round trip's seeds — and `coverage-report`, which names what a run did not cover, the Windows-only kill harness among it. `windows`, `portable-asan` and `portable-fuzz` all `needs: gates`, so a layering or licence violation stops everything before a runner installs a toolchain. `windows` runs on a **GitHub-hosted `windows-2025` runner since 2026-10-18** (self-hosted before that, because aqtinstall's releases cannot fetch Qt 6.11.1 and downgrading the pin to suit CI was rejected): it installs the pinned Qt, GCC, CMake and Ninja into `C:\Qt` with aqtinstall pinned to a main-branch commit, caches them and the vcpkg archives, and **asserts the SHA-256 of the five linked Qt DLLs and `g++.exe`** against the release machine's install, so *CI tests the bytes we ship* is checked rather than true by construction. `coverage-report` reads the `windows` job's **result**, no longer a repository variable. | everything |
| `.github/workflows/fuzz-soak.yml` | ✅ (S2, 2026-09-14 · extended S3 · T13) | **The ten-minute fuzz soak** for guide cases A-25 and A-26 (`Stage2-Rendering-Testing-Guide.md` §5), and so Stage 2's sign-off item 5 — **and since Stage 3 T13 for three targets**, Stage 3 guide A-83(b): a matrix of target and seed directories, the round trip seeded also from `corpus/ordinary/`, `corpus/signed/` and the `rawpage_fuzz_seeds` output its job writes first. Its first Stage 3 run, 35134357858, found Stage 3 guide I-13 in the round trip; run 35137107236, on the fix, was clean on all three. Run by hand (`gh workflow run fuzz-soak.yml -f seconds=600`) and **daily at 03:17 UTC** — weekly until 2026-09-21, when run 35580712267's finding showed the yield is a function of accumulated search time rather than of the diff, then nightly until 2026-10-16, when the private repository's 2,000-minute Actions allowance put it back to weekly (about 95 billed minutes a soak), and daily again since 2026-10-19, when the repository went public and its standard runners stopped being billed (the workflow's header has the figures); **never on push**, because `ci.yml`'s concurrency group would cancel a soak at the next push. One job per target, seeded from `corpus/malformed/` into a scratch corpus so the checked-in corpus is never written. `-timeout=30`, because libFuzzer's 1200 s per-input default could never fail a ten-minute soak. It fails on the exit code **and** on a sanitizer line in the log (Stage 2 I-38), and uploads the log and any crashing input. First run: 34781013867, green (two targets). |
| `.gitattributes` | ✅ (S0 · T1; amended S2 · T4) | Marks `corpus/**` and `golden/**` binary. Not cosmetic: Git's text heuristic misreads a minimal uncompressed PDF, and CRLF injection would shift every xref offset — silently turning a fixture into a different file than the test was written against. **T4 narrowed it**: `corpus/` is no longer only data, and `corpus/** binary` was marking several thousand lines of generator Python and Markdown binary too, so `git diff` showed nothing for them and a change to a generator could not be reviewed. `corpus/*.py`, `*.md` and `*.json` are now explicitly `text eol=lf … merge`; the `.pdf` data stays binary, which is the half that matters. **T17 extended it to `golden/*.json` and `golden/*.md` — and found that T4's own narrowing was INCOMPLETE (issue I-16)**: `binary` is a macro for `-text -diff -merge`, and the `.md` and `.json` lines supplied `text` and `merge` and not `diff`, so `corpus/PROVENANCE.md` had been committing as `Bin` for four days *inside the fix written to stop exactly that*. Only `corpus/*.py` escaped, because `diff=python` sets the attribute as a side effect. All four lines now spell `diff` out, and because the attribute is applied when a diff is rendered rather than when a blob is stored, **every past commit became readable in the same change**. | `corpus/`, `golden/` |

---

## 4. Breadcrumb convention

When a planned file is folded into another, its row **stays** with status ⤵ and a Role of
"folded into `X` — see that row", because a reader chasing a reference from an older stage needs the
trail. The same applies to renames. Rows are never silently deleted.

*(No foldings yet. Two rows are nonetheless breadcrumbs rather than plans: `platform/win/WinSpeech`,
withdrawn 2026-08-23 with the accessibility surface, and — recorded here because a reader will look
for it — `platform/PlatformModule.cpp`, Stage 0's placeholder translation unit, **deleted at Stage 1
T7** once `platform/` had real content, exactly as its own header comment instructed. It never had a
row, because it never had a responsibility to fold anywhere. **`pdf/PdfModule.cpp` was the last of
those three and was deleted at Stage 2 T1**, in the change that gave `pdf/` its first real
translation unit — `core/CoreModule.cpp` and `ui/UiModule.cpp` having gone at Stage 1 T15 and T9.
None of the four ever had a row, for the same reason.)*

---

## 5. Primary flows

### A. Startup and wiring — `app/` is the only integrator

```
main()
 ├─ set per-monitor DPI awareness            // BEFORE any window exists
 ├─ CrashHandler::install()
 ├─ CommandLine::parse(argv)
 │    └─ if a headless verb → BatchRunner ─▶ exit(code)   // no window ever created
 ├─ SingleInstance::claim()
 │    └─ if secondary → ISingleInstanceBus.forward(files) ─▶ exit(0)
 └─ CompositionRoot::build()
      ├─ Settings(SettingsSchema, %APPDATA%\RawPage\settings.ini)
      ├─ PolicyOverlay(machine policy)          ─▶ layered under Settings
      ├─ platform = platform::createPlatform()  // ← the ONLY createPlatform() call
      ├─ backend  = pdf::createBackend()        // ← the ONLY createBackend() call
      ├─ ActionRegistry + KeyBindingStore(§8 defaults + user overrides)
      ├─ RenderScheduler(worker pool, RenderCache, platform.power)
      ├─ Services{ backend, platform, settings, actions, scheduler }
      └─ MainWindow(Services&)                  // receives INTERFACES, not concretes
 ├─ window.show()
 ├─ RecoveryPrompt::checkAndOffer(window, services)   // S3 · T11: BEFORE the files, so a recovered file is not opened clean
 └─ window.openFiles(files)
```
**Direction shown:** `app/` names both concretes; `MainWindow` receives only interfaces and could
be constructed in a test against mocks with no PDFium and no Win32.

### B. Opening a document — Explorer double-click into an already-running instance

```
Explorer double-click a .pdf
 └─ WinShellIntegration's registered verb launches rawpage.exe <path>
     └─ SingleInstance::claim() → already running
         └─ WinSingleInstanceBus.forward([path])        // platform/win
             └─▶ (primary process) MainWindow::openFiles([path])
                  └─ IDocumentBackend.open(path, PasswordCallback)
                      ├─ PdfiumDocument opens; on /Encrypt → prompt via callback
                      ├─ CosParser + XrefTable read the object graph
                      │    └─ on a broken xref → XrefRepair ─▶ RepairReport
                      └─▶ hydrate core/doc/Document {PageTree, Outline, Permissions, …}
                  ├─ DocumentWindow(Document, CommandJournal) added as a tab
                  ├─ MessageBar shows: repaired / protected / signed / has-form
                  │    (a REFUSED file gets no tab — its reason is a window-level open-failed banner)
                  ├─ PageView requests the first visible tiles      → flow C
                  └─ MainWindow::updateDocumentPanes()              // S4 · T5, T6
                       └─ SidebarDock's three panes are RE-POINTED, never rebuilt
                            ├─ ThumbnailsPane.setDocument(handle, revision,
                            │                             model pages, engine pages)
                            │    └─ requests its VISIBLE slots + one screen  → flow C
                            ├─ BookmarksPane.setDocument(handle, backend.outline(handle))
                            └─ DestinationsPane.setDocument(handle,
                                                            backend.destinations(handle))
     (the last two are POINTER FETCHES into models hydrated once at open -- D4-3.
      No engine call, no re-walk: showing a pane reads nothing, which is A-30)
```
**One pane per WINDOW, re-pointed at the tab in front** (S4 · T5): a pane rebuilt per tab would
throw away the thumbnails it had just rendered and ask the one worker for them again.
**And navigation runs the other way through one closure** (S4 · T6): a thumbnail click, a bookmark
and a named destination all end in `PageView::goToPage`, so the three cannot land in different
places for the same target. At Stage 4 a destination's FIT is carried and not applied — **I-05**,
T12's with D4-14.
**Direction shown:** `ui/` asked an *interface* to open a file. It never learned that PDFium exists,
and `platform/win` was reached only through `ISingleInstanceBus`.

### C. Tiled asynchronous rendering at the right DPI

```
PageView scrolled / zoomed
 └─ ViewportController → PagePresentation → visible page rects
     └─ DpiPolicy(zoom, IDisplayTopology.scaleFor(window)) ─▶ device resolution
         └─ TileGrid(page, resolution, viewport) ─▶ {visible, prefetch, evict}
             └─ TilePresenter
                  ├─ RenderCache.lookup(RenderRequest)  → hit  ─▶ paint immediately
                  └─ miss ─▶ RenderScheduler.enqueue(RenderRequest, Visible)
                              └─ worker thread: IPageRasterizer.raster(request)
                                   └─ PdfiumRasterizer + GlyphCache          // pdf/render
                                        └─ FontResolverBridge → IFontResolver // missing font
                              ◀─ deliver to UI thread ─▶ RenderCache.put ─▶ repaint tile
                                   └─ HeldRaster: RasterImage ─▶ QImage + its bytes
   (a tile scrolled out of view is cancelled, not rendered)

ThumbnailsPane scrolled                                          // S4 · T5, D4-10
 └─ visible thumbnail slots + ONE SCREEN of margin  (never the document)
     └─ per slot: RenderRequest{page, whole page at thumbnail scale,
                                flags None, revision, rotationDelta}
         └─ THE SAME RenderScheduler.enqueue(request, Speculative)
              ├─ strict priority: a Visible tile always goes first
              ├─ THE SAME RenderCache — one 256 MiB budget, O7's one cache
              └─ scrolled away ─▶ cancel the ticket, as TilePresenter does

SplitViewContainer: N PageViews in ONE DocumentWindow                // S4 · T9, D4-13
 └─ one handle, one Document, one journal, one revision
     └─ each pane runs the whole TilePresenter path above, independently
          └─ THE SAME scheduler and THE SAME cache -- a split divides the window's
             pixels rather than doubling them, so the tile count is about unchanged

A window that is NOT in front                                        // S4 · T11, A2's named work
 └─ MainWindow::refreshWindowFocusState()  (from activeShell(), not isActiveWindow():
     under `offscreen` no activation event ever arrives)
     └─ TilePresenter::setBackground(true)
          └─ its viewport enqueues at Prefetch, NOT Visible
               └─ strict priority then has something to sort by, and the window the
                  person is looking at keeps the worker  (measured at T14: the
                  two-window p95 fell from 18.91-19.33 ms to 4.01-4.75 ms)
```
**Direction shown:** the UI thread never parses or rasterizes; `core/render/` decides *policy* and
holds no PDFium type; the only PDFium call sits in `pdf/render/`. **And from S4 · T5 there are two
consumers of that one pipeline** — a viewport and a pane — which is the whole subject of A2 at
Stage 4 (`Tech-Stack-Plan.md` §3.3): one scheduler, one worker, one cache, and the pane's priority
is what keeps a 900-page column from starving the page being read.

### D. A mutation, end to end — the cross-cutting live path *(annotated)*

Rotating page 3 by 90°, triggered from the Thumbnails pane by `Ctrl+R`
([`Features.md` §8 → Page Thumbnails pane](./Features.md#page-thumbnails-pane)):

```
key press  Ctrl+R  while the Thumbnails pane has focus
 └─ ShortcutBinder: activeContext() == KeyContext::PageThumbnails
     └─ KeyBindingStore.lookup(Ctrl+R, PageThumbnails) ─▶ ActionId::PageRotate ("page.rotate")
         └─ ActionRegistry.dispatch(PageRotate, PageThumbnails)
             └─ ui/dialogs/RotatePagesDialog collects {pages:[3], angle:90}
                 └─ journal.run(make_unique<RotatePageCommand>({3}, 90))     ◀── ui/ STOPS HERE
                     │                                                           it never touches
                     ├─ Permissions::may(EditPages)? no ─▶ refuse, nothing changed   the document
                     ├─ Command::apply(Document&, MutationSink&)
                     │    ├─ PageTree.page(3).setRotation(90)      // core model only
                     │    └─ sink.setPageRotation(3, 90)           // INTENT, not bytes
                     │         └─ MutationSinkImpl → PageOps → CosObject /Rotate // pdf/ops
                     ├─ SHOW (S6 T4, D6-2): backend.showEdits composes + verifies the update;
                     │    refused ─▶ revert, error.edit.notShown, nothing recorded
                     ├─ RecoveryJournal.append(cmd.serialize()) + fsync
                     ├─ Revision++ ; dirtyChanged(true)
                     └─ mutated(Scope{page 3})
                          ├─ RenderCache invalidates only page 3's tiles; the scheduler re-keys
                          │    every queued tile of another page (S6 guide I-06)
                          ├─ PageView repaints page 3                 (not the document)
                          └─ ThumbnailsPane re-renders thumbnail 3
```
**Direction shown, and why this trace is the most important one in the document:** `ui/`'s entire
mutation vocabulary is *construct a `Command`, hand it to the journal*. `core/` expresses **intent**
to a `MutationSink` interface and never learns how a rotation is stored. `pdf/ops/` is the only code
that touches a COS object. Undo is free — `Ctrl+Z` calls `revert()` down the same chain. Crash
recovery is free — the serialized command is already on disk. Invalidation is precise — `Scope`
said one page. **Every** mutation in the product, from a highlight to applying redactions to a
keystroke in Write mode, follows exactly this shape.

**The annotation intents, since Stage 6 T3** (`Stage6-Annotations-Plan.md` D6-1). An annotation command
has the same shape with two differences. Its pre-flight is `Command::permitted()`, which asks
`may(Annotate)` and then `mayAnnotate(page)` for every page it touches (D6-3). And it speaks in
PLACEMENTS -- *the n-th entry of page p's `/Annots`* -- because the store and the sink hold the same
listing, read from the same array: `sink.insertAnnotations(at, annotations)` /
`sink.removeAnnotations(at)` first, all or nothing over the set, then `AnnotationStore::insert` /
`remove`. `MutationSinkImpl` keeps the desired listing per page and, at save, writes only the listings
that differ, each created annotation's dictionary (`AnnotationOps`) and its `/AP` (`AppearanceStreamBuilder`
over `pdf/cos/ContentStreamBuilder`); `RevisionWriter::verify` reads every listing back before the replace.
**Since Stage 6 T4** every command -- a rotation included -- also passes the journal's SHOW step before it
is recorded (D6-2): the update is composed and verified as Save does and the engine reloads it, so the
screen shows what Save writes; an annotation command's `Scope` is ANNOTATIONS of its pages with each
changed `/Rect` (D6-10), which keeps the page's text and drops only the tiles a rectangle reaches.

### E. Adding a highlight — a feature flow

`Ctrl+Alt+H`, then drag across text ([`Features.md` §4.1](./Features.md#41-annotation-tools)):

```
Ctrl+Alt+H ─▶ KeyContext::Viewer row (Q1) ─▶ ActionId::HighlightTool
 └─ KeyContext::AnnotationTools → Viewer → Common → Global      // the context chain (S6 · T5, D6-9)
 └─ ToolController.activate(MarkupTool{Highlight})
     └─ drag over text
         ├─ PageReading.caretAt → TextSelection range    // core/text (built S5 · T5)
         │    └─ over a PageTextModel the engine thread's text job extracted (D5-3)
         └─ release ─▶ AddAnnotationCommand{Highlight, quadPoints, colour from tool defaults}
             └─ journal.run(...)                          // ← identical shape to flow D
                 ├─ sink.insertAnnotations({page, end}, …) // INTENT, first (S6 · T3)
                 ├─ AnnotationStore.insert(…)             // core model, second
                 └─ SHOW, before anything is recorded (S6 · T4, D6-2):
                      backend.showEdits ─▶ RevisionWriter::compose + verify (Save's own; a failure
                      │                    takes the command back: error.edit.notShown)
                      └─ PdfiumDocument::show(original, update) ─▶ queued
             ─▶ mutated(Scope{annotations of page, its /Rect}) (D6-10)
                 ├─ RenderScheduler.documentChanged(…, engine pages): only the tiles the /Rect
                 │    reaches are dropped; the rest re-keyed to the new revision (tileTouchedBy, I-06)
                 ├─ DocumentIndex.invalidate: the page's text KEPT, its search units dropped
                 └─ PageView re-requests ─▶ render thread: the next engine call swaps in
                      FPDF_LoadCustomDocument(original ⧺ update), under the engine lock, between jobs
                      ─▶ the reached tiles drawn again with the highlight in them
```
The `/AP` stream is generated by us, not left to the viewer, so the highlight looks the same in
Acrobat and in a browser. **And what the screen shows is what Save writes** (S6 · T4): the engine draws
the very update the next save appends -- `tests/render/tst_annotdisplay.cpp` holds the live page and the
saved file reopened to 0 differing pixels.

### F. Save and crash recovery

*Rewritten at Stage 3 T8 to `Stage3-Mutation-Plan.md` §5.3's transaction (D3-4). The flow that stood here had one `IDocumentBackend.save(Incremental)` doing the whole job and appending to the original file in place; D3-4 writes a complete new file and replaces the target, and splits the work three ways. Its recovery half: `RecoveryJournal`'s discovery and replay are built (T9), and the prompt that offers them (T11) is drawn as built.*

```
Ctrl+S ─▶ file.save ─▶ SaveCoordinator.save(journal, path)                      core/doc (D3-4)
 ├─ no IDurableFiles?            ─▶ refused, error.save.noDurableFiles         (never a direct write)
 ├─ journal clean?               ─▶ refused, error.save.nothingToSave
 ├─ requiresSaveAs()?            ─▶ Save-As required, error.save.repairedDocument          (D3-6)
 ├─ still the opened file?  no   ─▶ Save-As required, error.save.fileChanged   path·size·time·SHA-256 (D3-16)
 ├─ remove .<name>.rawpage-<pid>.tmp whose writer is gone      IDurableFiles.processExists / removeFile
 ├─ backend.writeRevision(handle, <dir>/.<name>.rawpage-<pid>.tmp)          pdf/ops/RevisionWriter
 │    ├─ the newest revision on disk, COPIED VERBATIM
 │    ├─ IncrementalWriter.protectOriginal: open-ended objects re-homed, dangling numbers reserved (I-12)
 │    ├─ IncrementalWriter.append | appendComplete (a repaired document)         (D3-5, D3-6)
 │    └─ READ BACK FROM THE PATH: prefix identical · opens with no repair · every page as the model says
 │                                                   ─▶ else the file is removed, error.save.verifyFailed
 ├─ IDurableFiles.syncToDisk(temp)
 ├─ IDurableFiles.replaceFile(target, temp)       streams, DACL, creation time CARRIED onto temp, then
 │                                                ONE atomic rename over target (S4 I-31; was ReplaceFileW)
 │    └─ any failure above ─▶ temp removed, target untouched, journal still dirty
 │         └─ the FILE SYSTEM refused?  ─▶ Save-As required, carrying that error as its reason (I-15)
 │            our own bytes?  (Corrupted)  ─▶ refused, no dialog: no other path would take them
 ├─ backend.adoptRevision(handle, target)         the sink compares against the file now on disk;
 │                                                the ENGINE is not reloaded: it already draws what was
 │                                                written (S6 T4, D6-2); its page tree is the open's
 └─ journal.adoptSavedFile(backend) ─▶ new DocumentId and RepairReport ─▶ markSaved() ─▶ dirtyChanged(false)
                                        └─ RecoveryJournal rewrites meta.json, truncates lines   (T9)

… process killed before saving …

S6 T11 (D6-23): a serialized string over 64 KiB -- an attachment's bytes, an image stamp's pixels --
 └─ RecoveryJournal ─▶ <sha256>.side through the ONE whole-file write, flushed and in place
                    ─▶ THEN the line, naming it {"sideFile":"<sha256>"}       (the waiver census stays 15)

next launch                                                                        (T11, as built)
 └─ RecoveryPrompt::checkAndOffer: RecoveryJournal.discover(root)     a live owner's lock ─▶ not listed
     ├─ no operations ─▶ discardDirectory, never asked
     ├─ nobody to ask (offscreen) ─▶ every journal kept                                  (convention 16)
     └─ RecoveryDialog: per row Recover | Discard | Decide later
         └─ Recover ─▶ MainWindow::recoverDocument(candidate)
             ├─ source matches AND meta not untitled ─▶ into the file, under its path
             │  else                                  ─▶ an UNTITLED document; the source is never written
             ├─ backend.open(source) ─▶ DocumentWindow(handle, Recovery{dir, source, untitled})
             │    ├─ RecoveryJournal::replay(dir, journal)  BY UNDO STEP, no recorder attached
             │    │    ├─ each {"sideFile":h} read back from h.side, its SHA-256 h  (S6 T11, D6-23) ─▶ else stops there
             │    │    └─ a failure at step k keeps 1..k-1 and reports k             // never all-or-nothing
             │    └─ RecoveryJournal::open(root, source, id, seed = the applied lines, untitled)
             ├─ Recovered banner: steps restored · step k and why · a last change unfinished
             └─ discardDirectory(dir)             a refusal keeps it, and says so (OpenFailed)
```

### G. Find, then advanced search

*(Built as of Stage 5 T9 -- the dashed lines too, the folder; only the catalog index below them is Stage 16's: the find bar and the search half below it --
`SearchQuery`, `SearchEngine`'s match and run, `DocumentIndex` -- the highlights through
`SelectionOverlay`, and since T8 the four non-page classes, read through one facade reader rather than
the editing models (D5-12), part by part as engine tasks; and since T9 Advanced Search over a folder,
each file opened in pieces and searched by the same engine (D5-13).)*

```
Ctrl+F (Common) ─▶ FindReplaceBar of the tab in front, KeyContext::FindSearch inside it
 └─ each edit restarts a 200 ms debounce; SearchSession cancels the older search
 └─ SearchQuery{pattern, flags = search/*, classes = search/include*, scope, maxResults}
     └─ SearchEngine.start(DocumentIndex entry of this DocumentId, query)          (UI thread)
          ├─ each page the index lacks ─▶ RenderScheduler.enqueueText(…, Background) ≤ 32 at once
          │     └─ engine thread: ITextExtractor ─▶ PageTextModel ─▶ DocumentIndex (pumping thread)
          ├─ comments or fields on: each page's unit part ─▶ RenderScheduler.enqueueTask(…, Background)
          │  bookmarks / attachments on (whole document): the outline's and the attachments' parts
          │     └─ engine thread: IDocumentBackend::searchUnits(handle, part) ─▶ SearchUnitPart
          │          (an attachment's /Desc first-party: EmbeddedFileReader)  ─▶ DocumentIndex, once per part
          ├─ each slot whose pieces are held ─▶ a matching worker: PageReading, SearchablePage(options)
          │     built once, then the literal or the PCRE2 match, block by block, and findInUnit over
          │     each included unit                                               (neither UI nor engine thread)
     ─▶ deliverReady() on the pumping thread ─▶ hits in document order (the document's units first), the
        bound said, unmapped pages listed, a field shown by several widgets once
     ─▶ SelectionOverlay paints the hits over the tiles (a comment or a field: its /Rect)
     ─▶ the active pane reveals the current one: page text, a comment, a field ─▶ revealPageRect;
        a bookmark ─▶ goToDestination; an attachment ─▶ nothing opens (Stage 6)
Ctrl+Shift+F (Global, D5-15) ─▶ SearchPane (KeyContext::FindSearch), a folder, recursive or not   (T9)
 └─ FolderSearch: the folder listed on its HELPER thread, then one file at a time:
     ├─ IDocumentBackend::beginOpen / continueOpen, piece by piece:
     │    an ENGINE piece ─▶ RenderScheduler.enqueueTask(no document, Background)   (≤ 1 ms of page walk)
     │    every other piece (the read, the first-party reading) ─▶ the helper; the UI thread opens nothing
     ├─ DocumentIndex.attach(identity) ─▶ SearchEngine, as above: every class, the flags, what is left of the bound
     ├─ hits ─▶ SearchResults: snippets, relevance (hit count, ties by path), sort, filter, refine, CSV
     └─ detach ─▶ cancelDocument ─▶ an EXCLUSIVE close task; an encrypted file listed as skipped, never prompted
 ─▶ a result opened ─▶ MainWindow::openSearchResult ─▶ openFiles (not twice) ─▶ the tab's find bar presentHits
 ─ ─ the catalog index (IIndexer, CatalogIndex) and portfolios ─▶ Stage 16
```

### H. Convert to Writable — crossing into the second document model

```
ActionId::ConvertToWritable
 └─ FixedToReflowConverter.convert(Document&)
     ├─ PageTextModel + LayoutAnalyzer ─▶ lines → blocks → reading order → columns/tables
     ├─ TagTree consulted where the document is tagged (much better fidelity)
     └─▶ ReflowDocument{sections, paragraphs, runs, tables, styles}   + FidelityReport
 ├─ ConversionFidelityDialog shows what could not be reconstructed ─▶ user confirms
 └─ DocumentWindow swaps the viewer for ui/write/WriteCanvas ; KeyContext → WriteMode
      └─ typing ─▶ ReflowCommands (coalesced) ─▶ journal.run()   // same shape as flow D

ActionId::PreventFurtherEdits
 └─ ReflowToFixedComposer.compose(ReflowDocument&)
     └─ LineBreaker + Shaper ─▶ page layout
         ├─ sink.replacePageContent(...)  ─▶ ContentStreamBuilder ─▶ new content streams
         └─ sink.replaceStructureTree(...) ─▶ TagTree built FROM the reflow model      // O2
             // The reflow model is dropped, but its STRUCTURE survives as standard PDF
             // tags -- so the next Convert to Writable reads tags instead of re-guessing
             // from glyphs. (Until 2026-08-23 this also bought PDF/UA proximity for free;
             // accessibility is out of scope now, and the round-trip fidelity argument --
             // which was always the primary one -- is untouched.)
```
**Direction shown:** the same journal, the same sink, the same `Ctrl+Z`. Write mode is a second
*model*, not a second mutation mechanism — which is exactly why it does not double the architecture.

### I. Sign, then validate — logic in `core/`, crypto in `platform/`

```
ActionId::SignDocument
 └─ SignDialog ─▶ SignatureRequest{field, appearance, reason, permittedChanges, PAdES:B-LT}
     └─ ISigner.sign(request)                                  // core/backend.h
         └─ SignatureOps                                        // pdf/ops
              ├─ reserve /Contents; compute the byte ranges
              ├─ hash exactly those bytes
              ├─ ICryptoProvider.signHash(...)  ─▶ WinCryptoProvider  // smart card / HSM / store
              ├─ fetch a timestamp + OCSP; embed for LTV
              └─ IncrementalWriter appends the signature dictionary

open a signed file
 └─ SignatureValidator.validate(sig)                            // core/security — pure logic
      ├─ byte-range integrity check
      ├─ ICertificateStore.buildChain(...) ─▶ WinCertificateStore
      ├─ ICryptoProvider.verify(...) + revocation + timestamp-vs-signer-clock
      └─▶ ValidationReport ─▶ SignaturesPane
```
**Direction shown:** every signature *decision* is testable headlessly against a mock
`ICryptoProvider`; no `core/` file includes a Win32 crypto header.

### J. A settings change propagating

```
PreferencesDialog ─▶ SettingsPage, one row per SettingsKeys.def row whose group matches
 └─ the row's editor ─▶ Settings.set(keys::pageDisplay::lineWeight, true)   // a Key<T>, never a string
     └─ Settings.changed{ path, group }
         ├─ RenderCache invalidates every tile whose RenderRequest carried the old flag
         ├─ RenderScheduler re-enqueues the visible set
         ├─ StatusBar updates its indicator
         └─ every OTHER open SettingsPage in that group re-reads its rows
   (a key locked by PolicyOverlay is shown disabled, with which policy locked it)
```
**Real as of Stage 1 T14**, minus the two Stage 2 rows. Two things the diagram is precise about
rather than decorative. **`set` takes a `Key<T>` and not a path** — the page reaches its keys by
`#include`ing `SettingsKeys.def` itself, because a page built from the runtime descriptor table
would need the string-keyed API D1-10 exists to forbid, and adding one for the Preferences dialog's
convenience would make the guarantee decorative for the whole product. And the **last branch is the
half nobody builds**: a second window's page, a *Restore Defaults*, or a Stage 15 policy refresh all
change a value some other page is displaying, and without that subscription the control keeps
showing the old one until the dialog is closed and reopened.

### K. A batch run with no UI at all

```
rawpage.exe --batch redact-and-secure.seq --input C:\in --output C:\out --quiet --json
 └─ CommandLine::parse ─▶ headless verb ─▶ NO MainWindow is ever constructed
     └─ BatchRunner.run(sequence, inputs)
          └─ per file: backend.open ─▶ for each action: ActionRegistry.dispatch(ActionId)
                        └─ journal.run(Command)      // the SAME path as flow D
             ─▶ backend.save ─▶ BatchReport row
     └─▶ BatchReport as JSON on stdout ; documented exit code
```
**Direction shown:** this flow is the standing proof that the action layer is real. If any feature
were implemented in a widget's slot instead of behind an `ActionId` and a `Command`, it would be
unreachable here — and that is exactly the regression this flow's test catches.

---

## 6. Why this shape

- **Portability** — the fifteen `platform/` interfaces are wide but shallow, and *none* is in the
  document-editing path. A future `platform/mac/` implements the same header; `core/` and `pdf/`
  need no change. The list was derived from the "needs platform code" table in
  [`Tech-Stack-Plan.md` §4.3](./Tech-Stack-Plan.md#43-what-qt-gives-us-for-free-versus-what-needs-platform-code),
  not invented.
- **Testability** — `core/` links `QtCore` and nothing else. `tests/unit/` deliberately links
  *without* `rawpage_ui` and `rawpage_platform`, so the boundary is verified by the build on every run
  rather than asserted in a document. Every page operation, form calculation, search, signature
  decision, reflow line break, and command inverse is a headless unit test.
- **The mutation invariant holds by construction** — `ui/` has no vocabulary for changing a
  document other than constructing a `Command`. Undo, redo, crash recovery, precise invalidation,
  batch replay, and signature-preserving incremental save are therefore not five features; they are
  five consequences of flow D, and `tools/mutation-lint` makes a sixth impossible to add without
  tests.
- **The engine is replaceable in halves** — reading goes through `IPageRasterizer` and
  `ITextExtractor`; writing goes through `MutationSink`. Swapping the read half (if risk A2 fails)
  or the write half touches `pdf/` and nothing else.
- **One dispatch bus** — `ActionId` + `KeyContext` is what lets `Ctrl+K` mean *Preferences* while
  reading and *Insert hyperlink* while writing, and simultaneously lets flow K reach every feature
  with no window on screen. Those two properties come from the same file.
- **Two document models, one mutation mechanism** — `core/doc/Document` (fixed) and
  `core/reflow/ReflowDocument` (reflowable) are honestly separate, because the reference product's
  dual-mode behaviour tells us they *are* separate. Sharing the journal instead of the model is what
  keeps that honesty from doubling the architecture.
